# Data Kadai: YouTube idea catalogue (11 Oct 2026)

Every idea from the brainstorm: 40 Shorts and 55 long-form, ranked by score. The strategy and the order to make them in are in [YOUTUBE_IDEAS.md](YOUTUBE_IDEAS.md).

## How to read this

- **Virality (1-10)** is an editor's judgment of the chance the video is shared, argued over and rewatched. It is a judgment made by one AI reviewer reading the benchmark research, not a forecast. Nothing here has been tested on our channel.
- **Score** = virality x 0.5 + appeal x 0.2 + fit x 0.2 + distinctness x 0.1, minus a penalty for risk and for unsupported data, plus a small bonus when the data editor re-checked the numbers.
- **Data:** the data editor re-checked the numbers of 38 of the 95 ideas (the top of the list). For the rest, the numbers are the idea writer's and must be re-checked against the source before a script is written.
- **Red team:** three reviewers (growth, accuracy, viewer) voted on the shortlist. Two or more "drop" votes removed an idea.
- Judged by the audience reviewer: 95 of 95.

## What the completeness reviewer said was missing

- **A real 'How [state/UT] became part of India' and 'How [state] got its name' series. Only S015 (Goa) is the pure form, and the only name idea is S018 (virality 3). Build 6 to 8 episodes on the non-communal cases the research names (Sikkim, Puducherry, Lakshadweep, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu, plus Goa), sourced to gazetted Acts and treaties, one place per Short. Peg each to its own anniversary where one falls in the 12 Oct to 31 Mar window, and add a 'name origins' branch.** (Short (20 to 60 s, one place per Short, 6 to 8 episodes). The long versions already exist as L014 and L026.): This is the best-measured Shorts hook, yet it is 1 of 33 Shorts ideas. Measured: state formation, merger, history and what-if-state Shorts reach 1M+ in 26.7% of cases versus a 12.4% baseline (60 Shorts, 22 channels). The neutral 'became part of India' lane has 15 matching Shorts from only 9 channels, median 383K, 67% at 100K+. Yash Rajput's 21 border-history Shorts have a median of 1.3M against a 139K channel median. Name origins: 2 of 8 Shorts at 1M+ (thin sample). Caveats: the viral examples are Hindi-narrated. Anniversary dates such as Goa in December or Puducherry on 1 November come from my memory, not the files, so verify them in the gazette. Skip Hyderabad, Junagadh, Kashmir and Nagaland-style cases (communal or secession adjacency, hard rules). Show only the official outer boundary. L014 and L026 can be the long follow-ups.
- **A one-Short-per-state-and-UT roster ('[State] in 60 seconds': where it leads India, where it trails, one data paradox) in a fixed layout, plus a matching long 'State Files' title for each big-demand state. Regions with no dedicated idea: Rajasthan, West Bengal (only inside S032), Delhi, Madhya Pradesh, Assam and the rest of the Northeast, J&K and Ladakh, the island UTs. Meanwhile about 10 ideas are Tamil Nadu, 7 Bihar and 6 Kerala. Title the long ones the way people type: 'Bihar economy', 'Assam economy', 'Why is Kerala ...'. This roster also serves NRIs and migrants as 'your home state' with no extra work.** (Short for the roster (36 episodes over time). Long for the 8 to 10 highest-demand states (10 to 15 min).): Measured: Geo Study's fixed state-by-state series has 24 Shorts, median 982K, floor 212K. Bharat Flip World's state explainers reach 1.2M to 1.8M. Personalised 'your state' hooks draw 7 to 12 times the median comment rate. Typed searches: Delhi 46, West Bengal 43, Rajasthan 42, Maharashtra 50. Search for '[state] economy': 263 suggestions (my count), 67 of them bare state names, 82 with exam words (assam economy apsc, bihar economy bpsc), which also suits exam-prep sponsors. Caveats: the measured series are Hindi map-geography animations, not data. West Bengal, Bihar and Punjab long-forms hit 3x only 10 to 18% of the time (n=6 to 11), so each low-profile state needs its own hook, not just a profile. State GK earns no extra reach on exam channels (median ratio 1.05). 'tamil' is in 297 search modifiers, so TNPSC-style demand wants Tamil narration, which is out of scope. NRI-specific data demand is almost absent in autocomplete (5 'nri' suggestions, one relevant).
- **A district lane plus a physical-geography flagship. District: Tamil Nadu first (AK's home), then other states in English. The only two district ideas, S010 (Kerala's 14 districts by rainfall) and S021 (38 TN districts, women online), rest on data that is not collected (RBI T102 is 14 meteorological sub-divisions; NFHS-6 district sheets and Census district tables are 'not collected' or only in SOURCE_MAP; IMD district rainfall and MGNREGA district data are 'script can fetch' but unfetched). Add a data-collection step first. Also unused state-level physical tables already in hand: forest and tree cover (T099, T101), heat and cold wave days (T103, T104), rainfall sub-divisions (T102), calamity relief (T105), irrigation (T055 to T057). Add one 12 to 15 minute 'Geography of India Explained, in official numbers' video.** (Short series for districts (needs data collection first). One long flagship for geography.): Measured: Map Motion's six Tamil Nadu district Shorts (smallest, cleanest, coldest, hottest, rainiest) have median 1.25M, minimum 684K. District rankings in English for other states are an open lane (searches found only small Hindi or Marathi channels, best 109K). Autocomplete shows 'richest district in india' and 'poorest district in india'. Long-form: FactSpark's 'The Geography of India Explained' reached 2.5M (50x its median; 12 of its 66 long videos are 10x+). The Graphic Earth's 'Crazy Geographical Borders of India' reached 3.5M. The geography/maps tag hits 3x+ in 27.3% of videos versus 14.7% (n=110). Caveats: Map Motion (228K subs) already owns the Tamil Nadu niche. Past videos on heat and air quality had medians under 6K (15 results each, rough gauge). State-level substitutes are weaker than district hits. Do not promise a district GDP series; no official source was confirmed.
- **'Best state for ___' decision guides that let the viewer pick the priority: to live, to work, to study, to raise a child, to start a business. One flagship, 'The best state depends on what you need: 12 official numbers', built from data already in hand: daily wages (T112 to T115), CPI (T108 to T111), unemployment (PLFS, T008, T009), per-capita income (T019), GER (T001), doctors and beds (T016, T017), power per person (T138), SDG score (T106), life expectancy (T007). Then one Short per use-case. Show per-indicator leaders, never a single composite winner.** (Long flagship (12 to 15 min) with Short cut-downs, one per use-case.): This answers segments no idea targets: job seekers, students, parents, returning NRIs. Roughly 80 'best state' suggestions exist (my keyword filter, includes some noise): best state in India for job, to live, to settle, for study, for business, healthcare, education, tourism. Measured ceilings: Dhruv Rathee's 'Which is the Best State in India?' 7.2M (10 min) and 'Why is South India more Developed than North India?' 12.9M. A rewatchable decision tool suits long-form watch hours. Caveats: volume per exact query is small. 'Safest' needs NCRB crime data (not collected, violence rule). Salary queries (239 suggestions) are mostly occupation salaries that official state data cannot answer, so do not chase them. A 'best' verdict risks the thin-lead and neutrality rules, so frame it as 'best for what'.
- **What-if and curiosity-gap Shorts built from tables no idea touches. (a) 'India without [State]': the share of the national total that goes missing (factories T116, GSDP T021, exports T181, milk T091), framed as contribution, not secession. (b) Curiosity-gap numbers from railways, tourism and bank credit. Examples read quickly from the last column, all unverified: railway route km (T145, 2024) Mizoram 1, Meghalaya 9, Arunachal Pradesh 12, versus Uttar Pradesh 8,823 (Sikkim is not in the table); domestic tourist visits (T013, 2024) UP 646.81 million versus foreign visits (T182) led by Maharashtra 37.05 lakh, with only UP in both top-4 lists; credit-deposit ratio (T153, 2025) Andhra Pradesh 151.1% versus Bihar 51.4% and Goa 26.7%, cross-checked with T154 because 'place of sanction' distorts. Also unused: power and losses (T138 to T148).** (Short): What-if and curiosity-gap are two measured breakout hooks: 'What If Tamil Nadu Never Existed' 12M, 'Which Is Tamil Nadu's Capital Before Chennai' 11M, 'Kashmir Is Smaller Than You Think' 7.4M. Railways and transport Shorts hit 1M+ in 18.6% of cases versus 12.4% (n=177). Search shows 'most tourist visited state in india' and 'top 10 states in india for tourism'. The list has 6 'if a state were a country' ideas and zero subtract-a-state or rail, tourism or credit ideas. Caveats: Data Duck's flat what-if comparisons sit at 15K to 41K, so execution decides. All-India shares are not yet computed. State tourism counts follow state methods (check the handbook footnotes). Apply the thin-lead rule: Tamil Nadu 306.84 million versus Karnataka 304.56 million, and Gujarat 22.74 versus UP 22.69 lakh, are too close for a rank claim.
- **Serve everyday-life segments (parents, students, small-business owners, farmers) from the two richest primary-source holdings, both nearly unused. NFHS-6 (101 indicators x 36 states, NFHS-5 and urban/rural built in; the only dataset STATE.md marks 'verified') appears in at most S021, L052 and L064. The 4,008 Lok Sabha answers appear only in S011 and L069 (virality 3 and 4). Make (a) one long 'NFHS-5 to NFHS-6: what changed in four years' household report card, led by everyday indicators (women with own phone or bank account, health insurance, private-hospital C-sections, internet use, overweight, diabetes risk), and (b) a weekly 'Parliament was told' Short on everyday answers (road accidents 23 answers, school dropouts and closures, doctor vacancies, anganwadi 34, MSME 54, Startup India 31) after the PDFs are read.** (Long for the NFHS report card (12 to 15 min). Short for each Parliament answer (one number, one map).): Batch-001 already holds 100 NFHS scripts (22 clue quizzes, 19 then-vs-now) but no long-form, and the new 81 ideas ignore NFHS. Then-vs-now Shorts reach 1M+ in 18.5% of cases versus 12.4%. The Lok Sabha index is the channel's one unique primary-source asset and fits the 'serve it hot' goal and insurance and edtech sponsors. Caveats: health and education topics under-index on Shorts (lift 0.73). 'stunting india' and 'central tax devolution' returned no suggestions. Lok Sabha numbers are unknown until someone reads the PDFs. NFHS cells marked '*' or '(x)' are unreliable. Word violence and child-marriage indicators neutrally or skip them.
- **A city and everyday-price lane. No idea names a city or a price series. Add city duels or 'next' questions (Chandigarh versus the rest of India, Pune, Hyderabad, Bengaluru) and price Shorts (petrol, onion, tomato, rent). The sources are in SOURCE_MAP but NOT collected: NHB RESIDEX housing index by city, CPCB air quality by city, Consumer Affairs daily prices (browser only), PPAC fuel prices by state (browser only), MoHUA Swachh Survekshan, Agmarknet mandi prices.** (Long for city duels (12 to 20 min). Short for price items.): Cities are a measured over-indexer: city titles hit 3x+ in 27.3% of long videos versus 14.7% (n=205) and are 10.0% of outliers against 5.4% of videos. Adarsh Gupta's 'Chandigarh Vs Rest of India' reached 853K (12x) and 'Pune will be the Silicon Valley of India?' 721K. Infra Talks megaproject videos give a steady 3x to 9x. Price Shorts hit 1M+ in 16% of cases versus 12.4% (n=200), and search has 79 price and 'cheapest place' suggestions. Caveats: the biggest price hit (19M, petrol under prime ministers) is political then-vs-now and off-limits. 'state wise inflation' returned no suggestions, so skip CPI-index explainers. Air quality videos had medians under 6K. Every city or price number needs collection and a source check first.
- **A cheap, pre-set test of the language and narration risk: text-led maps with little or no narration. Make 6 matched Shorts (3 hook types x 2 styles) from existing templates: (a) text-on-map, music only, big English labels, no voice, versus (b) MiniMax English narration. Alternate days and use different but matched states (for example Sikkim versus Puducherry origin stories, two paradox states, two state profiles) so the channel never uploads a duplicate. Judge on viewed-vs-swiped, average percentage viewed, and views against the 73K map-story and 6.2K chart yardsticks.** (Short): Language is the research's biggest reach risk: Hindi is the detected language for 93 of the top 125 Shorts across 25 channels (YouTube language field, indicative only). English chart Shorts are the weakest (Data Duck median 15K, Dataverse 33.5K). The only silent evidence is Bharat Mapping's text-on-map hits at 5.8M to 11M, but its median is 38K and only 29% reach 100K, so it is not a floor. The cost is a voice-off flag in the config, not a new template. No idea defines this test, so every other Short would confound format, hook and voice. Caveat: 6 videos give a direction, not significance, and a cold-start channel adds noise.
- **A hub-and-spoke release plan so one piece of research becomes several different videos. Each hub gets 1 long anchor, 4 to 6 Shorts with different hooks (paradox, then-vs-now, personalised 'your state', what-if, one quiz), 1 chart post, and an end card pointing to the long. The raw material exists but is scattered: North vs South (L012, L056, L034, S005), women in work (L079 with S004, S022, S039, L050, L060), 5G (L062 with S003, S020, S033), split states (L063, L073, L026 with S005, S016), wheat (L059, L077, S001). Example: L056 becomes 12 single-number 'round' Shorts, 1 Higher-or-Lower, 1 chart post and a 'what the numbers don't show' follow-up.** (Both. Long anchor plus Short spokes.): AK has 5 to 7 hours a week, and the list has 48 long ideas against 33 Shorts. By my count the 48 collapse to roughly 30 distinct videos, so a plan prevents building near-duplicates. The winners in the research are event-style hooks on a recurring visual system, not clones: one repeated template can work (Geography By Geoff's 68 videos, floor 139K), but near-duplicate rankings decayed (Top 10 Richest States 62K down to 2K). A different hook per spoke also lowers the 'inauthentic or repetitive content' risk. Shorts views do not count toward the 4,000 long-form watch hours, so the Shorts-to-long hand-off matters; its conversion rate is unmeasured.
- **Interactive and trust formats the 81 ideas lack. (a) 'Request your state': a pinned comment plus a recurring 'Your state next' Short that feeds the roster. (b) A poll-then-answer loop ('which comparison next?'). (c) A visible corrections and updates log, and a 'this number changed' Short when a source revises (PLFS annual report 25 Mar 2027, RBI Handbook in December). (d) One 8 to 10 minute 'Find it yourself' walkthrough that redraws, rather than films, the RBI, MoSPI and NFHS tables and explains current versus constant prices and the period and unit on every number.** (Short for requests, polls and corrections. One long for the walkthrough.): The stated goal is attention and trust, and primary-source citation is the only differentiator against chart channels (Visual Capitalist Shorts median 2,300, Statista 644). Personalised and argue-about-it hooks pull 0.09% to 0.15% comment rates versus a 0.013% median. Requests cost almost no engineering. The list has 12 quiz and game ideas but no viewer-driven loop. Caveats: comments alone do not create reach (Data Duck had 3,400 comments on only 233K views). Whether Community posts are available to a brand-new channel is unverified. Moderation takes time. Name no individuals.

Over-represented in the list: Quiz and guess formats: 12 of 81 ideas (S008, S029, S030, S031, S033, S037, S038, L034, L035, L042, L053, L076), 7 of the 33 Shorts, and batch-001 adds 22 Guess the State scripts. Measured: 35 quiz-style Indian-state Shorts on 31 channels have a median of 63 views (best 31K), and quiz/guess titles reach 1M+ in 5.9% of cases (lift 1.01). The Geo Facts quiz evidence is global and untested for India. L034, L042 and L053 are near-copies of one '10 rounds, pick the winner' long video. Keep one or two as a controlled test.; 'If a state were a country': 6 ideas for one concept (S009, S024, S040, L054, L066, L075). Flat country-comparison versions measure 15K to 41K (Data Duck). World In Details' repeated state-vs-country series decayed from Tamil Nadu vs Pakistan 135K to Uttar Pradesh vs Pakistan 6.7K. All six need World Bank or IMF data that is not collected. Merge into one long plus one or two Shorts.; Richest states and per-capita income: four near-identical longs (L013, L048, L061, L070), plus S041 and L049 on the same tables. The research says publish one definitive official-source version and refresh it yearly. Repeats of 'Top 10 Richest States' fell from 62K to 2K, and 82% of richest/poorest Shorts sit under 1,000 views.; North vs South and same-pair duels: L012 and L056 are the same video (12 official numbers, zone by zone) and L034 is its quiz; L044 and L057 are both Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu; S029 adds a third Karnataka pairing. About 11 state-duel ideas in total. This is the strongest measured bucket (63% at 3x+), so keep one definitive version per pair. Duels between mid-profile states without a stake fell under 0.5x of the median (Infra Talks).; Same-data repeats: women in the labour force (S004, S022, S039, L050, L060, L079), the 5G and Bihar paradox (S003, S020, S033, L062), split states (L026, L063, L073), wheat (S001, L059, L077), and Kerala (L045 and L072 both ask why Kerala is different). Also duplicate pairs: L014 and L027 (the map drawn), L067 and L071 (population map), L055 and L074 (India vs China). Together roughly 48 long ideas are about 30 distinct videos (my count).; Data-release and calendar pegs with low virality scores: S036 Inflation Day (the seed 'state wise inflation' returned no autocomplete suggestions), S038 Budget quiz, L068 India This Month (score 2, the lowest on the list), L080, L081. They compete with established creators (Budget explainer median 75K, Economic Survey 56K) and depend on a release calendar that the digest says may change.; Ideas whose data is not collected: about 14 (S009, S024, S040, L054, L066, L075 and L055, L074 need World Bank, IMF or UN data; S010 and S021 need district data; L067 and L071 need Census; L014 and L027 need historical state-boundary shapes). S011 and L069 depend on unread Lok Sabha PDFs. Meanwhile the one dataset marked verified (NFHS-6) is nearly unused. Each of these ideas must state its collection dependency.; State coverage skew: about 10 Tamil Nadu ideas, 7 Bihar and 6 Kerala, but no dedicated idea for Rajasthan, West Bengal, Delhi, Madhya Pradesh or the Northeast. Tamil Nadu's lead is evidence-backed (75% at 3x+ on 16 videos) and it is AK's home, so the tilt is defensible. It needs balancing with a state roster for Shorts, where 'your state' is the hook..

## Shorts: every idea in full

### S003. Bihar: Lowest Income Per Person, 5G in 96 of 100 Villages

- **Original title:** Bihar Has 5G in 96 of 100 Villages. Telangana Has It in 19
- **Status:** Shortlisted, survived the red team. **Score** 7.4 (virality 7, appeal 8, distinct 7, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** Data Paradox
- **Template:** Myth-buster
- **Hook:** Lowest income per person in India. 5G in 96 of every 100 villages.
- **Outline:** 1. Hook: the income table first, Bihar last at Rs 69,321 per person (2024-25). 2. Flip to the 5G village map (DoT, Feb 2026): Bihar lights up, 42,902 of 44,888 villages, 95.6%. 3. India's average for scale: 57.0%. 4. The other side: Telangana, near the top on income per person, 19.1%; Tamil Nadu 56.9%, Karnataka 65.8%, Maharashtra 69.8%. 5. The bottom of the table: Odisha 5.9%, Meghalaya 4.8%. 6. Guardrail line on screen: coverage means a 5G signal has reached the village, not speed or use. 7. Close: 'Where does your state stand? Comment.' with the full map held.
- **Who shares it and why:** A Bihari student in a bigger city sends 'lowest income, 5G in 96 of 100 villages' to friends from richer states as pride, and friends from Telangana, Odisha or Meghalaya answer with their own no-signal villages.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: pride plus a surprise that overturns a belief. A Bihari student in Bengaluru sends it to friends from richer states; friends from Telangana, Odisha or Meghalaya answer in the comments with ground-reality arguments ('my village has no network'). Share line: 'Lowest income per person. 5G in 96 of 100 villages.' The guardrail beat (coverage is not speed) pre-empts the biggest argument and keeps the debate about the numbers.
- **Why it could flop:** The 96% figure contradicts what many Bihar villagers see, so the comment section may be 'fake data' instead of shares. That would hurt a brand whose whole promise is trust, and the coverage file was typed by hand from the PDF. 'Village has 5G' says nothing about speed or use. The only precedent (FactTechz, 4.6M) came from a Hindi presenter-led channel, and the Telangana half of the title splits the hook.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** 5G villages as on 28 Feb 2026: Bihar 95.6% (42,902 of 44,888); Haryana 96.8%; Kerala 94.4%; Punjab 93.1%; Gujarat 85.2%; Maharashtra 69.8%; Karnataka 65.8%; Tamil Nadu 56.9%; Andhra Pradesh 49.2%; Telangana 19.1% (1,945 of 10,203); Odisha 5.9%; Meghalaya 4.8%; India 57.0% (367,156 of 644,131). Income per person 2024-25: Bihar Rs 69,321 (lowest of the 25 states and UTs with data, and lowest in every year of the table); Telangana Rs 3,87,623.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json: Bihar 42,902/44,888 = 95.6%, Telangana 1,945/10,203 = 19.1%, India 367,156/644,131 = 57.0%, and the other state percentages match; the hand-typed file's seven columns sum exactly to the published total row and every row is internally consistent, so only an eyeball of the Bihar and Telangana rows against the PDF remains. RBI T019 (per capita NSDP, current prices) has Bihar Rs 69,321 lowest of the 25 reporting in 2024-25 and lowest in every year, and Telangana Rs 3,87,623 second of those 25 (Sikkim and Goa, above it in 2023-24, have no 2024-25 value), so keep 'near the top' and label it per capita NSDP.
- **Source:** public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json (Lok Sabha Starred Question 470, answered 25 March 2026, Annex II, Dept of Telecommunications; villages as per Registrar General of India); RBI Handbook T019 (per capita NSDP, current prices, 2024-25) for the income frame. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** The state-data paradox is a proven Shorts breakout: FactTechz 'Bihar is No. 1 in Terms of DATA (Internet Usage) While Having Lowest Penetration' reached 4.6M in 9 days, and 'your state' hooks pull 7-12x the median comment rate (0.09-0.15% against 0.013%). Caution: Bihar as a long-form subject is weak (17% of 6 videos reached 3x), so this is a Shorts bet only.
- **Risks:** Bihar is not No. 1 (Puducherry and Delhi are 100%, Haryana 96.8%), so never say it leads. The coverage file was typed by hand from the PDF: re-check the Bihar and Telangana rows against the PDF before voicing. 'Villages with 5G' is DoT's wording ('villages where 5G services have been brought'), not users or speed. Do not explain the gap with an unsourced theory. No mockery of Telangana or Bihar. Differs from batch-001's NFHS-only paradox scripts (S079-S084), which use no RBI or DoT data.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** keep. Closest copy of a measured breakout (FactTechz Bihar paradox, 4.6M in 9 days): one clean contrast and a built-in Bihar-pride versus other-states argument; put the 'coverage is not speed' guardrail after the payoff and re-check the hand-typed rows first.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. A single DoT-sourced fact (95.6% vs 19.1% of villages) with a built-in 'villages, not people, speed or use' guardrail; before voicing, check the Bihar and Telangana rows and the sum of the typed state rows against the PDF's printed India total, and say 'lowest of the 25 states and UTs with 2024-25 data'.
- **Red team (viewer):** keep. The two-number hook (lowest income, 5G in 96 of 100 villages) flips a belief in three seconds, Punjab at 93.1% gets a pat on the back, and it needs no stake in any one state, but cut it to Bihar, Haryana, Punjab and India (drop the Tamil Nadu/Karnataka/Maharashtra/Odisha tour) and add UP and Assam rows to the closing map.

### S032. Bengal Produces Less Than Half the Fish Andhra Pradesh Does

- **Original title:** Who Makes More Fish: Andhra Pradesh, or Bengal + UP Together?
- **Status:** Shortlisted, survived the red team. **Score** 7.3 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 7, fit 7, risk low)
- **Series:** Bigger Than Two
- **Template:** new: Bigger-than-two bar (one bar against two stacked bars; a variant of the countdown beat)
- **Hook:** Which is bigger: Andhra Pradesh's fish, or West Bengal and Uttar Pradesh combined?
- **Outline:** 1) Hook: three unlabelled bars, viewer guess pause. 2) Bar 1 fills: Andhra Pradesh 51.58 lakh tonnes (2023-24, provisional). 3) Bars 2 and 3 stack: West Bengal 22.02 plus Uttar Pradesh 11.60 = 33.62; the single bar wins. 4) Scale made literal: Andhra Pradesh alone is 51.58 of India's 184.02. 5) Lightning round: cold storage, Uttar Pradesh 14.88 million tonnes against West Bengal plus Gujarat 9.84 million; jute, West Bengal 7,027.6 of India's 8,801.6 thousand bales. 6) Close: 'Which state do you think is bigger than the next two combined? Comment it' (the next episode uses Maharashtra's financial services against Tamil Nadu plus Delhi).
- **Who shares it and why:** Bengali viewers send it to friends expecting to win and then argue 'production is not what we eat', while Andhra viewers share it as a rare national first.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Pride and counter-pride. Bengali viewers share it expecting to win and argue in the comments ('production is not what we eat', 'you left out hilsa'); Andhra viewers share it as a rare national first. The share trigger is the stacked bar of two big states still losing to one. The guess pause makes people replay to check their call.
- **Why it could flop:** The 'Bengal + UP combined' stack is an arbitrary pairing; a viewer asks why those two, and a clean 'Andhra Pradesh makes 2.3x Bengal's fish' is simpler and just as shocking. The lightning round (cold storage, jute) adds two unrelated facts that dilute a clean hook and push the length. The numbers are provisional (2023-24 P), inland and marine are mixed, and RBI warns state figures may not add up to the national total, so any 'combined' arithmetic needs care. Production is not consumption, so the Bengali counter-argument is valid and the Short must say so on screen. Check Andhra Pradesh is first in T093 before any 'top' wording. English narration of a numbers Short is the weakest reach family measured; the Bengal-identity hook is what has to carry it.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** T093 2023-24 (P), lakh tonnes: India 184.02; Andhra Pradesh 51.58; West Bengal 22.02; Uttar Pradesh 11.60. T098 2022 (tonnes): India 38,224,006; Uttar Pradesh 14,875,987; West Bengal 5,948,316; Gujarat 3,888,425. T066 2024-25 (thousand bales of 180 kg): India 8,801.6; West Bengal 7,027.6; Bihar 846.4. T045 2024-25 current prices (Rs lakh): Maharashtra 45,413,753; Tamil Nadu 15,084,148; Delhi 14,560,320.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. RBI T093 gives Andhra Pradesh 51.58 vs West Bengal 22.02 + Uttar Pradesh 11.60 = 33.62 (28.0% of India's 184.02); T098 (2022) gives 14,875,987 vs 5,948,316 + 3,888,425 = 9,836,741; T066 gives West Bengal 7,027.6 of 8,801.6; T045 gives Maharashtra 45,413,753 vs Tamil Nadu + Delhi 29,644,468, all matching. Uttar Pradesh is third in fish by only 0.29 lakh tonnes over Karnataka (11.31), so do not call it 'the next two' without that care, cold storage is a 30 Nov 2022 snapshot, and Gujarat has no 2023-24 or 2024-25 value in T045.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States T093 (fish production; Ministry of Fisheries), T098 (cold storage; Agriculture Statistics at a Glance), T066 (raw jute and mesta), T045 (financial services value added). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Scale made literal and a curiosity-gap fact in one question. Curiosity-gap titles reach 1M+ in 21.4% of Shorts (baseline 12.4%); ibx2cat's 'This American state has a larger GDP than 193 countries' got 1.0M; Map Motion's population-scale Shorts reached 1.3M-3.4M. Each question puts a state with a famous identity (Bengal and fish) against a data fact that does not match, which is the paradox structure behind FactTechz's 4.6M Short.
- **Risks:** Production is not consumption; say so on screen. Fish includes inland plus marine, and 2023-24 is provisional (P). State figures may not add to the all-India total (RBI note). Keep each claim to the table (do not infer why). Avoid implying any state is 'behind' overall. Cold-storage dates vary by state (RBI note). Totals favour big states, so use this only for share and concentration stories.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** keep. Bengal's fish identity is overturned (22.02 vs Andhra's 51.58, under half) and the guess pause gives a real counter-pride and rewatch trigger; re-cut to a plain Bengal-vs-Andhra bar and drop the arbitrary Bengal+UP stack and the cold-storage and jute lightning round.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. Plain arithmetic on RBI tables (51.58 vs 22.02+11.60 lakh t; 14.88 vs 5.95+3.89 million t) with no causal claim; just label provisional, 'production not consumption', and the different years in the lightning round.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. Bengal plus UP stacked against Andhra is an arbitrary pairing that only Andhra and Bengal fish-eaters care about, and the lightning round (cold storage, jute) is data-nerd filler with no reason to forward it.

### SG01. Puducherry Is 4 Separate Pieces Inside 3 States. How?

- **Original title:** How Puducherry Became Part of India (It's in 4 Pieces)
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.8 (virality 7, appeal 7, distinct 7, fit 6, risk low)
- **Series:** How [Place] Became Part of India
- **Template:** new: one-place origin story on the official-boundary map (Beat-family pacing, one thing on screen at a time, a year stamp plus the named Act or treaty on each beat, inset zooms for tiny enclaves, closing 'today' number card)
- **Hook:** This Indian territory is in four separate pieces, next to three different states. How?
- **Outline:** Beat 1 (0-3s) Hook over the official map: Puducherry, Karaikal, Mahe and Yanam light up one by one, each ringed in the colour of the state around it (Tamil Nadu, Kerala, Andhra Pradesh). Beat 2 French India: the enclaves stay under France until 1 Nov 1954, when administration passes to India (de facto). Beat 3 The paperwork: the Treaty of Cession (signed 1956, in force 16 Aug 1962) makes it legal. Beat 4 The Constitution (Fourteenth Amendment) Act, 1962 and the Union Territories Act, 1963 give it a legislature. Beat 5 Today card: 5G reaches 96 of 96 villages (DoT, 28 Feb 2026) and stunted children are 16.6% (NFHS-6; 20.0% in NFHS-5), the lowest of the 34 states/UTs with reliable cells. Beat 6 Close: 'Which place next?' with the slate. SERIES SLATE, 6-8 episodes, one place per Short, about 40 s each (every date below is from memory, verify against the gazetted Act): Puducherry (peg 1 Nov, 1954 transfer), Lakshadweep (peg 1 Nov; see the name-origin idea), Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu (peg 26 Jan, merger effective 2020), Himachal Pradesh (peg 25 Jan, statehood 1971), Meghalaya (peg 21 Jan, statehood 1972), Sikkim (16 May, outside the window; used as the voice-off test), Goa (19 Dec; S015 already covers it). Each episode ends on one 'today' number from our files, e.g. DNH&DD 5G in 94 of 95 villages (98.9%), Himachal Pradesh women's labour force 67.6%, Meghalaya women's labour force 71.8% yet 5G in only 4.8% of villages. Skipped on purpose: Hyderabad, Junagadh, Kashmir, Nagaland-style cases.
- **Who shares it and why:** People in Tamil Nadu, Kerala and Andhra Pradesh forward the frame where the four enclaves light up inside three different states ('Mahe and Yanam are inside Puducherry?'), and geography-trivia fans drop it in class and office groups.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** How a place came to be, plus a curiosity gap. Shared by people in Tamil Nadu, Kerala and Andhra Pradesh with relatives ('Mahe and Yanam are inside Puducherry?') and by geography-trivia fans in class and office groups. Likely comments: 'I am from Karaikal', 'why is Yanam in Puducherry', 'do Goa / Sikkim next'. Share trigger: the frame where the four enclaves light up in three different neighbour-state colours under the line 'One territory. Three neighbours.'
- **Why it could flop:** The origin lane is the best-measured hook (26.7% at 1M+ vs 12.4%), but its winners (Jodhpur, Hyderabad, Goa, Nagaland) carry conflict, and Puducherry's was a peaceful treaty; beats 3 and 4 (treaty, two Acts) are the driest in the lane. The 5G and stunting 'today' card has nothing to do with how the place joined India, so it breaks one-thing-at-a-time and hands swipers an exit. The winners are Hindi-narrated and English reach is unproven, and every date and the four-pieces fact still come from memory until read in the gazette.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Puducherry: 5G in 96 of 96 villages (100%), DoT as on 28 Feb 2026. Stunted children under 5: 16.6% in NFHS-6 (2023-24) vs 20.0% in NFHS-5; India 29.3%. Slate extras: DNH&DD 5G in 94 of 95 villages (98.9%); Meghalaya women's labour force participation 71.8% (PLFS 2023-24, age 15+) and 5G in 4.8% of villages; Himachal Pradesh women's labour force 67.6%.
- **Data editor:** source exists, numbers not re-checked. The today card recomputes: mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json gives Puducherry 96 of 96 and DNH&DD 94 of 95 (98.9%); nfhs6_states.json indicator 69 gives Puducherry 16.6% vs 20.0% in NFHS-5 and India 29.3%, the lowest of the 35 rows with data (Chandigarh next at 19.0); plfs_annual.json gives Meghalaya 71.8% and Himachal 67.6%. The history (1 Nov 1954, the 1956 treaty, 16 Aug 1962, the 1962 Amendment, the 1963 Act) is in no local file and India Code, legislative.gov.in and egazette are absent from SOURCE_MAP.md, so every date and the 'four pieces, three states' hook stay unchecked (Puducherry and Karaikal are reportedly split into several enclaves themselves, so check the boundary file, and use the Act's full name 'Government of Union Territories Act, 1963').
- **Source:** Acts and treaty text from the Gazette of India (India Code / legislative.gov.in / egazette.gov.in; none of these is in SOURCE_MAP.md yet, so add them first). Today card: public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json (DoT figures, Lok Sabha Starred Question 470, 25 Mar 2026) and public/datayt/nfhs6/nfhs6_states.json (NFHS-6, indicator 69, Puducherry). (answer PDF still to read)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 1 (origin series). Measured: state formation, merger, history and what-if-state Shorts reach 1M+ in 26.7% of cases vs a 12.4% baseline (60 Shorts, 22 channels); the neutral 'became part of India' lane has 15 matching Shorts from only 9 channels, median 383K, 67% at 100K+; Yash Rajput's 21 border-history Shorts have a median of 1.3M against a 139K channel median; prince.maps1k's Lakshadweep origin Short reached 1.2M. Only S015 (Goa) is the pure form today, so this adds the missing 5-6 places. The four-pieces fact adds the second measured hook, a curiosity gap (Map Motion 'Which Is Tamil Nadu's Capital Before Chennai' 11M).
- **Risks:** All dates, the treaty details and the four-pieces / three-states fact come from memory and must be checked against the gazetted Act and treaty text before scripting. Enclaves are tiny, so use inset zooms and keep the India outline whole (official boundary only). The viral precedents are Hindi-narrated, so English reach is unproven (the voice-off test idea runs the matched pair). Keep colonial history factual, no mockery of any country, community or place, no 'could have gone to X' framing. Re-read the NFHS-6 cell reliability flags before saying 'lowest'.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** keep. The hook is a pure geography oddity (four pieces, three states) that travels like the 7.4M Kashmir-size Short, in the only lane with a measured high floor (median 383K); cut the 5G and stunting 'today' card, accept it lacks the conflict that carried Goa and Jodhpur, and verify every date against the Acts.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. Every date, treaty and Act is from memory, 'four pieces' is loose (each of the four regions is itself split into enclaves, so say 'four regions'), and the 5G and stunting 'today' card tacked onto a history story implies the merger explains them.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. Four enclaves in three South states is a fun fact for Tamil, Kerala and Telugu viewers only, and the Act and treaty beats plus the random 5G/stunting card turn it into a lecture for everyone else.

### S008. Gujarat, Home of Amul, Is 4th in Milk. Can You Score 5 of 5?

- **Original title:** Higher or Lower: India's States Edition. Can You Score 5 of 5?
- **Status:** Shortlisted, survived the red team. **Score** 6.8 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 7, fit 8, risk low)
- **Series:** Higher or Lower
- **Template:** state vs state over five rounds, re-skinned as a higher-or-lower duel (two state names, a 2-second think beat, then the real numbers and a running score)
- **Hook:** Higher or lower: which makes more milk, Madhya Pradesh or Gujarat?
- **Outline:** 1. Hook and round 1: milk 2023-24, Madhya Pradesh 21.33 million tonnes beats Gujarat 18.31 million (Gujarat is fourth). 2. Round 2: size of the economy 2024-25, Tamil Nadu Rs 31.19 lakh crore beats Uttar Pradesh Rs 29.78 lakh crore (4.7% ahead). 3. Round 3: rice 2024-25, Telangana 17.45 million tonnes beats West Bengal 16.02 million. 4. Round 4: fish 2023-24, Uttar Pradesh 11.6 lakh tonnes beats Kerala 8.32. 5. Round 5: electricity available per person 2024-25, Goa 3,417.6 kWh beats Punjab 2,503.4. 6. Score card: '5 of 5?' with the pairs replayed in one line each. 7. Close: 'Your score in the comments. Which one fooled you?'
- **Who shares it and why:** A college group-chat member posts 'I scored 3 of 5, beat me' and friends race to beat the score, but only if round 1 hooks them.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: a quiz the viewer can fail. A college group chat gets 'I scored 3 of 5, beat me'; people comment their score and the round that fooled them ('Gujarat is Amul, this can't be right'). Share trigger: round 1 flipping a brand belief (Amul's state is fourth in milk) in the first seconds. Rewatch: viewers replay to check what they missed. Each round is a separate sharable clip.
- **Why it could flop:** There is no Indian precedent for state quiz Shorts (median 63 views, best 31K), and the Geo Facts evidence is global. Five unrelated pairs across nine states give no single identity pull, so viewers lack a reason to share beyond a score. Round 2 is a 4.7% gap on current prices with Gujarat missing from the table. A 60-second quiz with a think beat per round is slow, and viewers who miss the first pair swipe away. Only round 1 (Amul's state is 4th in milk) is a real share trigger.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Milk 2023-24 (thousand tonnes): Madhya Pradesh 21,326; Gujarat 18,312. GSDP 2024-25 current prices (Rs lakh): Tamil Nadu 311,859,030; Uttar Pradesh 297,822,351. Rice 2024-25 (thousand tonnes): Telangana 17,445.7; West Bengal 16,019.4. Fish 2023-24 (P), lakh tonnes: Uttar Pradesh 11.6; Kerala 8.32. Power availability per capita 2024-25 (kWh): Goa 3,417.6; Punjab 2,503.4. Percentage gap for round 2 (4.7%) is computed.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from RBI T091, T021, T058, T093 and T138: Madhya Pradesh 21,326 vs Gujarat 18,312; Tamil Nadu 311,859,030 vs Uttar Pradesh 297,822,351 lakh (+4.71%, Rs 31.19 vs 29.78 lakh crore); Telangana 17,445.7 vs West Bengal 16,019.4; Uttar Pradesh 11.60 vs Kerala 8.32; Goa 3,417.6 vs Punjab 2,503.4 (Goa highest of 34 rows), all matching. Gujarat is fourth in milk by only 0.95 pp of India's output over Maharashtra, so say 'outside the top three' rather than 'fourth', and keep the label that Gujarat has no 2024-25 GSDP in T021.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook: T091 (milk), T021 (GSDP, current prices), T058 (rice), T093 (fish), T138 (power availability per person). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Geo Facts' clue-style quizzes fill 5 of its top 10 (0.7M-1.6M each), and comment rates for 'argue about it' formats run 7-12x the median. Honest caution: Indian state quiz Shorts have no viral precedent in our search (35 Shorts on 31 channels, median 63 views, best 31K), so test three before building a series. The score-in-comments ending is built to create replies, which is the measured behaviour.
- **Risks:** No Indian precedent for quiz Shorts; treat as a test. Thin leads are avoided, but round 2 is only 4.7% ahead and must be labelled current prices, 2024-25, among states with data (Gujarat has no 2024-25 GSDP in the table). Fish is provisional. 'Electricity available per person' is supply availability, not use. Never claim viewers usually get it wrong (not measured). Add a pinned comment with each table number for people who dispute the answer.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. Indian state quiz Shorts have no viral precedent (35 Shorts, median 63 views, best 31K) and five unrelated pairs across nine states give no single identity pull.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. Five independent head-to-heads that recompute with no causal or sensitive content; replace or label round 3 (Telangana leads West Bengal by only 0.95 points of India's rice, 2024-25 stage unconfirmed) and label round 2 as current prices with revision risk (4.7% gap).
- **Red team (viewer):** keep. A guess-then-reveal game with the Amul flip in round 1 keeps me watching to see my score, and UP beating Kerala on fish plus the narrow TN-over-UP economy give me something to argue; trim to four rounds and drop the Telangana vs West Bengal rice pair nobody outside those states cares about.

### S016. India's Youngest State Now Grows More Rice Than Punjab (2024-25)

- **Original title:** A 12-Year-Old State Is Now a Top-3 Rice Producer
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.7 (virality 5, appeal 5, distinct 6, fit 9, risk low)
- **Series:** Young States
- **Template:** existing: Beat family top-five countdown (rice production, 2024-25) with one then-vs-now beat for the highlighted state. Config only; later episodes: Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Uttarakhand (born November 2000, dates to verify).
- **Hook:** Telangana is India's youngest state. It is already one of the top three rice producers.
- **Outline:** 30-35 s. 1) Hook: India map, Telangana lit, stamp 'a state since 2 June 2014' (Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014; verify). 2) Rice area up from 1,418.4 to 4,809.5 thousand hectares, 2012-13 to 2024-25 (+239%). 3) Top-five countdown, rice production 2024-25: Chhattisgarh 10.4, Punjab 14.4, West Bengal 16.0, Telangana 17.4, Uttar Pradesh 20.8 million tonnes. 4) Then vs now: 4th in 2020-21 with 10.2 million tonnes; top three in each of the last three years. 5) All foodgrains: 8.2 to 21.6 million tonnes (+163%). 6) On-screen caveat: 'The table shows what, not why.' 7) Close: 'Which state has risen fastest where you live? Comment it.'
- **Who shares it and why:** Telangana viewers, including those abroad, send it to Andhra, Punjab and West Bengal friends as a rice-bowl bragging point, and farm and exam groups pass it on.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: surprise that overturns a belief ('rice means Punjab and Bengal') plus new-state pride. Telangana viewers send it to friends in Andhra Pradesh, Punjab and West Bengal; farm and exam groups pass it on. Expect: 'we were number one in 2023-24', yield per hectare, irrigation, 'Punjab is still better per acre'. Trigger frame: the Telangana bar sliding past Punjab and West Bengal in the countdown.
- **Why it could flop:** A crop-production countdown is a chart Short in a story costume, and only Telangana and farm audiences care. The top-five countdown graphic shows Telangana at 2nd place, which contradicts the rule against saying 'second' on a 0.95-point lead over West Bengal, so the template needs an unranked 'top three' variant. The +239% area rise starts at a 2012-13 base that pre-dates the state and may be a weak year (4,647.6 thousand t then, 10,217.1 in 2020-21), and rank swung between years (1st in 2023-24, 2nd in 2024-25), so check the whole series before using the endpoints.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Rice production 2024-25 (thousand tonnes): Uttar Pradesh 20,758.7; Telangana 17,445.7; West Bengal 16,019.4; Punjab 14,360.9; Chhattisgarh 10,368.5; India 150,184.3. Telangana: 4,647.6 (2012-13), 10,217.1 (2020-21, 4th), 16,874.1 (2023-24, 1st). Rice area 1,418.4 to 4,809.5 thousand ha. All foodgrains 8,232.7 to 21,634.6 thousand t.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from RBI T058, T067 and T062: the 2024-25 top five and India 150,184.3 match; Telangana ranks 4th in 2020-21 (10,217.1), then 2nd, 1st, 2nd in 2022-23 to 2024-25; rice area 1,418.4 to 4,809.5 is +239.1% and foodgrains 8,232.7 to 21,634.6 is +162.8%; the margins over West Bengal (0.95 pp) and Uttar Pradesh (0.64 pp) are as the risks line says. The 2012-13 start predates the 2014 state (the table back-casts it) and is a low-area year, so use 2014-15 (area 1,415.0 gives +240%, production +293%), check Uttar Pradesh's 30% one-year rice jump (15,990 to 20,759 vs India +9%) before voicing it, and read the 2 June 2014 date from the Act or notification, which is not in SOURCE_MAP.md.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T058.json (rice production), T067.json (rice area), T062.json (all foodgrains); original agency (Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers' Welfare) from the table note. Statehood date: Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014 (India Code, not in SOURCE_MAP). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Story-led one-thing-at-a-time Shorts beat chart Shorts about 12x on median (73K vs 6.2K). A state-data surprise is a named breakout hook (FactTechz Bihar 4.6M in 9 days) and a fixed series has a high floor (Geo Study state series: 24 Shorts, median 982,500, minimum 212K). Telangana interest is proven in long-form: Angel One 'Telangana vs Andhra Pradesh: Who is Winning?' 494K, 61x its channel median, on a 28K-subscriber channel.
- **Risks:** Thin lead (hard rule): Telangana's margin over West Bengal in 2024-25 is 1,426 thousand t, which is 0.95 percentage points of India's rice, and in 2023-24 it led Uttar Pradesh by 0.64 points. So say 'top three in each of the last three years', never 'second' or 'first'. Statehood and irrigation credit are political: no causes, parties or leaders. Pre-2014 Telangana values are recast to its present area: read the table note before scripting. One monsoon year can reorder the top three.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. A chart Short in a story costume that only Telangana and farm audiences care about, with a thin lead over West Bengal that forces a hedged 'top three' claim.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. A 'state since 2 June 2014' stamp plus a then-vs-now beat from 2012-13 implies statehood caused the rice rise in a politically charged topic, the +239% and +163% rest on a single pre-statehood base year, and the countdown graphic shows Telangana 2nd on a 0.95-point lead while the voice-over must say 'top three'.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. A Telangana rice countdown is a South-and-farm-group story, and it parks Punjab at 4th as the foil, which a Punjabi viewer will read as a dig at the rice bowl.

### S006. For Every 100 Units of Groundwater Punjab Can Use, It Takes 157

- **Original title:** Punjab Uses 157% of Its Yearly Extractable Groundwater
- **Status:** Shortlisted, survived the red team. **Score** 6.6 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 6, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Template:** Breakdown (100-square grid), small change: extra squares spill past 100
- **Hook:** 100 squares = the groundwater Punjab can extract in a year. It takes out 157.
- **Outline:** 1. Hook text with an empty 100-square grid. 2. The grid fills: 17.63 billion cubic metres can be extracted each year in Punjab. 3. The squares overflow: Punjab actually extracts 27.66 billion, 156.87% (57 extra red squares). 4. Who else is over the line in 2024: Rajasthan 149.86, Haryana 135.96, Delhi 100.77; India overall about 60.5%. 5. The direction: Punjab was 165.77 in 2017 and is 156.87 in 2024, improving but still over 100. 6. Close: the all-states map in green and red, 'Is your state over 100? Comment.'
- **Who shares it and why:** Punjab, Haryana and Rajasthan farm and family groups, plus environment-minded friends elsewhere, forward the frame where 57 red squares spill past the grid, each to argue paddy, power and rain.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: a strong but fair question ('can this last?') made literal. People in Punjab, Haryana and Rajasthan share it in farm and family groups; environment-minded viewers forward it to friends in other states. Expected comments: arguments about paddy, power and rain, and 'what is my state's number?'. Share trigger: the frame where 100 squares fill and 57 red squares spill out. The map close drives your-state replies.
- **Why it could flop:** 'Stage of extraction' is jargon, and viewers will read 157% as 'Punjab will run dry' or as Punjab-bashing. We are barred from naming policy, party or farmers, so the 'why' comments go unanswered and the Short can read as alarm without explanation. Water-crisis explainers are a common topic. Delhi's 100.77 is barely over the line and invites a thin-margin objection. Scale-made-literal is a proven format, but this version has no hook beyond the number.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Stage of ground water extraction 2024 (%): Punjab 156.87; Rajasthan 149.86; Haryana 135.96; Delhi 100.77; India 60.475. Punjab 2024: extractable resource 17.63 and extraction 27.66 billion cubic metres; stage was 165.77 in 2017, 164.42 in 2020, 165.99 in 2022, 163.76 in 2023. Context: Punjab is third in wheat (17,993.2 thousand tonnes) and fourth in rice (14,360.9 thousand tonnes) in 2024-25 (T059, T058).
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. RBI T107 (Central Ground Water Board) gives Punjab 2024 extraction 27.66 / extractable 17.63 billion cubic metres = 156.9% (table 156.87, so 57 extra squares); Rajasthan 149.86, Haryana 135.96 and Delhi 100.77 are the only other states above 100, India 60.475, and Punjab's 165.77, 164.42, 165.99, 163.76 history matches; T059 and T058 give Punjab third in wheat (17,993.2) and fourth in rice (14,360.9). Punjab is 7 points ahead of Rajasthan, so 'clear outlier' is overstated, Delhi is over by only 0.77, and the 2017-to-2024 fall is assessment to assessment (both the resource, 21.58 to 17.63, and the extraction, 35.78 to 27.66, changed), so avoid 'recovering'.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T107 (State-wise Status of Ground Water Extraction; annual extractable resource, annual extraction and stage of extraction, assessments 2017 to 2024); underlying publisher Central Ground Water Board, Ministry of Jal Shakti. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Scale made literal is one of the winning formats in the benchmark (VC2 technique; ibx2cat 'This American state has a larger GDP than 193 countries' 1.0M), and why/how explainers reached 1M+ in 16.9% of 593 India-titled Shorts against 12.4% baseline. Four places over 100 and one clear outlier give a concrete frame, and a Punjab hook adds the identity pull.
- **Risks:** Farm-policy territory (free power, support prices, parties): stay on the measured number, never name a policy, party or group of farmers. 'Stage of extraction' is extraction divided by the annually extractable resource, not a depletion forecast; do not say a state 'will run dry' or give years. Some assessment units inside a state are safe, so say 'state average'. The extra-squares animation is a small template change. Verify the 2024 table against the CGWB assessment release in case a 2025 edition is out.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** keep. A 100-square grid overflowing by 57 red squares is a genuine scale-made-literal visual with a Punjab identity pull and a your-state close (Rajasthan, Haryana, Delhi also over 100), but replace the jargon 'stage of extraction' and keep strictly to the measured number.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. An official CGWB ratio (27.66/17.63 = 156.9%) stated with its correct definition; drop or caveat 'improving' unless the 2017 and 2024 assessments are shown to use comparable methods, and keep crops, policy and farmers off screen.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. A Punjab viewer feels blamed, everyone else gets a doom lecture, and because the rules bar naming any cause or policy the Short raises 'why?' and never answers it.

### S020. The State With India's Lowest Income Has 5G in 95.6% of Villages

- **Original title:** Bihar: Lowest Income Per Person, but 5G in 95.6% of Villages
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.6 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 6, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** Two Lists, One State
- **Template:** new: Two Lists. One state outline between two rank ladders: a list where the state is last, a list where it is near the top, then a 'catching up' line. Config = state, up to three entries (indicator, value, rank, India value, source), bridge line, caveat. Later episodes: Odisha, Meghalaya, Delhi, Goa.
- **Hook:** Bihar has India's lowest income per person. It also has 5G in 95.6% of its villages.
- **Outline:** 35 s. 1) Hook. 2) List one: income per person ₹69,321 (2024-25); the next lowest, Uttar Pradesh, ₹108,572. 3) List two: 5G reaches 42,902 of 44,888 villages (95.6%) against 57.0% for India (Department of Telecommunications, 28 Feb 2026). 4) List three: power available per person rose from 121 to 343.9 kWh in 12 years, 2.8x, the fastest rise of any state (India 1.6x). 5) Honest line: 'Still under a third of India's 1,208.2 kWh.' 6) Caveat: 'Network reach is not income.' 7) Close: 'Which state is last on one list and near the top on another? Comment it.'
- **Who shares it and why:** Biharis, including students and migrants in other cities, share it in reply to put-down posts, and everyone else passes on the 'lowest income, near-top 5G' surprise.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: state-data paradox plus underdog pride. Biharis (students, migrants in other cities) share it in reply to put-down posts; others share the surprise. Expect: '5G is not development', remittances, 'Bihar is rising' against 'coverage is not income', and requests for Jharkhand and Odisha (Odisha is the reverse case: 5G in only 5.9% of villages). Trigger frame: the two ladders side by side, last on one and 95.6% on the other.
- **Why it could flop:** The paradox is soft: 5G village coverage likely reflects where operators built networks, not whether people can afford a plan, so the first comments will say 'coverage is not development', and the 'but' in the title can read as a jab at the poorest state. Three lists in 35 seconds blur the single punch, Bihar is only 5th on 5G, it clones FactTechz's proven hook rather than adding one, and Bihar long-form place videos mostly do not hit (17% at 3x+).
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Bihar income per person ₹69,321 (T019, 2024-25, 25 states reporting); Uttar Pradesh ₹108,572. Bihar 5G villages 42,902 of 44,888 = 95.6%; India 57.0% (367,156 of 644,131). Bihar power per person 121 kWh (2012-13) to 343.9 (2024-25); India 740.6 to 1,208.2.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from RBI T019, T138 and mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json: Bihar Rs 69,321 vs Uttar Pradesh Rs 108,572 (next lowest of 25), 42,902/44,888 = 95.6% vs India 57.0% with Bihar fifth on 5G, and power per person 121 to 343.9 kWh = 2.84x, the fastest of 34 rows (Chhattisgarh next at 2.16x; India 740.6 to 1,208.2 = 1.63x), with 343.9 = 28.5% of 1,208.2. This is the same Bihar story as S003, so keep only one, and the 2.8x rise starts from a very low base, which the 'still under a third' line already covers.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T019.json and T138.json (RBI Handbook; confirm original agencies from the table notes); public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json (Lok Sabha Starred Question 470, answered 25 March 2026, Department of Telecommunications, as on 28 Feb 2026). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** A state-data paradox is a named breakout hook: FactTechz 'Bihar is No. 1 in Terms of DATA (Internet Usage) While Having Lowest Penetration' reached 4.6M in 9 days. Bihar is among the most-typed states (59 suggestions). Caveat from the long-form data: Bihar place videos do not reliably hit (17% at 3x+ on 6 videos), so treat this as a Short test only.
- **Risks:** Mocking risk: Bihar-as-lowest can read as a jab. Lead with the 5G and power rises, give all three lists the same neutral styling, never use 'backward' or acronym nicknames. Rank traps: Bihar is 5th on 5G (Puducherry 100, Delhi 100, DNH and DD 98.9, Haryana 96.8), so say 'above 95%', never 'first'; Manipur (328.9) is lower than Bihar (343.9) on power per person, so never say 'lowest power'; the 2024-25 income column lacks nine states and UTs, though Bihar is lowest across all 34 in 2022-23 (₹54,637). It is not a strict paradox (coverage is not income): call it 'two different lists'.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. A softer duplicate of S003 whose extra power-per-person beat dilutes the paradox and whose Bihar-as-lowest framing adds mocking risk, so ship S003 only.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. The same DoT and RBI facts as S003 with honest caveats built in; the 'fastest rise of any state' power claim must be scanned across all rows (it is a ratio from a low base, and the absolute gap to India widened), and Bihar must be styled neutrally as 5th, not 1st, on 5G.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. Same story as S003 but it opens by naming Bihar as lowest and adds a third power-per-person list that turns a clean surprise into a three-part data lecture; run S003 only.

### S002. Tamil Nadu Has the Most Factories. Gujarat Makes the Most Output

- **Original title:** Who Is India's Factory Capital? Tamil Nadu, Gujarat, Maharashtra
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.5 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 6, fit 7, risk low)
- **Series:** Three Crowns
- **Template:** state vs state over five rounds (small change: three lanes, one crown per measure)
- **Hook:** Which state is India's factory capital? There are three answers.
- **Outline:** 1. Hook: three state names, one question. 2. Count the factories (2023-24): Tamil Nadu 40,121, Gujarat 33,311, Maharashtra 26,539. Crown 1 to Tamil Nadu. 3. Count the people: Tamil Nadu 2.48 million factory workers out of India's 15.52 million (about 1 in 6). 4. Count the money: Gujarat has Rs 9.03 lakh crore of fixed capital and Rs 26.39 lakh crore of output, the most of any state, and 26.6% of India's goods exports. Crown 2 to Gujarat. 5. Count what is left after costs: net value added, Maharashtra Rs 3.41 lakh crore, ahead of Gujarat 2.91 and Tamil Nadu 2.14; of every Rs 100 of output Gujarat keeps about 11, Maharashtra about 15. Crown 3 to Maharashtra. 6. Three crowns on screen, one per state. 7. Close: 'Which crown matters most? Comment.'
- **Who shares it and why:** A Tamil engineer sends the factory-count crown to Gujarati and Mumbai colleagues, and each of them forwards their own crown back as proof.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: friendly rivalry where everyone wins a crown. A Tamil engineer sends it to a Gujarati colleague and a Mumbai friend; each gets one crown, so each can forward it as proof. Expected comments: 'Gujarat is refineries', 'Maharashtra has services too', 'where is Karnataka?', 'Haryana grew fastest'. Share trigger: the three-colour podium frame with one crown per state. Rewatch: people pause on the output-versus-value-added beat.
- **Why it could flop:** 'Everyone wins a crown' means no verdict, and the outline carries six metrics (factories, workers, fixed capital, output, net value added, exports) in one Short. That is the number-dense, chart-style shape that sits at the 6,200-view median, and generic state-vs-state Shorts have a median of 5,100. The Tamil Nadu place tag is measured on long-form, not Shorts. The fight that feeds comments is removed when every state gets a prize.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Factories 2023-24: Tamil Nadu 40,121; Gujarat 33,311; Maharashtra 26,539; India 260,061 (Tamil Nadu 15.4%). Workers: Tamil Nadu 2,475,675; Gujarat 1,978,830; Maharashtra 1,849,633; India 15,519,957 (Tamil Nadu 16.0%). Fixed capital (Rs lakh): Gujarat 90,327,908; Maharashtra 55,229,136; Tamil Nadu 37,427,225. Gross output (Rs lakh): Gujarat 263,949,936; Maharashtra 221,850,214; Tamil Nadu 154,939,545. Net value added (Rs lakh): Maharashtra 34,145,981; Gujarat 29,099,304; Tamil Nadu 21,395,650. Exports 2024-25 (USD million): Gujarat 116,332 of India 437,417 (26.6%). Conversion to lakh crore and the net-value-added-to-output ratios (Gujarat 11.0%, Tamil Nadu 13.8%, Maharashtra 15.4%) are computed from the cells.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from RBI T116, T122, T117, T126, T127 (2023-24) and T181: factories 40,121 / 33,311 / 26,539 of 260,061 (Tamil Nadu 15.4%), workers 2,475,675 of 15,519,957 (16.0%), Gujarat fixed capital Rs 9.03 and output Rs 26.39 lakh crore (both highest), net value added Maharashtra 3.41, Gujarat 2.91, Tamil Nadu 2.14, output-keep ratios 11.0% / 13.8% / 15.4%, Gujarat exports 116,332 of 437,417 = 26.6%, all matching. Every crown margin is at least 2.4 pp of the India row (smallest is Maharashtra's net-value-added lead), and state rows sum to only 90% of India's factories, so quote shares against the India row as the idea does.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T116 (factories), T122 (workers), T117 (fixed capital), T126 (value of gross output), T127 (net value added), all 2023-24 (Annual Survey of Industries via RBI Handbook); T181 (state exports 2024-25, USD millions) for the export share. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Tamil Nadu is the strongest place tag in the long-form set (75% of 16 Tamil Nadu place videos reached 3x their channel median; Indian Insight 'How Tamil Nadu SECRETLY Became WORLD's FACTORY' got 307K, 9.9x its median), and rival-state pairs are what people type (Tamil Nadu vs Gujarat/Maharashtra in the search suggestions). Honest caution: plain state-vs-state Shorts have a median of only 5,100 views (70 Shorts), so the 'three different answers' twist has to carry it, not the duel.
- **Risks:** The Annual Survey of Industries covers registered factories only, not small units or services. Gujarat's low net-value-added share reflects what it makes (explain as input-heavy output, do not speculate on products without a source). Tamil Nadu and Gujarat are only 0.3% apart on total emoluments, so do not use that table. Say 'the most', never 'best'. Check that 'workers' versus 'persons engaged' labels match the table notes. Make the Short readable with captions alone, since viral Indian map Shorts are mostly Hindi/Hinglish and English narration is an unproven reach risk.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. Six metrics and 'everyone wins a crown' give no verdict and no single shareable fact, which is the number-dense chart-Short family with a 6,200 median.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. Every crown recomputes from ASI and export tables, but 'Gujarat keeps Rs 11 of every Rs 100' is wrong wording (net value added is not what anyone keeps; say 'adds'), and the screen must say registered factories only and goods exports.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. Six metrics and three crowns for Tamil Nadu, Gujarat and Maharashtra is a number-dense scoreboard with no verdict, and nothing in it is about UP, Punjab or Assam.

### S031. Punjab Isn't in India's Top 3 for Rice. Telangana Is.

- **Original title:** Which of These 4 States Is Not in India's Top 3 for Rice?
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.5 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 6, fit 7, risk low)
- **Series:** Not in the Top 3
- **Template:** new: Odd One Out beat (a variant of the clues beat: four state cards, 3-2-1, reveal the one that is not in the top three)
- **Hook:** Punjab, Uttar Pradesh, Telangana, West Bengal. One is NOT a top-3 rice state.
- **Outline:** 1) Hook: four cards, 3-2-1 pause. 2) Reveal: Punjab is fourth (14,360.9 thousand tonnes), behind Uttar Pradesh 20,758.7, Telangana 17,445.7 and West Bengal 16,019.4 (2024-25). 3) Fairness beat: Punjab has the highest rice yield, 4,428 kg a hectare against India's 2,929, so volume and yield tell different stories. 4) Round 2, wheat: Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh, Punjab, Haryana; Haryana is the odd one (11,399.9), and Madhya Pradesh (24,509.1) is second ahead of Punjab (17,993.2). 5) Round 3, milk: Uttar Pradesh, Rajasthan, Madhya Pradesh, Gujarat; Gujarat is fourth (18,312, 2023-24) behind Madhya Pradesh (21,326). 6) Score and the question 'Which famous state should we check next? Comment it'.
- **Who shares it and why:** Punjabis share it with 'but we lead on yield' and friends from Telangana, West Bengal and Uttar Pradesh tease them back, and Gujaratis do the same with the milk round.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Identity defence. A Punjabi viewer shares it to say 'but we lead on yield', a Gujarati viewer defends milk, and friends from the other states tease them; the quick replies are the comments ('Punjab is still the rice bowl', 'check yield'). The share line is the four-card frame with the fourth state revealed at rank 4 with its number. The fairness beat (Punjab's yield lead) keeps it from reading as an attack.
- **Why it could flop:** It is still a 'top states for a crop' GK list, the saturated lane, wrapped in a quiz format that has no Indian precedent (quiz Shorts median 63 views). The strongest surprises, Punjab missing from the top three and Telangana second, are given away by the title or the first two seconds, and three rounds (rice, wheat, milk) make it long for a Short. Round 3 repeats S025's milk fact, so running both wastes one. Punjab's 4th place is only in the table if no other state interleaves (check Andhra Pradesh, Odisha, Chhattisgarh, Bihar against T058), and the yield fairness beat softens the very claim that makes the hook. If the viewer already guessed Punjab, the Short has nothing left.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Rice 2024-25 (thousand tonnes): India 150,184.3; Uttar Pradesh 20,758.7; Telangana 17,445.7; West Bengal 16,019.4; Punjab 14,360.9. Rice yield: Punjab 4,428 kg a hectare, India 2,929. Wheat 2024-25: India 117,944.5; Uttar Pradesh 35,652.9; Madhya Pradesh 24,509.1; Punjab 17,993.2; Haryana 11,399.9. Milk 2023-24: India 239,299; Uttar Pradesh 38,780; Rajasthan 34,733; Madhya Pradesh 21,326; Gujarat 18,312.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from RBI T058, T059, T091 and T080: rice Uttar Pradesh 20,758.7, Telangana 17,445.7, West Bengal 16,019.4, Punjab 14,360.9 (fourth); Punjab yield 4,428 vs India 2,929, the highest (Andhra Pradesh next at 3,928); wheat 35,652.9 / 24,509.1 / 17,993.2 / 11,399.9; milk Madhya Pradesh 21,326 vs Gujarat 18,312, all matching. The third-vs-fourth gaps are 1.10 pp (rice) and 1.26 pp (milk) of India, but Haryana is fourth in wheat by only 0.11 pp over Rajasthan and Gujarat fourth in milk by 0.95 pp over Maharashtra, so say 'outside the top three' and never 'fourth' for those two.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States T058 (rice production), T059 (wheat), T091 (milk), T080 (rice yield); original sources Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers' Welfare and Basic Animal Husbandry Statistics (as cited in the tables). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** It turns a myth-buster into a quiz the viewer can fail. Curiosity-gap hooks are 21.4% at 1M+ against a 12.4% baseline, 'Kashmir Is Smaller Than You Think' hit 7.4M, and identity hooks about a viewer's own state travel (Ankit Map 'Which state is your state famous for' 3.5M; Georvix 'Most loyal states' 5.7M). Plain rankings (largest, richest, population) are saturated with 79%-91% of search-hit Shorts under 1,000 views, so the angle has to be the belief that breaks, not the list.
- **Risks:** Totals favour big states, so never call anyone 'best' or 'worst'. Periods differ (rice and wheat 2024-25, milk 2023-24); label them. Do not use the 2024 tourism table (T013): its 2024 figures are estimates (RBI note). Keep to what the table says and drop brand claims (such as cooperatives) that are not in the data. State figures may not add to the India total (RBI note). Quiz format is unproven in India, so it needs a small test.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. A quiz format with no Indian precedent wrapped around a top-states-for-a-crop GK list, which is the saturated lane.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. The fourth-place reveal for Punjab (14,360.9 vs West Bengal 16,019.4) recomputes and the yield fairness beat is correct, but the boundary is only 1.1 points of India's rice and the 2024-25 stage is unconfirmed, so label stage and period on screen.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. It is a crop league table in quiz clothing, and its opening reveal is that Punjab is not in the top three for rice, which stings before the yield rescue arrives.

### SG08. India Without Uttar Pradesh: Nearly Half Its Sugarcane Disappears

- **Status:** Shortlisted, survived the red team. **Score** 6.5 (virality 6, appeal 6, distinct 7, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** India Without [State]
- **Template:** Breakdown (100-square grid)
- **Hook:** Take one state out of India's farm output, and nearly half the sugarcane vanishes.
- **Outline:** Beat 1 (0-3s) Hook: 100 squares = all of India's sugarcane (454,611 thousand tonnes, 2024-25); Uttar Pradesh's squares turn grey. Beat 2 The counter falls: 48.6 squares gone, 220,801 thousand tonnes. Beat 3 A fresh 100 squares: cold-storage capacity, Uttar Pradesh holds 38.9% (2022). Beat 4 Total foodgrains 17.8% (63,848 of 357,732 thousand tonnes, 2024-25) and milk 16.2% (38,780 of 239,299 thousand tonnes, 2023-24). Beat 5 Friendly-rivalry beat: 'Maharashtra is second in sugarcane at 24.2%; together the two grow 72.8%.' Beat 6 Close: 'Pick a state to subtract next.' MAP RULE: the state is greyed inside the whole official outline, never cut out. SLATE (occasional, one state and one table per episode): West Bengal (79.8% of jute and mesta), Rajasthan (47.5% of wool), Andhra Pradesh (28.0% of fish), Tamil Nadu (16.0% of factory workers).
- **Who shares it and why:** Uttar Pradesh viewers forward 'UP grows nearly half of India's sugarcane' with pride, and Maharashtra and Karnataka viewers answer 'we are second' in the comments.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** What-if plus state pride and friendly rivalry. Shared by Uttar Pradesh viewers ('UP feeds India'), answered by Maharashtra and Karnataka viewers ('we are second'), and used by teachers and students as a quick fact. Likely comments: 'do Maharashtra / Punjab / my state', 'per person?', 'wheat too?'. Share trigger: the squares greying out one by one while the counter drops from 100 to 51.
- **Why it could flop:** What-if titles as a group sit below baseline (11.1% at 1M+ vs 12.4%); the breakouts were Tamil Nadu identity stories, and flat what-ifs sit at 15K to 41K (Data Duck). 'UP grows nearly half the sugarcane' is already common knowledge, and a share of volume mostly reflects UP's size, so 'per person?' will be the top comment. Subtracting a state sits close to secession and division-of-UP politics, which the hard rules treat carefully, and the main audience for UP content is Hindi-first while the narration here is English. The Breakdown grid is chart-like, so the greying-out animation has to carry everything.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Sugarcane production 2024-25 (T065): Uttar Pradesh 220,801 of India 454,611 thousand tonnes = 48.6%; Maharashtra 24.2%; Karnataka 10.6%. Cold-storage capacity 2022 (T098): 14,875,987 of 38,224,006 tonnes = 38.9%. Total foodgrains 2024-25 (T062): 63,848 of 357,732 thousand tonnes = 17.8%. Milk 2023-24 (T091): 38,780 of 239,299 thousand tonnes = 16.2%. Slate: West Bengal 79.8% of jute and mesta (T066, 2024-25); Rajasthan 47.5% of wool (T094, 2023-24); Andhra Pradesh 28.0% of fish (T093, 2023-24); Tamil Nadu 16.0% of factory workers (T122, 2023-24).
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Shares recomputed against each table's own India row: T065 Uttar Pradesh 220,801.3/454,611 = 48.57%, Maharashtra 24.2%, Karnataka 10.6%, the two together 72.76%; T098 38.92%; T062 17.85%; T091 16.21%; slate T066 79.84%, T094 47.53%, T093 28.03%, T122 15.95%, with state rows summing to 1.00 of the India row in the first four tables. Cold storage is capacity (a 30 Nov 2022 snapshot), not farm output, so the second grid needs its own label; every rank lead is at least 1.7 pp (Uttar Pradesh over Rajasthan in milk).
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook T065 (sugarcane), T098 (cold storage), T062 (foodgrains), T091 (milk); shares computed from each table's own India row (state rows sum to the India row, ratio 1.00). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 5 (what-if, subtract-a-state). What-if is one of two measured breakout hooks: Map Motion's 'What If Tamil Nadu Never Existed' reached 12M and its 'independent country' version 8.4M, and the list has zero subtract-a-state ideas. Execution decides, since Data Duck's flat what-ifs sit at 15K to 41K, so this one is a visual (100 squares going grey) with a surprising near-half number, not a flat comparison. All-India shares were not computed before; they are computed here from the tables' own India rows.
- **Risks:** Frame as contribution, not secession: say 'adds' and 'grows', never 'independent' or 'leaves'. Grey the state inside the whole official outline; never cut it out of the map. Shares are volumes, not per person; population is the likely reason a big state leads, and population share is not in our files, so make no 'above its weight' claim. Uttar Pradesh is politically charged: no leaders, parties or elections on screen, and moderate comments. Cold-storage data are from 2022. Keep the series occasional with a different state and table each time or it reads as a template.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. Only the sugarcane beat surprises; the foodgrain and milk shares are roughly what a big state's size gives (the idea concedes population), and flat what-ifs sit at 15K to 41K while the breakouts were Tamil Nadu identity stories.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. All shares recompute against each table's own India row (48.6%, 38.9%, 17.8%, 16.2%); retitle away from 'India Without' and 'disappears' toward 'Uttar Pradesh grows 48.6% of India's sugarcane', since the title contradicts the idea's own 'contribution, not secession' rule.
- **Red team (viewer):** keep. A UP viewer gets pride and a forward-to-friends line ('UP grows nearly half of India's sugarcane'), the squares going grey is a real visual, and nobody is mocked; keep only sugarcane and cold storage, because foodgrains 17.8% and milk 16.2% look close to UP's share of India's people (my recollection, unchecked) and will read as 'of course', and consider a title that does not invite 'India without UP' jokes.

### S005. Telangana vs Andhra Pradesh: 12 Years After the Split, Who Leads?

- **Original title:** Telangana vs Andhra Pradesh, 12 Years After Bifurcation: 5 Rounds
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.3 (virality 6, appeal 6, distinct 5, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** Five Rounds
- **Template:** state vs state over five rounds
- **Hook:** In 2014 one state became two. Twelve years on, who leads on what?
- **Outline:** 1. Hook with the birthday card: 2 June 2014, one state becomes two (date to be confirmed in the Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014). 2. Round 1, rice 2024-25: Telangana 17.45 million tonnes against Andhra Pradesh 8.16 million; Telangana's rice area is up 239% since 2012-13. Point to Telangana. 3. Round 2, fish: Andhra Pradesh 51.58 lakh tonnes against Telangana 4.56; Andhra is 28% of India's fish. Point to Andhra. 4. Round 3, income per person 2024-25: Telangana Rs 3,87,623, Andhra Rs 2,66,240. Telangana. 5. Round 4, renewable power capacity (2024): Andhra 9,419 MW, Telangana 5,199 MW. Andhra. 6. Round 5, photo finish on the size of the economy: Telangana Rs 16.41 lakh crore, Andhra Rs 15.93 lakh crore, 3.0% apart. 7. Score 3-2 and the question: 'Which round would you add?'
- **Who shares it and why:** Telugu cousins in Hyderabad and Vijayawada forward the 3-2 scoreboard to each other to claim their state's rounds and to suggest a sixth round.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: friendly rivalry plus how a place came to be. Telugu families are split across both states, so it lands in family WhatsApp groups between cousins in Hyderabad and Vijayawada. Expected comments: 'add Hyderabad's IT', 'Andhra has the coast', 'Telangana has the rice' and a flood of 'round 6' suggestions. Share trigger: the 3-2 scoreboard freeze with 'which round would you add?'. Rewatch: viewers replay to check each round.
- **Why it could flop:** The five rounds (rice, fish, income, renewables, GSDP) are hand-picked, so the winner depends on the picks. Comments will say 'you left out Hyderabad's IT', which the neutrality rule stops us from engaging. The 'photo finish' round rests on a 3% GSDP gap. Generic state-vs-state Shorts have a median of 5,100, and the Angel One 494K evidence is a 16-minute presenter video, not a Short. Telugu viewers may also prefer Telugu narration. The bifurcation is emotionally charged, and pre-2014 Telangana figures are back-cast.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Rice 2024-25 (thousand tonnes): Telangana 17,445.7; Andhra Pradesh 8,159.1; India 150,184.3. Telangana rice area 1,418.4 to 4,809.5 thousand ha (2012-13 to 2024-25, +239%). Fish 2023-24 (P), lakh tonnes: Andhra Pradesh 51.58; Telangana 4.56; India 184.02. Income per person 2024-25: Telangana Rs 387,623; Andhra Pradesh Rs 266,240. Renewable capacity end-March 2024 (MW): Andhra Pradesh 9,419; Telangana 5,199. GSDP 2024-25 current prices (Rs lakh): Telangana 164,090,143; Andhra Pradesh 159,306,198. Extra rounds if needed: eggs 2023-24 Andhra 254,874 vs Telangana 183,850 (lakh numbers); power per person 2024-25 Telangana 2,306.1 vs Andhra 1,481.6 kWh.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from RBI T058, T067, T093, T019, T143, T021, T092 and T138: rice 17,445.7 vs 8,159.1; area +239.1%; fish 51.58 vs 4.56 lakh tonnes; income Rs 387,623 vs 266,240; renewables 9,419 vs 5,199 MW (grid-interactive, so label it as excluding large hydro); GSDP 164,090,143 vs 159,306,198 lakh (Telangana +3.0%); eggs 254,874 vs 183,850; power per person 2,306.1 vs 1,481.6 kWh, all matching and giving the 3-2 scoreboard.
