Round 5 is only a 3.0% margin so call it a photo finish, the rice-area base 2012-13 predates statehood (2014-15 gives +240%), and the 2 June 2014 date still has to be read from the Act or notification, which is not in SOURCE_MAP.md.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook: T058 (rice production), T067 (rice area), T093 (fish), T019 (income per person), T143 (renewable capacity), T021 (GSDP). Statehood date: Andhra Pradesh Reorganisation Act, 2014 (India Code), to be read. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Angel One's 'Telangana vs Andhra Pradesh: Who is Winning?' reached 494K, 61x its channel median, and verdict-style place titles hit 3x+ in 43.5% of cases; origin and formation Shorts are an open lane (15 matching Shorts, median 383K, 67% at 100K+). Honest caution: generic state-vs-state Shorts have a median of 5,100, so the birthday opening and the 'add your round' ending carry the load.
- **Risks:** The split is emotionally charged: neutral wording, no mention of the capital dispute, parties or who gained, and the date comes from the Act. RBI pre-2014 Telangana figures are back-cast to present boundaries, so say 'present boundaries' on the first use. The choice of rounds drives the score, so disclose that on screen and invite other picks. The GSDP lead is 3.0%, so call it a photo finish, not a win. Provisional 2023-24 fish data (marked P).
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. Five hand-picked rounds invite 'you left out Hyderabad' while the neutrality rule bars answering the capital argument, and generic state-vs-state Shorts have a median of 5,100.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. The 3-2 scoreboard in a bitter state split is decided by five hand-picked rounds and the final GSDP round is a 3.0% gap on a 2024-25 figure, which fails the project's own thin-lead rule, so the 'winner' is an artefact of the picks.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. A Telangana vs Andhra scoreboard is a family-WhatsApp story for Telugu people only, built on hand-picked rounds and a still-charged split.

### S022. Meghalaya 71.8, Delhi 18.5: Where Women Are in the Labour Force

- **Original title:** Where Do Most Women Work? Not Where You'd Guess
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.1 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 6, fit 9, risk medium)
- **Series:** The Map Flips
- **Template:** existing: Myth-buster (the usual guess, then the league table), shown with Beat-style one-state-at-a-time reveals. Later episodes use other flips, for example a state that leads on one list and trails on another.
- **Hook:** In Meghalaya, 71.8 of every 100 women work or look for work. In Delhi, 18.5.
- **Outline:** 30-35 s. 1) Hook: two numbers, two outlines. 2) The usual guess: 'Kerala? Goa? Delhi?' 3) Answer: Meghalaya 71.8, Sikkim 68.2, Himachal Pradesh 67.6, Arunachal Pradesh 66.5, Nagaland 64.3 (2023-24); India 41.7. 4) The other end: Delhi 18.5, Lakshadweep 17.1, Haryana 24.2, Goa 29.3. 5) The jump since 2017-18: Arunachal Pradesh 14.7 to 66.5, Nagaland 16.7 to 64.3, India 23.3 to 41.7. 6) Caveat on screen: 'Work or look for work, age 15+, includes unpaid family work.' 7) Close: 'Where does your state sit?'
- **Who shares it and why:** Northeast and Himachal students and diaspora in Delhi and Bengaluru share it as 'the map is not what you think', and gender-and-work readers share the Delhi number.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: surprise plus regional pride. Northeast and Himachal students and diaspora in Delhi and Bengaluru share it as 'the map does not look like you think'; gender-and-work readers share the Delhi number. Expect 'because of ...' guesses, 'what counts as work' and 'why is Delhi so low'. Trigger frame: Meghalaya 71.8 beside Delhi 18.5, both outlines on screen.
- **Why it could flop:** The surprise deflates under its own caveat (the figure counts unpaid family work and people looking for work, not paid jobs). The hook uses the dramatic 2023-24 extremes while a newer calendar-2025 release exists in our own file and shows Meghalaya at 64.3 (not 71.8), Delhi at 13.1 (not 18.5) and Sikkim first, so the ranks are unstable and the hook can look cherry-picked or stale; small-state survey noise may explain swings of 5 to 7 points.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Female labour force participation 2023-24: Meghalaya 71.8, Sikkim 68.2, Himachal Pradesh 67.6, Arunachal Pradesh 66.5, Nagaland 64.3, India 41.7; Delhi 18.5, Lakshadweep 17.1, Haryana 24.2, Goa 29.3; men: Meghalaya 82.3, Delhi 70.1. 2017-18: Arunachal Pradesh 14.7, Nagaland 16.7, Assam 12.7, India 23.3. Calendar 2025: Sikkim 66.7, Himachal Pradesh 64.7, Nagaland 64.5, Meghalaya 64.3, Delhi 13.1.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from plfs_annual.json (LFPR, female, rural+urban): 2023-24 Meghalaya 71.8, Sikkim 68.2, Himachal Pradesh 67.6, Arunachal Pradesh 66.5, Nagaland 64.3, India 41.7; Delhi 18.5, Lakshadweep 17.1, Haryana 24.2, Goa 29.3; 2017-18 Arunachal 14.7, Nagaland 16.7, India 23.3; men Meghalaya 82.3, Delhi 70.1, and 2025 ranks (Sikkim first, Meghalaya fourth) all match. Both endpoints of the 2017-18 to 2023-24 jump are July-June survey years; Delhi is not the lowest in 2023-24 (Lakshadweep 17.1 is), so never call it the lowest, and keep 'work or look for work' in the title framing.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (MoSPI PLFS, labour force participation rate, female, rural plus urban, usual status, 2017-18, 2023-24 and 2025). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** A surprise that overturns a belief with a state map is the pattern behind the state-data paradox hit (4.6M). Jobs and wages are a large search theme (302 suggestions). Plain state lists are saturated (79 to 91% of unemployment and salary list Shorts are under 1,000 views), so the flip, not the list, is the hook. Regional identity content for the Northeast is thinly supplied in English (inference from the benchmark, not measured).
- **Risks:** Definition: participation counts people working or seeking work, including unpaid family helpers, so never say 'women earn' or 'employed'. Ranks move by year (calendar 2025 puts Sikkim first and Meghalaya fourth), so quote the five-state group and do not claim a single first (Meghalaya leads Sikkim by 3.6 points in 2023-24). The jump since 2017-18 may partly reflect survey changes: check MoSPI's note before scripting. Do not explain by community, tribe, religion or caste: the comments will, the script must not. Showing Delhi as the low end can read as mocking: present it as one end of a range. Small-sample UTs (Lakshadweep) need a footnote.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. Delhi at 18.5 is a real surprise, but the unavoidable 'includes unpaid family work' caveat deflates it and S004 and S039 reuse the same dataset; hold it as the one women-and-work swap-in for 8 March 2027.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. 'Where do most women work?' is wrong because the measure counts job-seekers and unpaid family work and India's own figure is 41.7%, the 2017-18 to 2023-24 jumps in tiny Northeast samples carry unknown sampling error, and ranks flip by year.
- **Red team (viewer):** keep. Meghalaya 71.8 beside Delhi 18.5 is a belief-flipper that Northeast and hill-state viewers will send with pride and nobody's state is the punchline, but keep only the two-number flip plus one short caveat line and drop the Haryana, Goa and Lakshadweep detail.

### S015. Goa Was Not Part of India in August 1947. Here's How It Joined

- **Original title:** How Goa Became Part of India
- **Status:** Shortlisted, survived the red team. **Score** 6 (virality 7, appeal 7, distinct 5, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Became India
- **Template:** new: Place Story. Official-boundary map, one dated event per beat on a timeline ribbon, zoom to the place, one 'today' number, closing question. Config = place, dated events (date, Act or notification, one neutral line), today-number (table, value, period). The same engine runs Puducherry, Dadra and Nagar Haveli, Lakshadweep and later Sikkim, once a neutral script is approved.
- **Hook:** On 15 August 1947, India became independent. Goa was still not part of it.
- **Outline:** 35-45 s. 1) Hook: official map of India, Goa greyed out, stamp '15 Aug 1947'. 2) Who held Goa and since when (Portuguese rule; start year to verify). 3) End of Portuguese rule and entry into the Indian Union (December 1961 from memory; verify the date and the legal instrument), captioned with the Government of India notification, no weapons or flags imagery. 4) Goa, Daman and Diu as a Union Territory, then statehood under the Goa, Daman and Diu Reorganisation Act, 1987 (verify date and ordinal). 5) Today beat: Goa has the most power available per person of any state, 3,417.6 kWh against India's 1,208.2. 6) Close: 'Which place joined India last? Comment your state's year. Next: Puducherry.'
- **Who shares it and why:** Goans and the Goan diaspora forward it into family and alumni groups as a 'did you know', history and exam students pass it on, and viewers from the next state in the series comment 'do mine next'.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: 'how a place came to be' plus state pride. Goans and the Goan diaspora send it into family and alumni groups ('did you know Goa joined in 1961'); history and exam students share it; people from the next state in the queue comment 'do mine next'. Expect comments about Goa's liberation day, the 1987 statehood, 'what about Daman and Diu' and 'Sikkim next'. Trigger frame: Goa greyed out on the 15 Aug 1947 map, then filling in on the date it joined.
- **Why it could flop:** Goa is the most-told place story in the lane (Logical Bharat's Hindi version has 12M), and our version must drop the military drama, footage and flags that carried it, so an English, faceless, neutral-wording cut risks reading as a dry dated list. The power-per-person beat is bolted on to a history story and splits the one punch in 35-45 seconds, and no date or legal instrument is in our files yet (Acts not read).
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Goa power available per person 3,417.6 kWh vs Punjab 2,503.4 and India 1,208.2 (T138, 2024-25). Goa unemployment rate 8.3% vs India 3.1% (PLFS 2025). No date is in our files: every date must come from the Act text.
- **Data editor:** source exists, numbers not re-checked. The today beat checks out: RBI T138 has Goa at 3,417.6 kWh, highest of the 34 rows (36% above Punjab; the table has no Dadra or Daman row) against India 1,208.2, and plfs_annual.json has Goa unemployment 8.3% vs India 3.1% for 2025 (second highest after Andaman and Nicobar at 10.0). The Portuguese-rule start, the December 1961 date, the legal instrument and the 1987 statehood date and ordinal are in no local file, and India Code and the Gazette are not in SOURCE_MAP.md, so the origin story itself rests on memory until the Acts are read.
- **Source:** Primary: the Acts and Government of India notifications (India Code, Gazette of India). Neither is listed in docs/datakadai/SOURCE_MAP.md, so add both before scripting. Today beat: public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T138.json (RBI Handbook table 138; name the original agency from the table note) and public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json. (answer PDF still to read)
- **Evidence it could work:** 'How a place became part of India' is the most open proven lane in the Shorts research: 15 matching Shorts on 9 channels, median 383K, 67% at 100K+, 5 at 1M+ (Logical Bharat 'How did Goa come to India' 12M; Yash Rajput 'How Jodhpur Became Part of India' 7.6M). The tag 'state formation, history, merger or what-if-state' reached 1M+ in 26.7% of 60 Shorts against a 12.4% baseline. Reach is not gated by size: Yash Rajput Map (48K subscribers) has 13 of 21 border-history Shorts at 1M+.
- **Risks:** Violence and politics rule: the 1961 events involved a military operation, so use dated neutral wording ('Portuguese rule ended', 'joined the Indian Union'), cite the instrument, show no weapons. Start only with non-communal cases (Goa, Puducherry, Dadra and Nagar Haveli); keep Hyderabad, Junagadh, Kashmir and Sikkim 1975 until a neutral script is approved. Official boundary only. All dates are unverified until read from the Act. Language risk: 93 of the top 125 viral state Shorts are Hindi, so ship a text-only cut beside the English-voice cut of the same story (Bharat Mapping reached 5.8M to 11M with text on the map). SOURCE_MAP has no legal-source entry.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** keep. 'Goa was not part of India on 15 August 1947' is a belief-overturn that works without the military drama, in a lane where 67% reach 100K, but Logical Bharat's 12M Hindi version sets the bar, so run it as the second origin test and cut the power-per-person beat.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. The dates, legal instrument and statehood ordinal are all unverified from memory, 'joined the Indian Union' glosses over a contested 1961 military operation (the very violence rule), and the power-per-person 'today' beat implies a causal link to joining India.
- **Red team (viewer):** keep. 'India was independent in 1947 and Goa was not part of it' is a universal curiosity gap about a state every Indian knows, it needs no stake in any region, and the neutral dated-timeline treatment is fair; cut the power-per-person beat, which is a non-sequitur, and verify every date from the Act.

### S001. Punjab Is Only 3rd in Wheat. The Top State Grows Nearly 2x as Much

- **Original title:** India's Top Wheat State Isn't Punjab. It Grows Nearly Twice as Much
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 6 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 4, fit 9, risk low)
- **Series:** Not Who You Think
- **Template:** Myth-buster
- **Hook:** Which state grows the most wheat? Not Punjab.
- **Outline:** 1. Plain India map, Punjab glows (the usual answer), caption: 'No. 1 wheat state?' 2. Wipe: the glow jumps to Uttar Pradesh, 35.65 million tonnes, about 30% of India's wheat. 3. Punjab drops to third, 17.99 million tonnes (15%), behind Madhya Pradesh at 24.51 million. 4. Scale made literal: UP grows almost twice Punjab's wheat on 9.60 million hectares against Punjab's 3.51 million. 5. Fairness twist so Punjab fans share instead of fight: yield per hectare, Punjab 5,123 kg is the highest in the table, India 3,595. 6. Close: 'Which state did you guess? Comment it.' plus a one-second tease of episode 2 (Amul's state is fourth in milk).
- **Who shares it and why:** A UP or Madhya Pradesh friend forwards it to a Punjabi friend to say Punjab is only third, and the Punjabi answers with the highest-yield frame.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: a surprise that overturns a belief, plus friendly rivalry. A friend from UP or MP sends it to a Punjabi friend ('you are third'); the Punjabi answers with the yield frame ('we get the most per hectare'), so both sides have a line to post and both are right. Expected comments: 'Punjab feeds India', 'UP has more land', 'what about Haryana?'. Share trigger: the frame where Punjab's glow slides to third place. Rewatch comes from the yield twist.
- **Why it could flop:** The myth is a straw man: UP's lead is mostly a size effect and 'UP is the top wheat state' is a standard GK answer, so the reveal gets a shrug, not a share. It also sits in the saturated 'top producing states' lane (79-91% of such Shorts under 1,000 views). The series is stronger than this episode: the milk reveal (Gujarat 4th) and the sugarcane reveal (UP 220.8 vs Maharashtra 110.0 million tonnes) overturn bigger beliefs, so lead with one of those. Confirm that 2024-25 wheat is not an advance estimate before voicing.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Wheat production 2024-25 (thousand tonnes): Uttar Pradesh 35,652.9; Madhya Pradesh 24,509.1; Punjab 17,993.2; India 117,944.5. Area (thousand ha): UP 9,598.8; Punjab 3,512.0. Yield (kg/ha): Punjab 5,123; Haryana 4,774; UP 3,714; India 3,595. Shares and ratios (UP 30.2% of India, Punjab 15.3%, UP/Punjab 1.98x) are computed from these cells. Episode 2, milk 2023-24 (thousand tonnes): UP 38,780; Rajasthan 34,733; Madhya Pradesh 21,326; Gujarat 18,312 (4th); India 239,299. Episode 3, sugarcane 2024-25 (thousand tonnes): UP 220,801.3 against Maharashtra 109,970.2 and Karnataka 48,060.0 (UP is 48.6% of India's 454,611).
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from RBI T059, T068, T081 (2024-25): UP 35,652.9, MP 24,509.1, Punjab 17,993.2 (3rd), India 117,944.5; UP 30.2%, Punjab 15.3%, UP/Punjab 1.98x, UP lead over MP 45.5%; area 9,598.8 vs 3,512.0; yield Punjab 5,123 highest in table, Haryana 4,774, UP 3,714, India 3,595. Milk (T091) and sugarcane (T065) teaser numbers also match (Gujarat 4th, UP 48.6% of 454,611); the T059 notes name the Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers' Welfare but give no advance-versus-final stage for 2024-25, so confirm that before voicing.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T059 (wheat production 2024-25), T068 (wheat area), T081 (wheat yield); the India row in each table gives the national total. Follow-up episodes: T091 (milk, 2023-24), T065 (sugarcane, 2024-25), T058 (rice). Underlying publishers: Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers Welfare and Dept of Animal Husbandry, via RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Curiosity-gap hooks reached 1M+ views in 21.4% of 103 India-titled Shorts against a 12.4% baseline (Shorts research, finding 8), and a fixed state-by-state series has a high floor (Geo Study state series: 24 Shorts, median 982K, minimum 212K). The bare topic 'top producing states' is the saturated GK-list lane (79-91% of such Shorts under 1,000 views), so the belief-flip, not the list, has to carry it.
- **Risks:** 2024-25 foodgrain figures may be advance or provisional estimates; confirm the stage in the RBI table note before voicing. Absolute output favours big states, so the yield beat is mandatory for fairness. UP's lead over MP is 45%, so no thin-lead problem. Say only what is counted ('grows more wheat'), never that a state 'feeds' more. If every episode is a 'not who you think' flip the series looks like a template factory, so vary the hook and the first frame.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. UP leading wheat is a standard GK answer, so the myth is a straw man and the reveal is a size effect in the saturated top-producing-states lane.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. Uttar Pradesh 35,652.9 vs Punjab 17,993.2 thousand t (1.98x) recomputes with a 45% lead over the runner-up, no causal claim, and the yield beat is fair; only confirm the 2024-25 estimate stage from the RBI note.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. UP being the top wheat state is mostly a size effect and a shrug for UP viewers, while Punjab is demoted to third and has to be rescued by a yield twist.

### S025. Gujarat Is Only 4th in Milk. India's No. 2 Is Rajasthan

- **Original title:** India's Milk Capital Isn't Gujarat: Top 5 States by Output
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 6 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 4, fit 9, risk low)
- **Series:** Made Where?
- **Template:** existing: Myth-buster (the usual guess, then the league table). The builder refuses the 'myth' if the guessed state is in the top three; Gujarat is fourth, so it passes. Later episodes: eggs, fish, sugarcane, wheat, cotton.
- **Hook:** Which state produces the most milk in India? Not Gujarat.
- **Outline:** 25-30 s. 1) Hook. 2) The usual guess: Gujarat, Punjab, Haryana. 3) Reveal the top five, 2023-24, million tonnes: Uttar Pradesh 38.8 (16.2% of India), Rajasthan 34.7 (14.5%), Madhya Pradesh 21.3 (8.9%), Gujarat 18.3 (7.7%), Maharashtra 16.0 (6.7%). 4) On-screen note: 'Total output, not per person.' 5) Teaser: 'Next: where India's eggs come from' (Andhra Pradesh, Tamil Nadu and Telangana together 46%). 6) Close: 'What should we map next? Rice, sugar, eggs?'
- **Who shares it and why:** Gujarat and Amul loyalists share it to argue back, and exam aspirants share it as a GK fact, but nobody has a strong reason to tag a specific friend.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: belief overturn plus state rivalry. Shared in farm, dairy and exam groups, and by Gujarat, Uttar Pradesh and Rajasthan commenters arguing it out. Expect 'but per person Punjab', 'cows or buffaloes', 'what about Amul'. Trigger line: 'Gujarat is fourth.'
- **Why it could flop:** It is a top-5 league table of 'which state produces most X', the lane where 79-91% of search-hit Shorts sit under 1,000 views, and English-narrated chart-style Shorts are the weakest family measured (6,200 median against 73,000 for story-led maps). 'UP is first in milk' is already standard exam GK, so the only real overturn is Gujarat, which is a mild one. The stronger hook is buried: Rajasthan being second (34.7 vs Gujarat 18.3) is the actual 'wait, what'. Gujarat is 7.7% against Maharashtra's 6.7%, a lead of about 1 point, so keep the claim to 'fourth' and never rank 4th against 5th as a story. The 'totals not per person' note will draw the 'but per capita' comments without helping reach.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Milk 2023-24 (thousand tonnes): Uttar Pradesh 38,780; Rajasthan 34,733; Madhya Pradesh 21,326; Gujarat 18,312; Maharashtra 16,045; Punjab 14,000; India 239,299. Eggs 2023-24 (lakh numbers): Andhra Pradesh 254,874; Tamil Nadu 223,325; Telangana 183,850; West Bengal 162,394; India 1,427,716 (the first three are 46.4%).
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from RBI T091 (2023-24): UP 38,780 (16.2%), Rajasthan 34,733 (14.5%), MP 21,326 (8.9%), Gujarat 18,312 (7.65%, 4th), Maharashtra 16,045 (6.7%), Punjab 14,000, India 239,299; UP leads Rajasthan by 11.7%, so no thin lead, and Gujarat has been 4th since 2019-20. Eggs in T092 also match (AP, TN, Telangana = 46.4% of 1,427,716); the T091 note names Basic Animal Husbandry Statistics, Ministry of Fisheries, Animal Husbandry and Dairying as the original source.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T091.json (RBI Handbook table 91; original agency, Department of Animal Husbandry and Dairying per SOURCE_MAP, confirm from the table note). Eggs: T092.json. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Surprise-overturns-belief hooks drive the state breakouts, and food and culture maps are a proven lane (184 Shorts, 21 at 1M+, 11.4%; EduMap 'Indian States and Their Famous Food' 4.9M). Generic top-state GK lists are saturated, so the hook has to be the overturned belief rather than the list (79 to 91% of such list Shorts are under 1,000 views).
- **Risks:** Totals favour big states: add a per-person beat once population is collected, and do not imply productivity or quality. Gujarat pride: state the fact flatly, no 'myth' tone. Dairy invites cow-politics comments: keep cattle species out of the script. Lead check passed: Uttar Pradesh over Rajasthan is 1.7 points of India's milk. 2023-24 figures are estimates; name the original agency on screen, not only the RBI.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. 'Gujarat is fourth in milk' is a decent flip, but it is still a top-5 league table in the lane where 79 to 91% of Shorts sit under 1,000 views.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. Gujarat 4th (18,312 vs Madhya Pradesh 21,326 thousand t) and the top five recompute, the lead of Uttar Pradesh over Rajasthan is 11.7%, and leaving out cattle species and 'myth' tone avoids the sensitive angles.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. It is a top-five milk league table that ignores Punjab per person, which Punjab viewers will notice, and the 'Gujarat is fourth' surprise is already used as round 1 of S008.

### S004. Himachal: 65 of 100 Women in the Workforce. Next Door, Haryana: 24

- **Original title:** Women in the Workforce: Himachal 65%, Next-Door Haryana 24%
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 5.9 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 6, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** Next Door
- **Template:** Face-off
- **Hook:** Two states share a border. In one, 65 of every 100 women are in the workforce. In the other, 24.
- **Outline:** 1. Hook: Himachal Pradesh and Haryana on the map, one border line. 2. Himachal: women 64.7, men 82.5 (2025). 3. Haryana: women 23.8, men 75.0. 4. The scale beat: men's participation runs only from 67.8 to 85.3 across all 36 states and UTs (a 17.5-point spread); women's runs from 13.1 (Delhi) to 66.7 (Sikkim), a 53.6-point spread. 5. Definition card so nobody misreads it: 'in the workforce' means working or looking for work, including unpaid help in a family farm or business, age 15 and above. 6. Close: the all-states map with 'Where does your state fall? Comment.'
- **Who shares it and why:** A woman from Shimla or the Northeast sends the 65-vs-24 contrast with pride to a cousin in Haryana or Delhi, who replies with how the work is counted.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: identity and pride, with your-state personalisation. A woman in Shimla or Shillong sends it to a cousin in Gurgaon or Chandigarh; Himachal and Northeast viewers share it with pride; Haryana viewers add context in the comments (farm work, how it is counted). Share line: 'Men: a 17-point spread. Women: a 54-point spread.' The closing 'where does your state fall' map invites replies.
- **Why it could flop:** The measure counts unpaid family helpers, so Himachal's 65% is largely farm work, not jobs, and the definition card the Short needs for accuracy drains the hook. Haryana viewers will read it as a dig. Our neutrality rule bars us from explaining why, so the top comments will go unanswered and the thread may turn into a culture fight. Small-state estimates (Sikkim, Lakshadweep) are noisy, and the idea relies on the personalised closing map to carry the comments.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** LFPR 2025 (women / men): Himachal Pradesh 64.7 / 82.5; Punjab 30.2 / 79.2; Haryana 23.8 / 75.0; Delhi 13.1 / 71.5; Sikkim 66.7 / 81.3; Bihar 24.7 / 75.7; India 40.0 / 79.1. Men's range 67.8 (Mizoram) to 85.3 (Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu); women's range 13.1 (Delhi) to 66.7 (Sikkim). 2023-24 (women): Meghalaya 71.8; Himachal Pradesh 67.6; Haryana 24.2; Delhi 18.5; Lakshadweep 17.1; India 41.7. Optional follow-up, Bihar women 4.1 (2017-18), 30.5 (2023-24), 24.7 (2025).
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from plfs_annual.json (2025, rural+urban): Himachal Pradesh 64.7/82.5, Haryana 23.8/75.0, Punjab 30.2/79.2, Delhi 13.1/71.5, Sikkim 66.7/81.3, Bihar 24.7/75.7, India 40.0/79.1; men's range 67.8 (Mizoram) to 85.3 (DNH and DD) = 17.5 points, women's 13.1 to 66.7 = 53.6 points over 36 states/UTs, and the 2023-24 and Bihar 4.1/30.5/24.7 figures match. Say '17.5-point' rather than '17-point' on screen, and note this is the third Short on the same PLFS table (with S022 and S039), so space the releases.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json: MoSPI Periodic Labour Force Survey, LFPR, age 15+, usual status (PS+SS), rural + urban, female and male, years 2025 and 2023-24. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** 'Your state' and paradox hooks are where comment rates run 7-12x the median (0.09-0.15% against 0.013%), and pride maps for the Northeast and hill states travel (Bharat Mapping text-on-map Shorts with no detected speech reached 5.8M and 11M). The comparison of next-door states supplies the paradox without ranking any community.
- **Risks:** Gender topics are sensitive: state numbers only, no explanation of why and no blame on any state or culture. The measure includes unpaid family helpers (confirm in the MoSPI note) and says nothing about pay. State estimates for small places (Sikkim, Lakshadweep) are noisy, and the same state can swing (Bihar 30.5 then 24.7), so label periods exactly: 2025 is a calendar year, 2023-24 is a survey year. Check the 2017-18 Bihar value (4.1) against the MoSPI release before using it, since it looks like a first-round artefact. No thin-lead '#1' claim.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. The Himachal 65 vs Haryana 24 contrast is real, but the unpaid-family-work definition card drains it and Haryana viewers will read it as a jab.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. Himachal's 64.7% is largely unpaid family work, so the 'pride' share line misleads, the Himachal-vs-Haryana pairing invites gender-norm blame on a named state, and the '17 vs 54 point spread' is a min-max range driven by tiny-sample outliers like Sikkim.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. Haryana at 24 is the butt of the contrast, the closing 'where does your state fall' map will expose Punjab and UP at the low end, and the definition card about unpaid family work drains the hook; S022 does this job better.

### S017. Which Indian States Share the 1 November Birthday? Is Yours One?

- **Original title:** Six Indian States Share One Birthday: 1 November
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 5.6 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 6, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Statehood Day
- **Template:** new: Birthday Card. Calendar date on screen, state outlines pop in with their age in years, one official number per state, 'wish your state' close. Config = date, states (Act, year), one table value each. Reusable on every statehood day through the year.
- **Hook:** Six Indian states share one birthday. Is yours one of them?
- **Outline:** 30-40 s. 1) Hook: a calendar page, 1 November. 2) Six outlines pop in with their ages: Kerala, Karnataka, Madhya Pradesh, Andhra Pradesh, Haryana, Chhattisgarh (years to verify from the Acts). 3) One number each, two states per beat: Kerala, 5 infant deaths per 1,000 births (India 25); Andhra Pradesh, 51.58 lakh tonnes of fish, 28% of India's. 4) Madhya Pradesh, 46.6 million tonnes of foodgrains, second only to Uttar Pradesh; Karnataka, 8.6 million tonnes of coarse cereals, second after Rajasthan. 5) Haryana, wheat yield 4,774 kg per hectare against India's 3,595; Chhattisgarh, foodgrain yield up 60% since 2012-13. 6) Close: 'Wish your state in the comments.' Post on 1 November 2026, 21 days after this brief.
- **Who shares it and why:** On the day, people from the six states, especially those living in other cities and countries, forward it in family and college groups as a birthday greeting and tease each other (Kerala vs Karnataka).
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: timeliness plus state pride. On the day, people from each of the six states, especially diaspora in other cities and countries, send it into family and college groups as a birthday greeting. Expect: 'Happy birthday Kerala / Karnataka', Kerala-versus-Karnataka banter, 'you forgot Punjab', 'why is Andhra Pradesh on 1 November' (it also has a 2014 date). Trigger line: 'Six states, one birthday.'
- **Why it could flop:** It is a one-shot that must publish around 30 October to 1 November against thousands of greeting posters, and its body is six unrelated statistics (infant deaths, fish, foodgrains, coarse cereals, wheat yield, yield growth), each the state's best-looking number, with no through-line. If the list of states or the count of six is wrong against the Acts (Punjab 1966 and other 1 November dates), the comments become a correction thread. It also competes with S023 for the same date, and it may be missing the stronger milestone hook if the 1956 reorganisation turns 70 on 1 November 2026 (from memory, verify).
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Kerala infant mortality 5 per thousand, India 25 (T004, 2023). Andhra Pradesh fish 51.58 lakh t of India's 184.02, West Bengal 22.02 (T093, 2023-24 provisional). Madhya Pradesh foodgrains 46,631.3 thousand t, Uttar Pradesh 63,848.2, Punjab 32,777.7 (T062, 2024-25). Karnataka coarse cereals 8,590.9, Rajasthan 9,451.5, Madhya Pradesh 7,775.4 (T060). Haryana wheat yield 4,774 kg/ha, India 3,595 (T081). Chhattisgarh foodgrain yield 1,506 to 2,409 kg/ha, +60%, the fastest rise of any state (T084, 2012-13 to 2024-25).
- **Data editor:** source exists, numbers not re-checked. Every number recomputes from RBI tables: T004 Kerala 5 vs India 25 (2023; Manipur is lower at 3), T093 Andhra Pradesh 28.0% of fish, T062 Madhya Pradesh 46,631.3 second to Uttar Pradesh, T060 Karnataka 8,590.9 second to Rajasthan 9,451.5, T081 Haryana 4,774 vs 3,595 (Punjab higher at 5,123), T084 Chhattisgarh +60.0%, the fastest rise (Maharashtra next at 51.6%). The headline, six states on 1 November and their ages, rests on Acts that are absent from SOURCE_MAP.md and has ambiguous ages for Andhra Pradesh (present boundaries date from 2014), Madhya Pradesh (reorganised 2000) and Karnataka (renamed 1973), so the count of six is not settled.
- **Source:** Dates and the list of states: States Reorganisation Act 1956, Punjab Reorganisation Act 1966, Madhya Pradesh Reorganisation Act 2000, Mysore State (Alteration of Name) Act 1973 (India Code; not in SOURCE_MAP). Numbers: RBI Handbook T004, T093, T062, T060, T081, T084 under public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/. (answer PDF still to read)
- **Evidence it could work:** Timeliness and identity. 'Your state' hooks are the proven comment drivers ('Which state is your state famous for' 3.5M; 'Most famous Sports in your state' 5.8M; comment rates 7 to 12 times the median). Formation stories over-index (26.7% at 1M+ against a 12.4% baseline). Kerala is the most-typed data state (179 suggestions) and Karnataka-versus-Kerala banter is natural.
- **Risks:** The list must be checked against the Acts: the count may be higher (Punjab took its present form on 1 November 1966; Delhi, Lakshadweep and Puducherry have 1 November dates), Karnataka was renamed in 1973 and Andhra Pradesh was re-formed in 2014. Change the title if the count changes. One number per state invites 'why not our best number': label it 'one number each, not a ranking', use only numbers with a stated rank and a clear lead, and put the other candidates in the chart post. Fish is provisional. Do not use income ranks (Gujarat, Goa and Sikkim are missing from the latest GSDP columns).
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** keep. A time-locked 'six states share one birthday' curiosity gap rides greeting-forwarding behaviour and expires around 30 Oct to 1 Nov, so decide now; strip the six unrelated stats down to ages only and verify the dates and the count in the Acts.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. The premise 'six states share one birthday' is contestable and would need correcting (Punjab's present form, Delhi, Lakshadweep and Puducherry also have 1 November dates; Andhra Pradesh is 1956 or 2014 and Telangana's split is politically charged; Madhya Pradesh's age is ambiguous), and the six flattering unrelated statistics are cherry-picked.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. Six unrelated statistics for six states is a numbers dump, none of the six is mine, and Punjab (reorganised on 1 November 1966, per the idea's own risk note) is conspicuously missing from a Short that asks whether your state is on the list.

### S007. Kerala Pays Rs 922 a Day for Rural Construction. India: Rs 441

- **Original title:** Which State Pays India's Top Rural Wage, Yet Has a Top-4 Death Rate?
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 5.6 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 6, fit 8, risk low)
- **Series:** Data Paradox
- **Template:** 5-clue quiz
- **Hook:** Guess the state: India's highest rural construction wage, and the 4th-highest death rate.
- **Outline:** 1. Hook: five clues, no name shown. 2. Clue 1: a rural construction worker earns Rs 922.6 a day (2024-25), 2.1x India's Rs 441.1 and 3.0x Madhya Pradesh's Rs 307.8. 3. Clue 2: 5 infant deaths per 1,000 births (2023); India 25, Uttar Pradesh 37. 4. Clue 3: a sustainable development score of 79, tied for the top of 36 with Uttarakhand. 5. Clue 4, the twist: a death rate of 7.2 per 1,000, the 4th-highest of 35 (India 6.4). 6. Reveal: Kerala. Resolve the twist with NFHS-6: 20.7% of people are aged 60 or older against 12.9% for India. 7. Close: Kerala's income per person is only 7th of 25 states and UTs, Rs 3,08,338; 'Why is Kerala different? Your take?'
- **Who shares it and why:** Malayalis share the Rs 922 wage as pride, and everyone else forwards it to ask why Kerala is different, with ageing as the clever answer.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: identity and pride with an argument built in. Malayalis share it as pride; the 'death rate' twist makes everyone else ask why, and the NFHS-6 age answer gives the sender a clever line. Expected comments: 'remittances', 'Gulf', 'cost of living', 'ageing', plus first-name guesses before the reveal (comments from people who guessed early). Share trigger: clue 4 landing right after three flattering clues.
- **Why it could flop:** Quiz-style Indian-state Shorts have no viral precedent (35 Shorts on 31 channels, median 63 views, best 31K). Clue 1 gives the answer away, because Kerala's high wages are common knowledge, so the guess has no tension. The 'twist' (a high death rate because the population is older) is mild and deflating: negative stat first, boring explanation second. 'Highest wage' is one table (men, rural construction, 21 states) and must be labelled. The strongest asset, the 'Why is Kerala different?' demand (179 suggestions), is wasted behind a quiz wrapper.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Rural construction wage 2024-25 (Rs/day): Kerala 922.6; Jammu and Kashmir 584.5; Tamil Nadu 583.2; Madhya Pradesh 307.8; India 441.1 (21 states report). Infant mortality 2023 (per 1,000): Kerala 5; Uttar Pradesh 37; India 25; Manipur 3 is lower than Kerala. SDG score 2023: Kerala 79 and Uttarakhand 79 tied; Tamil Nadu 78; Bihar 57; India 71. Death rate 2023 (per 1,000): Chhattisgarh 8.3; Odisha 7.7; Punjab 7.3; Kerala 7.2; India 6.4. NFHS-6 aged 60+: Kerala 20.7%, Tamil Nadu 16.3%, India 12.9%. Income per person 2024-25: Kerala Rs 308,338 (7th of 25).
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. Wage table T112 has 20 states plus the India row (not 21), and the SDG table T106 has 35 states/UTs plus the India row (not 36); every other clue matches (Kerala 922.6 = 2.09x India's 441.1 and 3.0x MP; IMR 5 vs UP 37, India 25; SDG 79 tied with Uttarakhand; death rate 7.2, 4th of 35, only 0.1 behind Punjab at 7.3; NFHS-6 60+ 20.7% vs 12.9%). Income Rs 308,338 is 7th only among the 25 jurisdictions that have 2024-25 values in T019 (Goa, Sikkim, Gujarat, Chandigarh and others missing); Kerala was 11th of 34 in 2022-23, so keep 'of 25' on screen.
- **Corrected numbers:** Rural construction wage: highest of 20 states in T112 (not 21). SDG 2023 (T106): tied top of 35 states/UTs (not 36). Income per person: 7th of the 25 with 2024-25 data (11th of 34 in 2022-23).
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook: T112 (rural daily wage, men, construction workers, 2024-25), T004 (infant mortality 2023), T106 (SDG score 2023), T003 (death rate 2023), T019 (income per person); public/datayt/nfhs6/nfhs6_states.json indicator 3 (population aged 60+). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** 'Why is Kerala...' is the richest why-cluster in YouTube autocomplete (Kerala has the most state-specific data suggestions, 179, and the cluster includes 'so developed' and 'different from other states'), and the state-data paradox is a proven hook (FactTechz Bihar internet paradox 4.6M). Honest caution: quiz-style Indian-state Shorts have no viral precedent (35 Shorts, median 63 views), so the paradox is the product and the quiz is only the vehicle.
- **Risks:** 'Highest wage' is one table (men, rural construction workers, 21 states reporting): put that on screen. Kerala is not No. 1 on infant deaths (Manipur is lower) and its life-expectancy lead over Himachal Pradesh and Jammu and Kashmir is only 0.7 years, so those two claims must not be used. Do not offer a cause beyond the age-structure data shown. Overlaps in subject (not in numbers) with batch-001 S013 (Guess the state: people aged 60 or older); here it is only the resolving clue. The title should not be rewritten as a quiz title, since quizzes under-perform.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. Quiz Shorts have no Indian precedent, clue 1 gives Kerala away, and the 'why' the hook invites cannot be answered under our no-causes rule.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. The 'high wage yet high death rate' paradox is manufactured (crude death rate mostly reflects age structure), the 'highest in India' wage is highest of 20 reporting states, 4th-highest death rate rests on 0.1-point gaps (Punjab 7.3 vs Kerala 7.2), and six stacked indicators with mixed periods multiply correction risk.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. It celebrates Kerala using UP's infant mortality of 37 as the foil, and the quiz gives itself away in clue 1 for anyone who has heard Kerala pays well.

### S019. Which State Pays Rural Construction Workers ₹922.6 a Day?

- **Original title:** Kerala's Rural Construction Wage: ₹922.6 a Day, Twice India's
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 5.6 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 5, fit 9, risk medium)
- **Series:** One State, One Number
- **Template:** new: State Spotlight. State outline zooms in, one giant number, runner-up and India bars, 'where is yours?' loop. Config = state, table id, value, period, unit, runner-up, India value, rank, source line. The builder refuses a claim if the lead is under 1 percentage point or the state has no latest-year value, and it must scan all rows including small states and UTs. A scan over RBI, NFHS-6, PLFS and DoT picks each state's best-supported number, so all 36 states and UTs run from config files.
- **Hook:** ₹922.6 a day. Which state pays rural construction workers that?
- **Outline:** 20-30 s. 1) Hook: the number alone on screen. 2) Reveal: Kerala outline, label 'average daily wage, men, rural construction workers, 2024-25'. 3) India's figure ₹441.1: Kerala is about 2.1x. 4) Next on the list: Jammu and Kashmir ₹584.5, Tamil Nadu ₹583.2; lowest, Madhya Pradesh ₹307.8. 5) Caveat on screen: 'Rupee wages; prices differ by state.' 6) Loop: 'Comment your state. We'll post its number next.'
- **Who shares it and why:** Malayalis and the Kerala diaspora share it with pride, and construction and migrant-labour groups in other states pass it on, starting the cost-of-living argument.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: 'your state' personalisation plus pride and argument. Malayali workers and diaspora share it, as do construction and migrant-labour groups in other states. Expect fights on cost of living ('real wage?') and migration, and 'do my state'. Trigger: the closing 'Comment your state, we'll post its number', which turns the comment box into the production queue.
- **Why it could flop:** A single number on a card sits closer to the chart-style Shorts family (median 6,200 views) than to the narrated map-story family the idea cites as evidence (Geo Study's state series is map profiles, not one-number cards). Kerala is the guessable answer and its high wages are a familiar talking point, the datum is narrow (men, rural construction, 21 states, current rupees), and the comment section is likely to become a migrant-labour and cost-of-living fight that needs moderating.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Kerala ₹922.6 a day (T112, 2024-25, men, rural construction workers); Jammu and Kashmir ₹584.5; Tamil Nadu ₹583.2; Himachal Pradesh ₹557.9; India ₹441.1; Madhya Pradesh ₹307.8, the lowest of the 21 states reporting.
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. T112 has 20 states plus an India row, so the caveat must read 'highest of the 20 states in the table', not 21; the numbers all match (Kerala 922.6, J&K 584.5, Tamil Nadu 583.2, Himachal Pradesh 557.9, India 441.1, MP 307.8 lowest, Kerala/India = 2.09x). The table note confirms the original agency is the Labour Bureau (Indian Labour Journal), and the India figure is a quotation-weighted average, not a worker-weighted one.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T112.json (RBI Handbook table 112; confirm the original agency, probably Labour Bureau, from the table note). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** This is the best-evidenced Shorts series: Geo Study's state-by-state animations (24 Shorts, median 982,500, minimum 212K, 11 at 1M+). The digest recommends 'one state, one hot fact' in a fixed layout, closing on 'where does YOUR state rank?', which lifted comment rates to 7 to 12 times the median. Kerala is the most-typed data state (179 suggestions) and 'why is Kerala so developed' is the richest why-cluster.
- **Risks:** Wages are in current rupees and prices differ: say so on screen and never say 'better paid'. Only 21 states report, so say 'highest of the 21 states in the table', not 'in India'. Labour and migrant politics draw heated comments: moderate, never rank workers by origin. The leads files scanned only 22 larger states and mislead for some claims (in 2022-23 and 2023-24 Goa and Sikkim, not Delhi, lead income per person; Manipur at 3 is below Kerala at 5 on infant deaths): the builder must scan every row. Template-factory risk: rotate the order and the indicator family (income, farm, power, work) so 36 episodes do not read as one shape.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. A single number on a card sits with the chart-Short family (6,200 median), and a 36-state config-driven run is exactly the repetitive-template pattern YouTube penalises.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. One well-bounded fact (Rs 922.6 vs India Rs 441.1, 2.09x, Kerala 58% above the runner-up) that is safe provided the screen says 'men, rural construction workers, highest of the 20 states in the table' (not 21) and 'rupee wages, prices differ'.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. A single wage number on a card is chart-Short territory, it makes Madhya Pradesh the bottom of the story, and 'comment your state and we'll post its number' sets up a comment section where low-wage states feel put on display.

### S039. Women at Work in 2025: Nagaland 64.5%, Delhi 13.1%. Your State?

- **Original title:** Women at Work: One State Went From 16.7% to 64.5%
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 5.6 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 7, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Template:** Guess the State (closing on the Change, then-vs-now, template)
- **Hook:** Guess the state: women in the workforce went from 16.7% to 64.5%.
- **Outline:** 1) Hook with a blank map and the two numbers. 2) Clue 1: it is not a big city. 3) Clue 2: it is in the Northeast. 4) Reveal Nagaland (16.7% in 2017-18, 64.5% in 2025) with Arunachal Pradesh close behind (14.7% to 61.7%). 5) India as a whole: 23.3% (2017-18), 41.7% (2023-24), 40.0% (2025). 6) The other end in 2025: Delhi 13.1%, Lakshadweep 21.9%, Haryana 23.8%. 7) What the number counts (women aged 15 and over who work or look for work, including unpaid family work) and 'Where does your state sit?' map. Calendar: International Women's Day, Mon 8 Mar 2027 (pre-build, publish that morning); then swap in calendar-year 2026 numbers within 48 hours of the PLFS Annual Report on Thu 25 Mar 2027 (state-wise).
- **Who shares it and why:** Northeast viewers send it to friends with pride and women's networks and workplaces pass it around on 8 March, while Delhi viewers argue 'why Delhi?' in the comments; the Nagaland-versus-Delhi contrast is the real hook, not the jump.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Identity and surprise. Northeast viewers share it with pride; women's networks and workplaces share it on 8 March as 'look at Nagaland'. Comments: 'is that real work or farm work?' (answered in the definition beat), 'why Delhi?', 'my state?'. The share trigger is the 16.7 to 64.5 count-up with the map revealing a state people did not guess.
- **Why it could flop:** The headline jump is the weakest number in the set. 2017-18 is a July-June survey year and 2025 is a calendar year, the measure counts unpaid family work, and small-state estimates carry sampling error. The first reply will be 'that is farm work, not jobs', and the video's own definition beat deflates its hook. 'One state' is fragile: Arunachal Pradesh went 14.7 to 61.7, and in 2023-24 its rise to 66.5 was bigger than Nagaland's to 64.3. Sikkim (66.7) is higher than Nagaland in 2025. Guess-the-state quizzes have no Indian lift on their own. The Women's Day pitch is a one-day window. Safer build: lead with the same-year 2025 gap (Nagaland 64.5, Delhi 13.1) and make the change a hedged side beat.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Women's LFPR: India 23.3 (2017-18), 41.7 (2023-24), 40.0 (2025). Nagaland 16.7 (2017-18), 64.3 (2023-24), 64.5 (2025). Arunachal Pradesh 14.7 (2017-18), 66.5 (2023-24), 61.7 (2025). 2025: Sikkim 66.7; Tamil Nadu 47.3; Kerala 41.2; Haryana 23.8; Lakshadweep 21.9; Delhi 13.1 (14.3 in 2017-18). Goa fell from 30.9 to 27.4. Unemployment rate 2025: India 3.1; Gujarat 0.9; Goa 8.3.
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. All figures match plfs_annual.json (Nagaland 16.7 to 64.5, Arunachal 14.7 to 61.7, India 23.3/41.7/40.0, Delhi 13.1, Lakshadweep 21.9, Haryana 23.8, Goa 30.9 to 27.4), but 16.7 to 64.5 spans a July-June 2017-18 year and calendar 2025, so lead with the like-for-like 16.7 to 64.3 (2017-18 to 2023-24). Nagaland is not the biggest riser: Arunachal rose more to 2023-24 (+51.8 vs +47.6) and Nagaland leads to 2025 by only 0.8 point (+47.8 vs +47.0), so do not say 'one state' or 'biggest'; 8 Mar 2027 is a Monday and the 25 Mar 2027 release date comes from the digest, not re-verified.
- **Corrected numbers:** Nagaland 16.7 (2017-18) to 64.3 (2023-24); 64.5 is calendar 2025. Change to 2023-24: Arunachal +51.8, Nagaland +47.6; change to 2025: Nagaland +47.8, Arunachal +47.0 (0.8 apart).
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (MoSPI NSO PLFS, LFPR, female, rural plus urban, age 15+, usual status PS+SS; 2017-18 to 2025); PLFS Annual Report for calendar year 2026 (MoSPI Advance Release Calendar, 25 Mar 2027). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Fits the strongest Shorts hooks: a surprising India trend (The Economist 'India's surprising baby bust' 2.8M), a then-vs-now frame (Forceman 6.1M) and a belief-overturning state fact that is not on the saturated lists (literacy, richest, population). It ends on 'your state', which drew 7-12x the median comment rate. Honest limit: jobs and salary Shorts are thin in the sample (42 Shorts, 7 at 1M+, mostly company stories), so this is a bet on the hook, not the topic.
- **Risks:** LFPR counts unpaid family work, so never write 'paid jobs'. Periods differ (2017-18 is a July-June survey year; 2025 is a calendar year). Small-state estimates carry sampling error. Nagaland and Arunachal Pradesh differ by only 0.8 point in the 2017-18 to 2025 change, so never claim 'biggest jump'. Keep Delhi and Goa wording neutral (no 'worst'). India dipped from 41.7 to 40.0, show it. The PLFS monthly bulletin on the 15th may not carry state tables (unverified), so rely on the annual report.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. The headline jump mixes a July-June 2017-18 year with calendar 2025 (the data editor had to correct it), it is a guess-the-state quiz with no Indian precedent, and it duplicates S022.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. The headline 16.7% to 64.5% splices a July-June survey year with a calendar year, a roughly fourfold jump in a tiny Northeast sample is more likely partly sampling noise than a real change we can vouch for, and the measure counts unpaid family work.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. It overlaps S022, mixes a July-June survey year with a calendar year (the data editor flagged the 64.5 figure), and a Nagaland count-up has no pull for viewers outside the Northeast.

We'll answer with its rank.' Pilot metric chosen after the data pull: women who have used the internet (NFHS-6 district sheet), or rainfall (IMD), or density (Census).
- **Who shares it and why:** Tamil Nadu natives and the diaspora drop it into district, college and alumni WhatsApp groups with their own district's rank, and the channel's replies with ranks keep the thread alive.

Expect district names in the comments ('Coimbatore!', 'Chennai bias', 'where is Ariyalur'), and the channel replies with ranks, which keeps the thread alive. Trigger: the map filling with names and the last frame 'comment your district'.

Map Motion (228K subscribers, Tamil-English voice) already owns the Tamil Nadu district lane, no district data is collected, the 38 current districts do not match the 32 in Census 2011, and many Tamil-speaking viewers search in Tamil, which an English voice cannot serve.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** 
- **Data editor:** source exists, numbers not re-checked. NFHS is in SOURCE_MAP.md, but nfhs6_states.json is state-level only and docs/DATAYT_BACKLOG.md says non-alcohol district data is still 'waiting', so no district value for women who have used the internet is in hand and the metric is not even chosen. District estimates have wide sampling error, so check that #1 is not statistically tied with #2 before using 'most'; the Map Motion figures cited (684K to 2.3M, median 1.25M; 2,400 comments on 1.69M = 0.142%) match research_shorts_india.md.
- **Source:** NFHS-6 district fact sheets (NFHS, IIPS, listed in SOURCE_MAP) for the pilot. The repo already holds NFHS-6 numbers for Tamil Nadu's 38 districts for the alcohol map (docs/DATAYT_BACKLOG.md item 5), so the fetch route exists, but nothing district-level is in our folder. Alternatives in SOURCE_MAP: India Meteorological Department district rainfall, Census of India. (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** Map Motion's six Tamil Nadu district Shorts reached 684K to 2.3M each (median 1.25M); its 'Top 5 Cleanest Districts' got 2,400 comments on 1.69M views, a 0.142% comment rate, about 11x the 0.0127% median. Tamil Nadu is the strongest place in the long-form data (75% at 3x+ on 16 videos). English district rankings for other states are an open lane (searches for Kerala, Karnataka, Gujarat, Uttar Pradesh and Maharashtra districts found only small Hindi or Marathi channels, best 109K).
- **Risks:** District data is not collected. Boundaries changed: Census 2011 has 32 districts and Tamil Nadu now has 38, so use only sources built on current districts or apportion with a stated method. Check the NFHS-6 vintage and suppressed cells. Map Motion (228K subscribers) owns the Tamil Nadu superlative lane (smallest, cleanest, hottest, coldest, rainiest): use official-indicator angles, not the same topics. Do not rank districts by caste, religion or party. District names in English letters only (English-only rule). Language risk as for the other Shorts: ship a text-only cut.
- **Effort:** medium

### S009. If Tamil Nadu Were a Country, Which Economies Would It Beat?

- **Original title:** If Tamil Nadu Were a Country: Where Would Its Economy Rank?
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.9 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 5, fit 4, risk medium)
- **Series:** If It Were a Country
- **Template:** new: world GDP ladder (a scrolling list of countries by GDP, the state's bar drops into its slot as the narration names the neighbours above and below)
- **Hook:** Tamil Nadu's economy is Rs 31.19 lakh crore. Which countries is that bigger than?
- **Outline:** 1. Hook: the state's size in rupees. 2. Convert at the year's official average exchange rate, and show the method on screen (to be collected). 3. The ladder: the countries just above and just below Tamil Nadu in the World Bank GDP list (to be collected). 4. Per person: Tamil Nadu Rs 3,61,619 against those neighbours' per-person figures (to be collected). 5. Plain line: 'This is a size comparison, nothing more.' 6. Close: 'Which state next? Maharashtra, Uttar Pradesh, Karnataka? Comment.'
- **Who shares it and why:** Tamil viewers send it to relatives abroad and friends in other states when the bar passes a country they know, but only if that country is a surprise.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: a what-if built on pride and scale. Tamil viewers send it to relatives abroad and to friends in other states ('ours is bigger than country X'); the comments become a list of countries and 'do Karnataka next', and the series invites a state per week. Share trigger: the frame where the bar passes a country the viewer knows.
- **Why it could flop:** The whole idea depends on where Tamil Nadu lands on the ladder, and no world data is collected yet, so a forgettable mid-sized neighbour gives no brag. Flat country-comparison Shorts sit at 15K-41K (Data Duck), and the state-vs-Pakistan template decayed from 135K to 6.7K on World In Details. The Map Motion hits (8.4M, 12M) ran on independence drama we must avoid. It also needs an exchange-rate conversion and an Indian fiscal year matched to a calendar year, which invites 'your method is wrong' comments. It is the most expensive build in the set (effort high).
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Tamil Nadu GSDP 2024-25 at current prices: Rs 311,859,030 lakh (about Rs 31.19 lakh crore), second of the 25 states and UTs with data. Income per person 2024-25: Rs 361,619. All world figures: none until collected.
- **Data editor:** source exists, numbers not re-checked. Tamil Nadu figures verified in RBI T021 (2024-25 Rs 311,859,030 lakh = Rs 31.19 lakh crore, 2nd of 25 reporting behind Maharashtra, 4.7% ahead of UP) and T019 (Rs 361,619), but every world number is uncollected: World Bank API is in SOURCE_MAP.md, yet no FBIL row is mapped (only RBI DBIE and BIS list exchange rates), so the rate source must be chosen. Also Gujarat, Goa and others have no 2024-25 GSDP, so say 'second of 25 reporting', and match the April-March fiscal year to the calendar-year World Bank GDP.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T021 (GSDP, current prices, 2024-25) and T019 (income per person), in hand. World Bank Open Data API (GDP, current US$) and the RBI or FBIL average exchange rate for the matching year are mapped in SOURCE_MAP.md but not collected. (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** Map Motion's 'What If Tamil Nadu Became an Independent Country' reached 8.4M and 'What If Tamil Nadu Never Existed' 12M; ibx2cat's 'larger GDP than 193 countries' format got 1.0M and Forceman's 'Neighbor Countries Unite' 4.3M. Tamil Nadu is also the second most-typed state in data searches (116). Honest caution: Data Duck's flat country-comparison versions sit at 15K-41K, so execution (ladder motion, one thing at a time, a neighbour-by-neighbour reveal) matters.
- **Risks:** Wording must read as a size comparison, not secession: never 'independent', 'breakaway' or a political line (Map Motion's frame sits close to our neutrality rule). State the conversion (market exchange rate, not PPP) and the year match (Indian fiscal year against calendar year) on screen. GSDP and World Bank GDP are not identical concepts, so say 'comparable'. Do not mock any neighbouring country; list countries neutrally. Tamil Nadu's lead over Karnataka in GSDP is 8% and over Uttar Pradesh 4.7%; no '#2' claim beyond the table's coverage. Gujarat is missing in 2024-25.
- **Effort:** high

### SG05. Tamil Nadu's 38 Districts: Who Got More Rain Than Normal?

- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.9 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 5, fit 4, risk medium)
- **Series:** Your District, Ranked
