- **Hook:** Which of Tamil Nadu's 38 districts got the most rain this season? Guess before the reveal.
- **Outline:** Beat 1 (0-3s) Hook over the district map, all 38 districts grey. Beat 2 One line on the yardstick: 'normal' is IMD's long-period average, cumulative rainfall since the season started, as on a stated date. Beat 3 Top five wettest districts against normal, one at a time, with percent above normal. Beat 4 Bottom three: districts furthest below normal. Beat 5 'Find your district' card: all 38 in five bands (much above, above, normal, below, much below). Beat 6 Close: 'Kerala's 14 districts next? Comment your district.' RELEASE: repeat while the northeast monsoon (October to December, the IMD season) is running, then roll English district rankings to Kerala, Karnataka, Maharashtra, Gujarat and Uttar Pradesh. STEP ZERO, data collection before any script: (a) pull IMD district-wise rainfall (actual, normal, departure) from 1 Oct onwards; (b) get district boundaries that sit inside the official outer boundary; (c) cross-check against RBI T102 Tamil Nadu sub-division totals.
- **Who shares it and why:** Tamil Nadu district and college WhatsApp groups forward the frame where their own district flips above or below normal rain, which is live news while the northeast monsoon runs.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** 'Your district' personalisation plus timeliness and a quiz the viewer can fail. Shared in district and college WhatsApp groups ('Madurai got 40% less than normal') and by farming and student families. Likely comments: viewers naming their district with rain stories, 'my taluk got more', 'do Kerala'. Share trigger: the frame where the 38-district map flips from grey to above/below-normal colours with 'Find yours' on screen.
- **Why it could flop:** Map Motion owns the Tamil Nadu district niche (six Shorts, median 1.25M) and its rainiest-districts Short was its weakest at 684K, so rain is the weakest topic in the proven series. 'Versus normal' replaces a simple rank with a harder concept, and IMD district figures are provisional, sometimes missing, and change daily, so the Short is stale within days. Nothing is collected: no district data and no district polygons, at high effort, and a Tamil-and-English audience may want Tamil narration (297 'tamil' suggestions). It only works if published inside the monsoon window.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** RBI T102, Tamil Nadu IMD sub-division annual rainfall: 1,131.0 mm (2022), 1,036.5 mm (2023), 1,173.4 mm (2024). District figures: none collected yet.
- **Data editor:** source exists, numbers not re-checked. RBI T102 matches for Tamil Nadu (1,131.0 mm in 2022, 1,036.5 in 2023, 1,173.4 in 2024), and SOURCE_MAP.md lists IMD rainfall 'by sub-division and district' as script-fetchable, but that entry is from general knowledge, no district figure or polygon file is in hand, and the 38-district departure-from-normal table has not been seen. Percent-above-normal early in the October to December season rests on tiny bases and can be noise, and Map Motion's 'Rainiest' Short was its weakest at 684K.
- **Source:** IMD district-wise rainfall (SOURCE_MAP: India Meteorological Department, script can fetch, not yet fetched); district polygons not collected; state-level cross-check RBI Handbook T102. (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 3 (district lane). Measured: Map Motion's six Tamil Nadu district Shorts (smallest, cleanest, coldest, hottest, rainiest) have a median of 1.25M and a minimum of 684K; the rainiest was its weakest at 684K, still about 9x the 73K map-story median. English district rankings for other states are an open lane (searches found only small Hindi or Marathi channels, best 109K). Our edge over Map Motion (228K subscribers, owns Tamil Nadu) is official IMD numbers that refresh weekly in season, plus a 'find your district' card aimed at the 7 to 12 times comment rate of personalised hooks.
- **Risks:** No district data is collected, and IMD district figures are provisional and sometimes missing; show the 'normal' definition and the as-on date on every frame. Map Motion owns the Tamil Nadu district niche, so do not copy its topics (smallest, cleanest, coldest, hottest). No flood, death or disaster framing. Weekly updates cost hours, so run a few episodes in season only. District polygons must sit inside the official outer boundary.
- **Effort:** high

### SG12. Sikkim Was a Kingdom. How Did It Become an Indian State?

- **Original title:** How Sikkim Became an Indian State
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.8 (virality 5, appeal 5, distinct 5, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** Voice-off vs narration test (3 hook types x 2 styles)
- **Template:** Same origin template as the Puducherry Short with a voice-off flag: text on the map, own music only, big English labels, no narration
- **Hook:** SIKKIM WAS A KINGDOM BEFORE IT WAS A STATE. (on screen, no voice)
- **Outline:** Beat 1 (0-3s) On-screen hook over the official map, Sikkim highlighted. Beat 2 '1950: a treaty makes it a protectorate of India' (verify in the treaty text). Beat 3 '1975: the Constitution (36th Amendment) Act makes it a state' with the year stamped huge (verify). Beat 4 Today card: '68.2% of women are in the labour force (India 41.7%)' and '5G in 349 of 461 villages (75.7%)'. Beat 5 Close text: 'Which place next? Goa? Puducherry?' TEST PROTOCOL (set before posting): six matched Shorts, 3 hook types x 2 styles, alternate days, different but matched places so nothing is duplicated. ORIGIN: Sikkim (voice-off) vs Puducherry (MiniMax voice). PARADOX: Meghalaya (voice-off) vs Haryana (MiniMax; 5th in income per person, third-lowest women's labour force). PROFILE: West Bengal (voice-off) vs Rajasthan (MiniMax). Judge on viewed-vs-swiped, average percentage viewed, and views against the 73K map-story and 6.2K chart yardsticks. Decision rule fixed in advance: voice-off stays the default if it matches or beats narration on viewed-vs-swiped in at least 2 of 3 pairs; if narration leads in all 3, keep the voice and move the Hindi track up.
- **Who shares it and why:** Sikkim and Himalayan-belt families and geography-trivia fans could forward a silent text-on-map Short across languages, but 'a kingdom before a state' is a known fact with little to argue about.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Origin hook (the best-measured Short hook) with a language-free share path: a silent text-on-map Short can be forwarded across Hindi, Bengali, Nepali and Tamil speakers without translation. Sent to relatives and friends from Sikkim and the Himalayan belt and to geography-trivia fans. Likely comments: 'do Goa / Puducherry', 'was it really a kingdom', 'which state next'. Share trigger: the giant year stamp snapping onto the highlighted state.
- **Why it could flop:** The test is only six Shorts on a cold channel, which cannot separate narration from hook or place, as the idea itself admits. Bharat Mapping's silent hits (currency notes, sports in your state) are list-style, not narrative, and its median is 38K with only 29% of Shorts at 100K+, so silent text alone may not carry a treaty-and-amendment story. The hook states a known fact instead of opening a curiosity gap, the labour-force and 5G card has nothing to do with the merger, and Sikkim's 1975 merger is politically sensitive (China border, the monarchy), so wording must stay strictly to the gazetted Acts.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Sikkim: women's labour force participation 68.2% (PLFS 2023-24, age 15+) vs India 41.7%; 5G in 349 of 461 villages = 75.7% (DoT, 28 Feb 2026). Protocol extras: Haryana income per person 353,182 rupees, 5th (RBI T019, 2024-25); Haryana women's labour force 24.2% (PLFS 2023-24); Haryana 5G in 96.8% of villages.
- **Data editor:** source exists, numbers not re-checked. The Today-card numbers recompute: Sikkim 68.2% vs India 41.7% (plfs_annual.json 2023-24), 349 of 461 villages = 75.7% 5G (mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json), Haryana 24.2% and 96.8%, and Haryana Rs 353,182 (T019 2024-25). The 1950 treaty and 1975 36th Amendment dates are not in any local file and the Gazette of India or India Code is not in SOURCE_MAP.md, so they must be read from the primary text, and Haryana's '5th' is only 5th of 25 reporting jurisdictions (7th of 34 in 2022-23).
- **Source:** Act and treaty text from the Gazette of India (not in SOURCE_MAP.md). Today card: public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (LFPR, 2023-24, female, rural + urban, age 15+) and public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json. (answer PDF still to read)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 8 (language and narration test). Measured: Hindi is the detected language for 93 of the top 125 Shorts across 25 channels, and English-narrated chart Shorts are the weakest (Data Duck median 15K, Dataverse 33.5K). The only silent evidence is Bharat Mapping's text-on-map hits at 5.8M to 11M, but its median is 38K and only 29% of its Shorts reach 100K, so it is not a floor. No idea defines this test, so every other Short would confound format, hook and voice. The cost is a voice-off flag, not a new template. It runs on the best-measured Short hook (origin), so the test has its best chance of showing a voice effect.
- **Risks:** Six videos give a direction, not significance, and a cold-start channel adds noise; matched places are similar, not identical, so treat the result as a reason to run more pairs. Dates and treaty names are from memory and must be read in the gazette. Keep Sikkim's border strictly to the official outline: no border disputes, no neighbouring claims; word it 'became a state', never 'annexed'. Do not post duplicates across the two styles (different places per pair). Thin-lead rule: do not call Sikkim's women's labour force 'the highest' (Meghalaya 71.8%, Himachal Pradesh 67.6% are close).
- **Effort:** low

### S024. Which Country's Economy Is as Big as Tamil Nadu's?

- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.7 (virality 5, appeal 7, distinct 4, fit 6, risk medium)
- **Series:** Size of a Country
- **Template:** new: Country Ladder. The state's economy, in rupees then US dollars, scrolls down a ranked list of world economies and docks beside the nearest countries; 'size comparison only' label stays on screen. Config = state, GSDP, exchange rate and year, World Bank list. One episode per state, with different comparators.
- **Hook:** Tamil Nadu's economy is worth ₹31.19 lakh crore. Which country is that?
- **Outline:** 30-40 s. 1) Hook: the rupee figure, then the question. 2) Convert at the stated average exchange rate to US dollars (World Bank). 3) The ladder scrolls down the World Bank GDP list and stops between two countries (names from the data). 4) Same ladder for Karnataka (₹28.84 lakh crore) and Uttar Pradesh (₹29.78 lakh crore). 5) Label: 'Size comparison only. States are not countries. Financial year vs calendar year.' 6) Close: 'Where does your state land? Comment it.'
- **Who shares it and why:** State-pride accounts and diaspora professionals quote 'my state is bigger than X' in comments and group chats, and the Tamil Nadu, Karnataka and Uttar Pradesh camps argue about per-person size and PPP.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: identity and pride plus an economy-only what-if. State-pride accounts and diaspora professionals quote it ('my state is bigger than ...'). Expect 'per person matters', 'GDP or PPP', 'add Kerala next' and neighbour rivalry in the comments. Trigger frame: the state silhouette docking beside a country on the ladder.
- **Why it could flop:** The reveal is fragile and often flat: which country sits beside Tamil Nadu depends on the exchange rate and year chosen (financial-year GSDP converted at a market rate against calendar-year World Bank GDP), and neighbours on the ladder sit within a few percent, so 'as big as X' can flip and draw fact-check replies; a mid-ranked country gives no emotional payoff. Flat country comparisons sit at 15K-41K, repeated state-vs-country videos saturated (World In Details 135K down to 6.7K), three states in 40 seconds dilutes it, and the World Bank data is not collected.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** GSDP 2024-25, current prices (T021, ₹ lakh): Tamil Nadu 3.11859e+08 = ₹31.19 lakh crore; Uttar Pradesh 2.97822e+08 = ₹29.78 lakh crore; Karnataka ₹28.84 lakh crore; Maharashtra 4.53152e+08 = ₹45.32 lakh crore. 2022-23 (the only latest year with all 34 states): Maharashtra 36.4, Tamil Nadu 23.7, Karnataka 23.2, Uttar Pradesh 23.0, Gujarat 22.0 lakh crore.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T021.json (GSDP, current prices, ₹ lakh) is in hand. Still to collect: World Bank GDP in current US dollars (NY.GDP.MKTP.CD) and the official exchange rate (PA.NUS.FCRF) via the World Bank API listed in SOURCE_MAP. Gujarat needs MoSPI eSankhyiki. (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** Scale made literal works: ibx2cat 'This American state has a larger GDP than 193 countries' got 1.0M. Tamil Nadu what-ifs are huge when executed with a story (Map Motion 'What If Tamil Nadu Became an Independent Country' 8.4M) while flat country-vs-country charts sit at 15K to 41K (Data Duck), so execution decides. World In Details' 'Tamil Nadu vs Pakistan' did 135K (9x its median) but 'Gujarat vs Pakistan' 10K and 'Uttar Pradesh vs Pakistan' 6.7K: the template saturates, so vary comparators.
- **Risks:** Secession reading: Map Motion's frame was independence; ours must say 'size comparison' and never use 'independent' or 'break away'. Skip neighbour-country gloating (no 'vs Pakistan'). Financial-year GSDP converted at a market rate against calendar-year World Bank GDP: state the approximation. Data traps: the RBI latest-year column lacks nine states and UTs and Gujarat has nothing after 2022-23, so the leads' 'top three' list is only the top of 25 states; Tamil Nadu's lead over Karnataka and Uttar Pradesh is thin (2022-23: 23.7, 23.2, 23.0), so never say 'second-largest'. Cannot be built until the World Bank pull is done.
- **Effort:** medium

### S037. Pongal Pot: One State Leads in Rice, Milk and Cane. Guess Which

- **Original title:** Pongal Pot Quiz: Which State Grows the Rice, Milk and Cane?
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.6 (virality 3, appeal 5, distinct 5, fit 8, risk low)
- **Series:** Festival Plate
- **Template:** 5-clue quiz (beat family: clues), cut to three ingredient rounds
- **Hook:** A Pongal pot has rice, milk and jaggery. Which state grows the most of each?
- **Outline:** 1) Hook: a pot graphic with three empty slots. 2) Round 1, rice: four cards (Punjab, Tamil Nadu, West Bengal, Uttar Pradesh), 3-2-1, reveal Uttar Pradesh 20,758.7 thousand tonnes, ahead of Telangana 17,445.7 (2024-25). 3) Round 2, milk: reveal Uttar Pradesh 38,780 ahead of Rajasthan 34,733 (2023-24). 4) Round 3, sugarcane (the stand-in for jaggery): reveal Uttar Pradesh 220,801.3, nearly half of India's 454,611 (2024-25). 5) Twist: 'The same state three times.' 6) Tamil Nadu's real strength: cane yield 102,000 kg a hectare against India's 83,416, higher than any state (only Puducherry, at 123,000, is higher), while its output ranks 10th in rice, 11th in milk and 5th in cane. 7) Close: 'Happy Pongal. What goes in your pot? Comment your state's harvest dish.' Calendar: Thai Pongal about Thu 14 Jan 2027 (confirm the date); build by 5 Jan, publish 12-13 Jan, no new data needed.
- **Who shares it and why:** Tamil families might forward it to relatives in other states on Pongal morning as a festival-plus-pride curiosity, but nothing measured says people forward production tonnage, so this is a hope, not a trigger.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Festival group chats. Tamil families forward it on Pongal morning to relatives in other states, and viewers in Punjab, Kerala and Telangana reply in the comments with their own harvest dishes and arguments. The share trigger is 'Uttar Pradesh, Uttar Pradesh, Uttar Pradesh' followed by Tamil Nadu's yield bar. It is the lowest-effort festival idea and can recur (Diwali sweets use milk and sugar; other harvest festivals use their own crops).
- **Why it could flop:** The reveal is the generic 'biggest state wins' GK answer (Uttar Pradesh three times), which sits in the saturated production and largest-state lane. The research found no Indian precedent for clue quizzes (35 quiz-style state Shorts, median 63 views, best 31K). The Tamil Nadu viewer who clicks for pride then sees Tamil Nadu ranked 10th, 11th and 5th. The festival window is about three days and a zero-subscriber channel has no distribution to catch it. 'Forwarded on Pongal morning' is an assumption nobody measured. Small honesty wobbles: sugarcane standing in for jaggery, milk is not 'grown', and the years differ (milk is 2023-24).
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Rice 2024-25 (thousand tonnes): Uttar Pradesh 20,758.7; Telangana 17,445.7; Tamil Nadu 7,093.6 (10th). Milk 2023-24: Uttar Pradesh 38,780; Rajasthan 34,733; Tamil Nadu 10,808 (11th). Sugarcane 2024-25: India 454,611; Uttar Pradesh 220,801.3; Maharashtra 109,970.2; Tamil Nadu 13,350.8 (5th). Cane yield 2024-25 (kg a hectare): Puducherry 123,000; Tamil Nadu 102,000; Maharashtra 94,230; India 83,416.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States T058 (rice), T091 (milk), T065 (sugarcane production), T087 (sugarcane yield), T080; original sources Ministry of Agriculture and Farmers' Welfare and Basic Animal Husbandry Statistics. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Food-and-culture state hooks are a proven lane: 184 Shorts in that tag, 11.4% at 1M+ (EduMap 'Indian States and Their Famous Food' 4.9M, dances 5.7M). Tamil Nadu is the state with the strongest measured pull (long-form place videos naming it hit 3x in 75% of 16 videos; Map Motion's Tamil Nadu Shorts reached 8.4M-12M). A festival gives a reason to post now, and the twist (one state three times, then Tamil Nadu's yield) overturns a belief while giving a Tamil audience a pride number.
- **Risks:** Jaggery is not in the tables, so say sugarcane stands in for it. Years differ (milk 2023-24). Totals favour big states, so present Tamil Nadu's position as yield, not as weakness, and show the 10th, 11th and 5th ranks neutrally. Verify the Pongal date against the almanac. No Tamil lines in narration (English only); use 'Pongal' as a name only. One-day shelf life, so schedule it for publication on the day.
- **Effort:** low

### SG02. Lakshadweep Means 'One Lakh Islands'. How Many Are There?

- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.6 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 5, fit 6, risk medium)
- **Series:** How [State] Got Its Name
- **Template:** Beat family: 5-clue quiz (clues build the name from its two Sanskrit parts; the real island count is the last reveal)
- **Hook:** Lakshadweep means 'one lakh islands'. Guess how many there really are.
- **Outline:** Beat 1 (0-3s) Hook text and the name split in two: laksha (one lakh, 100,000) + dweep (island). Beat 2 Guess: 'Is it 100,000, 10,000, 1,000, 100 or fewer?' with a 3-second thinking pause. Beat 3 Reveal: islands appear one by one in an inset on the official-boundary map until the counter stops; the figure is read from the UT administration and checked against the Census before scripting (sources count atolls, reefs and islets differently, so name the source and date on screen). Beat 4 The old name: Laccadive, Minicoy and Amindivi Islands, a Union Territory from 1 Nov 1956, renamed Lakshadweep by an Act effective 1 Nov 1973 (dates from memory, verify in the gazette). Beat 5 Today card: all 27 villages have mobile coverage, 5G reaches 5 of 27 (18.5%); 2024 annual rainfall 2,144.1 mm (IMD sub-division, RBI T102). Beat 6 Close: 'Whose name should we decode next?' NAME-BRANCH SLATE (one per Short, only names with an official, uncontested origin; dates from memory): Meghalaya (peg 21 Jan statehood), Uttarakhand (renamed from Uttaranchal, peg 1 Jan 2007), Tamil Nadu (Madras State renamed, peg mid-Jan 1969), Himachal Pradesh, Jharkhand, Odisha (renamed 2011).
- **Who shares it and why:** Trivia-minded friends send the 'guess the island count' challenge to each other, and Kerala and Lakshadweep families forward the name-versus-reality twist, but the audience for one tiny UT is small.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A surprise that overturns a belief (the name promises a lakh, the map delivers a handful) plus a quiz the viewer can fail. Shared with Kerala and Lakshadweep families and trivia-minded friends as 'guess before you scroll'. Likely comments: guesses typed in ('I said 100!'), corrections of the count, 'what does my state's name mean?'. Share trigger: the counter freezing far below 100,000 while the name stays on screen.
- **Why it could flop:** Quiz-style Indian-state Shorts are the weakest measured format (35 Shorts, median 63 views, best 31K; quiz/guess tag 5.9% at 1M+ vs 12.4% baseline), and the idea is built on a 3-second guess pause. The 'name promises a lakh, reality is tiny' twist is real but is a well-known trivia fact, and the island count is not in our files and differs by source (atolls, reefs, islets). The 5G and rainfall 'today' card is unrelated to the name, and Lakshadweep is a sensitive topic area (religion, demography, administration disputes) that has to be avoided entirely, which leaves a thin story.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Lakshadweep: 27 of 27 villages with mobile coverage; 5G in 5 villages (18.5%) as on 28 Feb 2026 (DoT, Lok Sabha SQ 470). Annual rainfall 2,144.1 mm in 2024 (RBI Handbook T102, IMD sub-division). Island count: not in our files, to be read from the source.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** Name and renaming from the Act texts (Gazette of India; not in SOURCE_MAP.md). Island count from the Lakshadweep UT administration and the Census of India (SOURCE_MAP: Census of India, script can fetch; not collected). Today card: public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json and RBI Handbook T102 (Lakshadweep, 2024). (answer PDF still to read)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 1 (name-origins branch). The only name idea so far is S018 (virality 3). Measured: name-origin Shorts are 2 of 8 at 1M+ (thin sample): Finology 'This is how Indian States got their name' 3.9M and Map Motion's capital-before-Chennai Short 11M, while Ankit Map's all-states version reached only 262K, which suggests one-place Shorts with a twist beat the list format. Lakshadweep has proven origin demand (prince.maps1k 1.2M) and a built-in guess the viewer can fail.
- **Risks:** Island count differs by source (atolls, reefs, islets, inhabited vs total), so show source and date on screen and verify first. Etymology and dates are from memory. Avoid religion, demography and 1947-style stories on Lakshadweep entirely (hard rule: neutral on communal topics). Post on 2 Nov, not 1 Nov, to avoid a pile-up with the 'Six Indian States Share One Birthday' Short. Use only names with documented official origins (no folk etymologies for Kerala, Gujarat, Maharashtra, Haryana or Chhattisgarh).
- **Effort:** low

### S029. Karnataka vs Maharashtra: Who Wins 5 Official Rounds?

- **Original title:** Karnataka vs Maharashtra: Who Wins 5 Rounds? Kadai Cup Match 1
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.3 (virality 4, appeal 6, distinct 5, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Kadai Cup
- **Template:** state vs state over five rounds (beat family: versus)
- **Hook:** Karnataka or Maharashtra? Five official numbers. You call each round before the reveal.
- **Outline:** 1) 0-3s: two state shapes, hook line, scoreboard 0-0. 2) Round 1, income per person 2024-25: Karnataka Rs 3,80,906 vs Maharashtra Rs 3,09,340 (3-2-1 pause, reveal). 3) Round 2, life expectancy 2019-23: 70.0 vs 72.8 years. 4) Round 3, rural construction wage per day 2024-25: Rs 484.8 vs Rs 407.9. 5) Round 4, villages with 5G (28 Feb 2026): 65.8% vs 69.8%. 6) Round 5, infant deaths per 1,000 (2023): 14 vs 14, stamped 'too close to call' (rule: a gap under the thin-lead threshold is a draw, so no state ever loses every round; the builder refuses a match where one side wins all five). Scoreboard 2-2 with one draw, each state's best round shown. 7) End card: a Round of 8 drawn from the eight most-searched states (Kerala, Tamil Nadu, Bihar, Maharashtra, Delhi, Karnataka, West Bengal, Rajasthan); pinned comment asks 'A or B for the next match'. Small template change: a bracket scoreboard end card and a draw rule.
- **Who shares it and why:** A Karnataka fan sends it to a Maharashtra friend with 'we took the money and wage rounds', and the friend answers with life expectancy and 5G.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Friendly rivalry plus participation. A Karnataka fan sends it to a Maharashtra friend with 'we took the money and wage rounds'; the other replies with the life-expectancy round. Comments: 'do Kerala vs Tamil Nadu next', 'wrong year', 'my state wins 3-2 on my numbers'. The share trigger is the scoreboard frame after round 5 showing both states with real wins and a 'too close to call' stamp, which reads as fair rather than as an attack. The pinned A-or-B comment turns viewers into voters, and a bracket gives a reason to return.
- **Why it could flop:** State-vs-state Shorts have a 5,100 median and 59% under 10K, and the one 1.9M outlier (Georvix) was a loaded 'which is better' question, not a scoreboard. The match is built to end 2-2 with one draw, so the promised 'who wins?' gets 'nobody', and viewers will say the five metrics were chosen to force a split (rural wage and 5G villages are obscure picks). Karnataka-Maharashtra is not among the typed search pairs, and it carries the Belagavi border dispute, so the comments will be about language and borders, not your numbers. Match 1 should be a pair people actually type, and the owner's own Tamil Nadu (116 suggestions, best long-form hit rate) is absent. The 5G round also depends on the hand-typed coverage table.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** T019 2024-25: Karnataka 380,906; Maharashtra 309,340 (Rs). T007 2019-23: 70.0 vs 72.8 years. T112 2024-25: Rs 484.8 vs Rs 407.9 a day. 5G villages: 65.8% vs 69.8%. T004 2023: 14 vs 14. Search suggestions: Kerala 179, Tamil Nadu 116, Bihar 59, Maharashtra 50, Delhi 46, Karnataka 46, West Bengal 43, Rajasthan 42.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States, tables T019 (income per person), T007 (life expectancy, SRS), T112 (rural wage, Labour Bureau), T004 (infant mortality); DoT 5G villages via Lok Sabha Starred Q 470, 25 Mar 2026, Annex II (public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json). Seeds from the search-demand counts in out/RESEARCH_DIGEST.md. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Rivalry is the strongest place hook but duels in Shorts are a lottery: 70 state-vs-state Shorts had a median of 5,100 views and 59% under 10K, yet Georvix 'Which State Is Better? Maharashtra or Karnataka' reached 1.9M against a 26K channel median. A fixed series gives a floor (Geo Study state series: 24 Shorts, median 982K, minimum 212K). Demand is typed: 'vs' appears in 1,151 YouTube suggestions, with 291 bare state pairs. In long-form, Angel One's two head-to-heads got 494K (61x) and 1.1M (136x) while its single-state profiles got 34K-55K. The viewer-picks-next loop is new and adds a reason to comment that plain duels lack.
- **Risks:** Duels have a low median on other channels (5,100), so judge the series on viewed-vs-swiped and comment rate, not on one match. Fairness rules are essential: every match must give each state at least one real win, use rates or per-person numbers (not totals), skip thin leads, and never use crime, religion, caste or party indicators. Gujarat is missing from the 2024-25 income and GSDP tables, so a Gujarat match needs another round set. Viewer voting can drift toward communal or hostile pairings: the pinned comment should offer two pre-vetted pairs rather than an open field. Seeds from search counts have ties (Delhi and Karnataka both 46). English narration is an unproven reach risk, so run one match as a text-led cut.
- **Effort:** medium

- **Why it could flop:** Plain state-vs-state Shorts have a 5,100 median, and Punjab and Haryana is the weakest place cluster in the long-form data (about 10% at 3x+, median 0.6x). The 3-3 tie is visibly engineered by picking six rounds from different years (2023, 2023-24, 2024-25), which a skeptical viewer will call out, and the topic invites Chandigarh, river-water and language fights that the script must refuse to touch. It also shares a date with S017.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Income per person 2024-25: Haryana ₹353,182 vs Punjab ₹221,197 (2011-12: 106,085 vs 85,577). Value of gross output 2023-24: Haryana 110,833,060 vs Punjab 40,754,266 ₹ lakh. Exports 2024-25: Haryana 2,493 vs Punjab 1,842 USD million. Wheat yield: Punjab 5,123 vs Haryana 4,774 kg/ha (India 3,595). Infant mortality 2023: Punjab 17, Haryana 26, India 25. Power per person 2024-25: Punjab 2,503.4, Haryana 2,293.5, India 1,208.2.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook tables under public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/: T019, T126, T181, T081, T004, T138 (name the original agencies from the table notes). Anniversary: Punjab Reorganisation Act, 1966 (India Code; verify the date). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Head-to-head by category is a measured winner (Dataverse 'India vs Russia' 913K against a 34K channel median) and loaded state questions can break out (Georvix 'Which State Is Better? Maharashtra or Karnataka' 1.9M against a 26K median). But plain state-vs-state Shorts have a median of only 5,100 (59% under 10K), so the shared-birthday story and the 1 November 2026 sixtieth anniversary are the differentiators.
- **Risks:** Stay away from Chandigarh, river water and any religion or language framing; use only 2011-12 onward economic numbers. Round selection can steer the result: say the verdict depends on the rounds and publish every candidate round in the chart post. Different years in different rounds: label each. Confirm 'turns 60' from the Act. Next pair Maharashtra and Gujarat cannot use income rounds from the RBI table (Gujarat has no GSDP or income after 2022-23); use exports (Gujarat 116,332 vs Maharashtra 65,855 USD million), factories, power and bank credit.
- **Effort:** low

### S041. UP Is a Top-4 Economy but Bottom-3 in Income Per Person

- **Original title:** UP's Economy Is Top 4, but Income Per Person Is Near the Bottom
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 4, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Template:** Breakdown (100-square grid), closing on the Mapped template
- **Hook:** Guess your state's slice of India's economy. Then compare it with income per person.
- **Outline:** 1) Hook over a 100-square grid for India's economy (financial year 2024-25). 2) Guess pause: which state fills the most squares? Reveal Maharashtra. 3) Which is bigger, Tamil Nadu or Uttar Pradesh? Tamil Nadu by 4.7% at current prices and 9.4% at constant prices (T021, T022). 4) The paradox: Uttar Pradesh is in the top four economies but has one of the lowest incomes per person, Rs 1,08,572 against Karnataka's Rs 3,80,906 (T019). 5) Never rank Uttar Pradesh against Karnataka: they are 0.6% apart at constant prices. 6) 'Your state's slice and your state's income per person' on the all-states map. Calendar: Fri 26 Feb 2027 (MoSPI: Second Advance Estimates 2026-27 and Q3, First Revised Estimates 2025-26, Final Estimates 2024-25, the same year as the state tables); ship within 24 hours of release; alternative slots 30 Nov 2026 (Q2) and 7 Jan 2027 (First Advance Estimates).
- **Who shares it and why:** Uttar Pradesh viewers share and defend it ('big, but per person is low') and Tamil Nadu and Maharashtra viewers share their rank, but the argument ends at 'population' so it rarely travels beyond Uttar Pradesh.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Economy-size pride and defence. Tamil Nadu and Maharashtra viewers share their rank; Uttar Pradesh viewers share the paradox ('big, but per person is low') and argue about what it means. Comments: 'per person vs total', 'where is Gujarat?', 'what about Bihar?'. The share trigger is the title's contrast of top-four economy and near-bottom income per person.
- **Why it could flop:** The 'paradox' dissolves at the first comment: Uttar Pradesh has the most people, so a big total with a low per-person figure is arithmetic, not a paradox. The title promise only arrives at beat 4, after a slow grid and a Tamil Nadu versus Uttar Pradesh side quest. Richest/poorest-state Shorts sit in the saturated lane (82% of search hits under 1,000 views). 'Top four' holds only at current prices among states with data, since Gujarat, Goa and Sikkim lack 2024-25 values, and Uttar Pradesh sits within 0.6% of Karnataka at constant prices. '2nd-lowest' needs the gap to the 3rd-lowest checked before it goes on screen, so use 'bottom 3' unless the gap is clear.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** T021 2024-25 current prices (Rs lakh): Maharashtra 453,151,842; Tamil Nadu 311,859,030; Uttar Pradesh 297,822,351; Karnataka 288,390,323; West Bengal 181,500,991. Gujarat's latest year in T021 is 2022-23 (220,341,897). T019 2024-25 (Rs): Karnataka 380,906; Uttar Pradesh 108,572 (second-lowest of the 25 states with data); Bihar 69,321.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States T021 and T022 (GSDP), T019 (income per person); national GDP for 2024-25 from the MoSPI press release of 26 Feb 2027 (mapped, not collected). Gujarat, Goa, Sikkim and others need their own state statistical releases. (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** Economy-size content has proven demand: Tamil Nadu economy videos (15 search results) have a median of 63K views and a max of 1.9M, 'richest states' videos reached 3.0M, and the data-paradox hook produced FactTechz's 4.6M Short. This one pairs the size ranking with a paradox and ties it to an official release day, when the topic is already in the news. The Breakdown template already exists.
- **Risks:** State GSDP does not sum to national GDP, so call shares approximate and cite both sources. Gujarat, Goa, Sikkim and several small states lack 2024-25 data in the RBI table, so claims are limited to the 25 states in it. Uttar Pradesh and Karnataka are 0.6% apart at constant prices (current prices 3.3%), so only 'top four' is safe. Current and constant prices rank some states differently. Keep wording neutral about Uttar Pradesh. Released at the national level, not state level, so the peg is a reason to post, not new state data.
- **Effort:** medium

### S036. Is Your State's Inflation Above or Below India's This Month?

- **Original title:** Inflation Day: Is Your State Above or Below India's Average?
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 3.8 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 6, fit 5, risk medium)
- **Series:** Inflation Day
- **Template:** Guess the State (with the Mapped template as the closing beat)
- **Hook:** Guess which state's prices rose the fastest this month.
- **Outline:** 1) Hook: three state options, 3-2-1. 2) Reveal the month's highest state with its rate against the all-India rate (from the new release). 3) All 36 states and UTs sweep in colour bands on the official-boundary map (Mapped). 4) 'Above or below India?' beat for the viewer's own state, with the lowest state shown. 5) Streak beat, only if the data support it (in 2025 Kerala was in the top three every month). 6) Next release date and 'Comment your state'. Dated runs: Mon 12 Oct 2026 (first, tomorrow), 12 Nov, 12 Dec, 12 Jan, 12 Feb and 12 Mar 2027 (if a holiday, the next working day).
- **Who shares it and why:** Malayali viewers send 'our prices' to relatives in the Gulf and other states, and low-inflation-state viewers send 'ours is negative'.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Price pride and price pain. Kerala viewers send 'our prices' to relatives in the Gulf and other states; viewers from low-inflation states send 'ours is negative'. Comments: 'which items?' (answer: the headline index does not say; the video points to item data), 'my city', 'rent'. The share trigger is the map with one state far above the rest and the 'above or below India?' line for each viewer's own state. The first ship on 12 Oct 2026 is a test run.
- **Why it could flop:** A 'guess the state with the fastest price rise' Short whose answer was Kerala in the top three all 12 months of 2025 is boringly predictable, and a monthly series turns a shock into a routine. A news-timed Short lives for one or two days and gets almost no long-tail browse, and a brand-new channel has no subscribers to give the 24-hour window an audience. The thin-lead rule bites repeatedly: Lakshadweep (8.31%) and Kerala (8.27%) were effectively level in November 2025, so 'highest' is often unclaimable. The pitch itself flags that MoSPI may have rebased the CPI, and a base-year change breaks the 'Kerala streak' and the baseline file. Nobody types 'state wise inflation' (zero suggestions), the headline index cannot say which items drove it, and the monthly API pipeline is mapped, not built, for a solo creator with 5-7 hours a week. Treat tomorrow's first run as a test, not a launch.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Dec 2025, combined, year on year: Kerala 9.49% (rural 10.77%, urban 7.13%); India 1.33%; Lakshadweep 7.02%; Goa 3.56%; Bihar -1.37%; Manipur -4.23%. Kerala ranked in the top three in all 12 months of 2025. In Nov 2025 Lakshadweep 8.31% and Kerala 8.27% were effectively level.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/mospi/cpi_general.json (MoSPI NSO CPI, base 2012, Jan-Dec 2025, rural, urban and combined) for the baseline; each new month from the MoSPI CPI press release or the same API (api.mospi.gov.in/api/cpi/getCPIIndex, mapped under 'MoSPI eSankhyiki API' in SOURCE_MAP). Calendar: MoSPI Advance Release Calendar 2026-27 (out/cal/mospi_arc.txt). (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** Cheap to run, repeatable and timely, and the state gap in 2025 was striking. Measured: inflation videos have reached 3.4M (a big creator), Kerala is the most-typed state in data searches (179 suggestions), and 'your state' hooks drew 7-12x the median comment rate. Caution from the same research: nobody types 'state wise inflation' (zero suggestions), so reach must come from Shorts feed and notifications, not search. A monthly slot also trains the audience to return on the 12th.
- **Risks:** Ship within 24 hours of release, ideally the same evening; a stale inflation Short is wasted. The local file is the base-2012 series ending Dec 2025: MoSPI may have moved to a new base year in 2026, so check the series and never splice across a break. The data show where, not why; do not guess causes or name items. Thin leads (for example Nov 2025: 0.04 point) need 'joint top' wording. Small UTs (Lakshadweep, Manipur) are volatile. No policy or party commentary. A monthly template can look like a factory, so vary the angle (rural vs urban, streak, biggest mover).
- **Effort:** medium

### SG03. Rajasthan Has the Most Cropland and Uses 150% of Its Groundwater

- **Original title:** Rajasthan in 60 Seconds: Most Cropland, 150% Groundwater Use
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 3.8 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 4, fit 6, risk medium)
- **Series:** [State] in 60 Seconds
- **Template:** new: fixed-layout state card (four panels: Leads, Trails, Paradox, Your State) on the official-boundary map, assembled from Mapped and Breakdown parts; one config file per state
- **Hook:** Rajasthan has more cropland than any other state. It also uses 150% of its yearly groundwater.
- **Outline:** Beat 1 (0-3s) Hook, map highlights Rajasthan. Beat 2 LEADS: net sown area 18,198 thousand hectares, 13.1% of India's 138,992 (2023-24, provisional); wool 16,014 thousand kg, 47.5% of India's output (2023-24); renewable grid capacity 26,693 MW, ahead of Gujarat's 25,472 MW (end-March 2024). Beat 3 TRAILS: coarse-cereal yield 1,485 kg per hectare against India's 2,482 (2024-25), 29th of 30 states reporting. Beat 4 PARADOX: groundwater extraction at 149.86% of the yearly extractable resource (2024), second only to Punjab's 156.87%; India 60.5%. Beat 5 SURPRISE: rice output 222.5 to 917.7 thousand tonnes (2012-13 to 2024-25), 4.1 times, yet still 0.6% of India's rice. Beat 6 Close: 'Where does YOUR state rank on groundwater? Comment it; the most-asked state goes next.' ROSTER: 36 episodes in this fixed layout (about 40 s each); first eight by typed demand (Delhi, West Bengal, Rajasthan, Maharashtra, Madhya Pradesh, Assam, J&K with Ladakh, one island UT). Each state needs its own hook, e.g. Delhi (highest income per person, 493,024 rupees, yet 18.5% of women in the labour force), Madhya Pradesh (largest forest cover, 77,073 sq km, yet the lowest rural construction wage of 20 states, 307.8 rupees a day), West Bengal (79.8% of India's jute and mesta, 2nd in fish).
- **Who shares it and why:** Rajasthanis share the 'Leads' panel with family and Punjab and Haryana viewers answer with their own groundwater numbers, but a four-panel 60-second card gives no single line to forward.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Identity and pride plus 'your state' personalisation. Rajasthanis share the Leads panel with family ('we are number one in this'); commenters from Punjab, Haryana and Gujarat answer with their own groundwater or solar numbers; the end card turns every comment into the next episode request. Likely comments: 'what about my state', 'do Punjab', 'water is the real issue'. Share trigger: the Leads and Trails panels side by side with the groundwater dial passing 100%.
- **Why it could flop:** The outline packs about ten numbers into four panels, which is the number-dense chart-Short shape that sits at a 6,200 median; the fixed-series floor (Geo Study 982K) was measured on Hindi satellite-map animations, not data cards. The one real hook, the cropland-versus-150%-groundwater paradox, is buried as beat 4 of 6 instead of being the whole Short, and 'State in 60 Seconds' is a known genre (Bharat Flip World, Geo Study) with Hindi narration. Pairing farmland with over-extraction invites blame comments even if the script avoids cause, and 36 near-identical cards are exactly the template-factory pattern.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Rajasthan: net sown area 18,198 of India 138,992 thousand ha (T054, 2023-24 p), next Maharashtra 16,571. Wool 16,014 of 33,689 thousand kg = 47.5% (T094, 2023-24). Renewable capacity 26,693 MW vs Gujarat 25,472 (T143, March 2024). Coarse-cereal yield 1,485 vs India 2,482 kg/ha (T082, 2024-25). Groundwater extraction 149.86% vs Punjab 156.87%, India 60.5% (T107, 2024). Rice 222.5 to 917.7 thousand tonnes, 2012-13 to 2024-25 (T058), India 150,184.3 in 2024-25. Roster extras: Delhi 493,024 rupees per person (T019, 2024-25); Delhi women's labour force 18.5% (PLFS 2023-24); Madhya Pradesh forest cover 77,073 sq km (T099, 2023); West Bengal 79.8% of jute and mesta (T066, 2024-25).
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook: T054 (net sown area), T094 (wool), T143 (renewable capacity), T082 (coarse-cereal yield), T107 (groundwater), T058 (rice); roster extras T019, T099, T112, T066, T093; PLFS in public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 2 (state roster). Measured: Geo Study's fixed state-by-state series has 24 Shorts, median 982K, floor 212K; Bharat Flip World's state explainers reach 1.2M to 1.8M; personalised 'your state' hooks draw 7 to 12 times the median comment rate; Rajasthan has 42 typed suggestions and no dedicated idea. The research caveat is that the measured series are Hindi map animations and low-profile states need their own hook, so each episode carries a data paradox rather than a profile.
- **Risks:** Groundwater stage of extraction is a CGWB assessment; over 100% means extraction exceeds the yearly extractable resource, say so once and do not blame farmers or governments or imply cause between farmland and water. The renewables lead over Gujarat is 4.8%, so no '#1' claim. 'Trails' is a fact with a source, never mockery. Land-use data are provisional. One hook per state or the roster reads as a template factory; the measured examples are Hindi, so English reach is unproven.
- **Effort:** medium

### S030. Gujarat's Rural Construction Wage Is Below India's. Guess 5 More

- **Original title:** Higher or Lower Than India? Guess 6 State Numbers
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 3.7 (virality 3, appeal 5, distinct 6, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Higher or Lower
- **Template:** new: Higher or Lower beat (a variant of the versus beat: an India card fixed on screen, one state card at a time with the number hidden, running score)
- **Hook:** India's rural construction worker earns Rs 441 a day. Is Gujarat higher or lower?
- **Outline:** 1) Hook: India card Rs 441.1 a day (men, construction, rural, 2024-25), Gujarat card with the number hidden. 2) Gujarat, 3-2-1 pause, reveal Rs 355.3: lower than India. 3) Kerala: reveal Rs 922.6, more than double India's. 4) Maharashtra: reveal Rs 407.9, lower. 5) Haryana Rs 534.9 and Andhra Pradesh Rs 553.9: both higher, with a note that Andhra Pradesh was Rs 237.5 in 2014-15. 6) Madhya Pradesh Rs 307.8, the lowest of the 20 states in the table. 7) Score card: 'Comment your score out of 6 and your state'; teaser for the next episode (a different table, same India-card format).
- **Who shares it and why:** People send 'I got 5 of 6' to friends and Gujaratis argue with the Gujarat card, but a quiz score is a weak reason to forward a Short.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Score-brag plus a surprising card. People send 'I got 5 of 6' to friends and argue about the one they missed. The share trigger is the Gujarat card: a state people assume is rich lands below India's average wage, and Gujarati and Maharashtrian viewers reply in the comments with other measures. Comments: 'my score', 'add my state', 'use a different year'. The 3-2-1 pause invites a rewatch to check the answer.
- **Why it could flop:** The pitch admits the evidence: 35 quiz-style Indian-state Shorts had a median of 63 views and a best of 31K. Four of the six cards are obvious (Kerala high, Madhya Pradesh low, Haryana and Andhra Pradesh above average), leaving one real surprise, Gujarat, and that is the only card worth the open. A Short that spends 40 seconds on six cards for one surprise will lose people before the Gujarat reveal or after it. The metric is obscure ('rural construction workers' wage quotations', with a derived India figure), so a cold viewer has no feel for Rs 441, and any Gujarat or Maharashtra commenter can dismiss a wage-sample figure as unrepresentative. 'Comment your score' brings low-value comments. Better as a single-card Short with the Gujarat paradox.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** T112 2024-25 (Rs a day): India 441.1; Kerala 922.6; Andhra Pradesh 553.9 (237.5 in 2014-15); Haryana 534.9; Maharashtra 407.9; Gujarat 355.3; Madhya Pradesh 307.8. Punjab 437.8 is within 1% of India and must not be asked.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States T112 (Average daily wage rates in rural India, men, construction workers; source Labour Bureau, Indian Labour Journal). Later episodes: T138 (power per person), T019 (income per person), T153. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Be honest about the evidence: quiz-style Indian-state Shorts are weak (35 Shorts on 31 channels, median 63 views, best 31K), so a bare quiz will not travel. The format is worth a small test only because each question hides a surprise (rich-looking Gujarat and Maharashtra sit below the national wage). Curiosity-gap titles reached 1M+ in 21.4% of Shorts against a 12.4% baseline, and clue-by-clue quizzes are 5 of Geo Facts' top 10 (0.7M-1.6M each, global evidence). Hooks that make people argue drew 7-12x the median comment rate (0.09%-0.15% against 0.013%).
- **Risks:** No Indian precedent for quiz Shorts; test five episodes and stop if viewed-vs-swiped is poor. T112 is a sample of wage quotations, and the India figure is derived by dividing total wages by quotations (RBI note), so describe it as wage rates, not average earnings. Avoid thin pairs (Punjab). Do not extend Gujarat questions to income, since Gujarat is missing from the 2024-25 income tables. A repeated card format can look like a template factory, so cap it at two episodes a week and change the table each time.
- **Effort:** medium

### S033. Which Low-Income State Has 5G in 95.6% of Its Villages?

- **Original title:** Guess the State: Low Income, Yet 5G Reaches 95.6% of Villages
- **Status:** Not shortlisted: high risk. **Score** 3.7 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 7, fit 8, risk high)
- **Series:** Five Clues
- **Template:** 5-clue quiz (beat family: clues)
- **Hook:** Lowest income per person in the table. 5G in 95.6% of its villages. Which state?
- **Outline:** 1) Hook with the two numbers and a blank India map. 2) Clue 1: income per person is the lowest of the 25 states with 2024-25 data, Rs 69,321. 3) Clue 2: 5G has reached 57.0% of India's villages; this state is at 95.6%. 4) Clue 3: it is ahead of Karnataka (65.8%) and Tamil Nadu (56.9%). 5) Clue 4: the second-highest income per person in the table, Telangana at Rs 3,87,623, has 5G in only 19.1% of villages. 6) 3-2-1, reveal Bihar: 42,902 of 44,888 villages. 7) Close on the all-states map in 5G bands and the line 'Coverage is not use: this counts villages where the service has been brought'; 'Where does your state sit against 57.0%?'
- **Who shares it and why:** Bihari viewers and the diaspora forward a rare good-news number to relatives, while Telangana viewers dispute 19.1% in the comments.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Pride plus paradox. Bihari viewers and the diaspora forward a rare good-news number to relatives; Telangana and Hyderabad viewers dispute the 19.1% in the comments ('check the operator map'), which drives replies. The share trigger is 95.6% beside 19.1% with the income numbers underneath. Comments: 'coverage is not speed', 'where is my district', 'add 4G'.
- **Why it could flop:** The whole Short rests on a hand-typed table, and the pitch itself says Telangana's 19.1% 'looks unusual' (as do Odisha's 5.9% and Meghalaya's 4.8% next to Bihar's 95.6%). Extreme values are where transcription errors and definitional quirks hide, and a wrong headline number on a day-one channel is hard to recover from; do not publish until the Lok Sabha annexure is re-read. The quiz structure also wastes the surprise: 'lowest income per person' makes most viewers say Bihar in clue 1, so lead with the 5G number as a plain paradox. The Bihar internet precedent (4.6M in 9 days) was a different claim, usage versus penetration, so it supports the paradox type but not this number. 'Lowest income' is only true among the 25 states with 2024-25 data. 'Coverage is not use' will be the top comment.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** 5G villages as on 28 Feb 2026: India 57.0% (367,156 of 644,131); Bihar 95.6% (42,902 of 44,888); Delhi and Puducherry 100%; Haryana 96.8%; Kerala 94.4%; Karnataka 65.8%; Tamil Nadu 56.9%; Telangana 19.1% (1,945 of 10,203); Odisha 5.9%; Meghalaya 4.8%. T019 2024-25: Bihar Rs 69,321; Telangana Rs 387,623.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json (Lok Sabha Starred Question 470, answered 25 March 2026, Annex II, Department of Telecommunications; villages per Registrar General of India) and RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States T019. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** It uses the one hook with a single large Indian precedent: FactTechz's 'Bihar is No. 1 in Terms of DATA (Internet Usage) While Having Lowest Penetration' reached 4.6M views in 9 days. State-data paradoxes are one of the five hook types behind the 1M+ state Shorts. It also gives Bihar a positive number, in a topic area (Bihar long-form) that otherwise under-performs, and the closing map supports the 'your state' comment spike (7-12x the median).
- **Risks:** The coverage table was typed by hand from the PDF (column sums checked); re-read the Lok Sabha annexure and the 31 Jan 2026 snapshot before publishing, especially Telangana's 19.1%, which looks unusual. '5G villages' means service has been brought, not that residents use it. 'Lowest income' holds only among the 25 states with 2024-25 data (Gujarat, Goa, Sikkim and others are absent). Do not add the phones-per-100 table (T149): Bihar's figure includes Jharkhand's telecom circle and its 2025 column runs only to 30 Nov 2024. English narration is unproven; consider a text-led cut.
- **Effort:** low
