Honest caution: there is no measured precedent for parliamentary-answer Shorts, so this is a test of the 'Asked in Parliament' series; 4,008 answers with state tables exist to feed it.
- **Risks:** Numbers are unknown until the PDF is read; the 17-state table may be partial or in kilometres rather than counts. Telecom black spots are different from road-accident black spots (several MoRTH answers use the same word), so say 'telecom' in the first frame. Quote the ministry's definition and 'as on' date exactly; do not rank states as better or worse. Keep clear of blame on any company or government. PDF reading can introduce errors: transcribe and cross-check totals as was done for the 5G file.
- **Effort:** medium

### S040. Which Country Had as Many People as Your State? Census 2011

- **Original title:** Which Country Has as Many People as Your State?
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 3.5 (virality 4, appeal 6, distinct 3, fit 4, risk medium)
- **Series:** State = Country
- **Template:** new: State = Country card (a state shape beside three country outlines, a variant of the Face-off template)
- **Hook:** Which country has as many people as Uttar Pradesh?
- **Outline:** 1) Hook: the state shape with three country-outline options, 3-2-1. 2) Reveal Uttar Pradesh's matching country and the two numbers. 3) Punjab, same format. 4) Goa, same format. 5) Uttarakhand, same format. 6) 'Where does your state sit? Comment it and the next card is yours' with a map of all states. 7) Calendar note: these four are the first states to be counted: self-enumeration 16-30 Nov 2026, house-to-house 1 Dec 2026 to 4 Jan 2027, reference date 5 Jan 2027 (revision 5-9 Jan). Compare the same year only (Census 2011 against UN estimates for 2011), and say that 2011 is the last full count.
- **Who shares it and why:** Viewers post 'my state = [country]' in college and family groups and ask for their own state next, but that only works when the matched country is a surprise, and for Uttar Pradesh it largely is not.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** State pride and meme-sharing. 'My state = [country]' is posted in college and family groups, and each viewer's follow-up is 'do mine'. Comments: 'what about 2027?', 'Maharashtra next', 'Bihar?'. The share trigger is the state shape beside the country outline at the same scale.
- **Why it could flop:** 'This state has a country's worth of people' is an old factoid, so the reveal surprises few. The juiciest matches (neighbouring countries) are exactly the ones the border and no-mockery rules forbid. The data is Census 2011, fifteen years old, while the Census 2027 count is starting, so 'this is outdated' becomes the top comment. Flat country-comparison Shorts sit at 15K-41K (Data Duck, Dataverse), and the 'state vs Pakistan' series saturated from 135K down to 6.7K. Effort is high: two datasets not yet collected, country outlines, a new template. Four rounds in one Short also runs long. The date hook is weak (past Census 2027 videos: median 29K).
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** 
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** Census of India state totals, 2011 (mapped in SOURCE_MAP, not collected); country populations for the same year from UN World Population Prospects or World Bank Open Data (mapped, not collected). Dates from the Gazette of India notification S.O. 4729(E), 5 Sep 2026 (out/cal/census_gazette.txt). (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** The 'state equals a country' frame is measured to travel: Map Motion 'This Indian State Has a Country's Population' 1.6M and 'Top 5 Most Populated States' 1.3M (population tag: 5 of 39 Shorts at 1M+); ibx2cat's state-GDP-beats-countries 1.0M. The date itself does not carry the idea: past Census 2027 videos have a median of only 29K, so the state-equals-country meme must lead and the date is a reason to post now. It also sets up 'your state' as the closing comment prompt.
- **Risks:** Needs two datasets and country outlines from a public-domain source; draw the India map only on the official boundary and never show India's border beside neighbouring countries. Compare the same year only, or label projections with their source. 2011 numbers are old and will be replaced when the new count is published. The gazette covers only Goa, Uttar Pradesh, Punjab and non-snow-bound Uttarakhand; the main all-India phases are unconfirmed in our notes, so do not say 'India counts' for the whole country. Stay off Census questions on caste and religion entirely. Ship by 15 Nov 2026 (36 days away), with follow-ups on 1 Dec and 5 Jan.
- **Effort:** high

### SG10. School Dropouts, State by State: What Parliament Was Told

- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 3.1 (virality 3, appeal 4, distinct 8, fit 4, risk medium)
- **Series:** Parliament Was Told
- **Template:** Mapped
- **Hook:** Parliament was asked how many students leave school at each stage. Here is your state's answer.
- **Outline:** Beat 1 (0-3s) Hook with the source stamp: Lok Sabha, Ministry of Education, Unstarred Question 3603, 10 Aug 2026. Beat 2 Mapped: every state and UT in the answer coloured in bands (28 states/UTs in the table; ones not in the answer greyed and labelled 'not in the answer'). Beat 3 Highest three and lowest three, with the stage (primary, upper primary, secondary) and year exactly as the table states. Beat 4 One honest line on what the answer does not say (definition, reference year). Beat 5 Your-state card. Beat 6 Close: 'Next week: doctor vacancies, anganwadi centres or road safety. Vote in the comments.' WEEKLY PIPELINE (about 1 hour each, batch three at a time): pick from the index by everyday keyword, read the PDF, check the table against the answer text, fill a Mapped config, post with the PDF link in the description. Queue from the index: school closures (U3669, 10.08.2026, 36 states/UTs), student dropouts at transition stages (U2465, 03.08.2026, 8 tables), doctor vacancies (U3449, 07.08.2026, 17 states), road accidents (U1939, 30.07.2026, 27 states), anganwadi services (U1011, 24.07.2026, 36 states/UTs), MSME schemes (U3180, 06.08.2026, 29 states), Startup India (U1446, 28.07.2026, 32 states).
- **Who shares it and why:** Parents and teachers in school and coaching groups might forward their own state's figure, but no surprise is known until the PDF is read, so there is no real trigger yet.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** 'Your state' personalisation plus authority (question number and date on screen) and a strong but fair question. Shared by parents, teachers and exam aspirants in school and coaching groups. Likely comments: 'my state's figure differs on the ground', 'which stage?', and topic requests for next week. Share trigger: the stamp 'Lok Sabha, Unstarred Question 3603, 10 Aug 2026' appearing over the map, then the viewer's state lighting up.
- **Why it could flop:** The answer's numbers are unknown, so the video may have no twist at all; the PDFs are not in our folder and the table covers only 28 states and UTs, possibly with mixed years and definitions. School dropout is a downbeat topic, education Shorts under-index (lift 0.73), and a state ranking of ministry figures invites party-political comment even with neutral wording. The question-number stamp builds trust but is not a reason to share, and a weekly one-hour pipeline depends on reading and checking a new PDF each time, which breaks the small time budget when a table is messy.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Index metadata only: ls18-s8-U3603, 10.08.2026, 1 table, 28 states/UTs; ls18-s8-U2465, 03.08.2026, 8 tables, 28 states/UTs. The dropout values themselves are unknown until the PDFs are read.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/loksabha/index.json: ls18-s8-U3603 (Dropout Rate of Students at various Level of Schools, Education, 10.08.2026) and ls18-s8-U2465 (Student Dropouts during Transition Stages, 03.08.2026); the PDF links are in the index but the PDFs are not in our folder, so an answer is a lead until read. (answer PDF still to read)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 6 (Parliament was told). The 4,008 Lok Sabha answers in the index are the channel's one unique primary-source asset, yet only S011 and L069 (virality 3 and 4) use them; the series fits 'serve it hot' and edtech and insurance sponsors. The index counts everyday topics: MSME 54 answers, anganwadi 34, Startup India 31, road accidents 23. Measured: personalised 'your state' hooks draw 7 to 12 times the median comment rate, and primary-source citation is the only differentiator against chart channels (Visual Capitalist Shorts median 2,300). Caveat: health and education Shorts under-index (lift 0.73), so the story must open on the viewer's state, not on the policy.
- **Risks:** Numbers unknown until read; the table may cover 28 of 36 states, mix reference years or define dropout differently by stage, so say what is and is not in the answer. Ranking states by ministry figures can still invite party-political comment: no parties, leaders or elections on screen, and moderate. A weekly cadence is heavy on 5-7 hours a week, so batch. Do not reuse topics already planned (highway phone black spots, taps without water).
- **Effort:** medium

### S038. Budget 2027: Of Every 100 Rupees, How Many Go to Interest?

- **Original title:** Budget 2027 Quiz: Guess Where Each 100 Rupees Goes
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 2.9 (virality 3, appeal 5, distinct 2, fit 5, risk medium)
- **Series:** Budget Day
- **Template:** Breakdown (100-square grid)
- **Hook:** You pay it. Can you guess where each 100 rupees of government money goes?
- **Outline:** 1) Hook over a blank 100-square grid. 2) Guess 1: 'Where does the 100 rupees come from?' (four options), reveal with the Budget's own rupee-comes-from figures. 3) Guess 2: 'How many of the 100 go to interest payments?' (pause), reveal. 4) Guess 3: 'How many go to states?' reveal. 5) Whole grid coloured in with every slice labelled and its source page. 6) Close: 'Which slice surprised you? Comment your score.' Pre-build the grid and quiz questions in January with last year's figures marked TEST; pour in the new numbers after the speech.
- **Who shares it and why:** Colleagues and classmates may dare each other to guess the interest-payments slice on Budget day, but every outlet posts the same rupee chart, so there is no reason to share ours over another.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Office, classroom and family chats on Budget day: 'guess before you scroll'. Comments are scores ('I said 10, it was 20'), complaints and 'what about my tax'. The share trigger is the grid with the one slice most viewers underestimated. It is positioned as the first-to-publish checkable Short, before the TV recaps.
- **Why it could flop:** Budget-day 'where your rupee goes' is the most crowded Short of the year: TV channels and large finance creators post it within hours, and a zero-subscriber channel posting 3-6 hours late into a cold feed gets none of that surge. The quiz layer adds nothing shareable (quiz Shorts show no lift, median lift 1.01). The numbers are mapped, not collected, and the plan depends on a solo creator at 5-7 hours a week turning around accurate figures within hours of the speech. One misread slice would damage the 'from official sources' trust position, and the comments will drift to 'what about my tax' and party blame. The Budget's rupee chart is itself assumed to exist.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** 
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** Union Budget documents on indiabudget.gov.in (Budget at a Glance, receipts and expenditure; mapped in SOURCE_MAP, not collected) and the Economic Survey. The rupee comes-from and goes-to chart is assumed to exist as in past years (confirm on the day). (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** Budget explainers are a proven topic (15 search results: median 75K views, max 906K; Economic Survey median 56K), but they are long and crowded. The edge is speed and a quiz the viewer can fail, not another explainer; the Breakdown (100-square grid) template already exists, and a personal-stake question ('where does your money go') suits the 'your' hook family that drew 7-12x median comments.
- **Risks:** Date: Union Budget 1 Feb 2027 (Monday) with the Economic Survey the day before are known fixtures not re-verified today. Ship within about 3-6 hours of the speech and no later than that evening (assumption: late recaps lose the audience). Large channels dominate Budget day, so this must be a quiz, not an explainer. Numbers must be copied from the Budget documents and checked twice; keep a check script. No tax advice, no investing advice (SEBI rule), no party or election framing. Do not state any number until the document is out.
- **Effort:** medium

## Long-form: every idea in full

### L062. Bihar 96%, Meghalaya 5%: India's 5G Village Map

- **Original title:** India's 5G Map: 96% of Bihar's Villages, 5% of Meghalaya's
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 7.1 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 7, fit 8, risk low)
- **Series:** The Long Series
- **Template:** new: Long Series documentary on Long Kit v1 using the existing Mapped logic at 16:9 (every state numbered on the official map, text never on the map), a three-measure 'coverage, ownership, use' strip, and a village-dot scale card ('1 in 5 villages in Arunachal Pradesh').
- **Hook:** 5G has reached 57% of India's villages. In Bihar, 96%. In Meghalaya, 5%.
- **Outline:** Target 14 min. Ch1 The 5G map (2.5 min): villages where 5G services have been brought, 367,156 of 644,131, by state (DoT via Lok Sabha Starred Q470, 28 Feb 2026). Ch2 The extremes (2 min): Bihar 95.6%, Haryana 96.8%, Punjab 93.1% vs Meghalaya 4.8%, Odisha 5.9%, Andaman and Nicobar 6.3%, Mizoram 8.5%. Ch3 Twenty years of phones (2 min): phones per 100 people, India 7.1 in 2004 to 84.36 in 2025 (T149, by telecom circle). Ch4 Coverage is not use (3 min): women who have used the internet, India 33.3% to 64.3%, Bihar 20.6% to 58.4%; Karnataka 57.9% (31st of 35), Kerala 87.3%; women with their own mobile, India 53.9% to 63.6% (NFHS-5 to NFHS-6). Ch5 The last mile (2.5 min): 8,748 villages with no mobile signal; Arunachal Pradesh 1,176 of 5,993; five states hold 4,903. Ch6 What 5G coverage does and does not measure (1 min). Ch7 Pause-and-guess: where is your state on all three measures. Cut three Shorts: 'Bihar 96%', 'Karnataka 31st', 'Arunachal 1 in 5'.
- **Who shares it and why:** Biharis send the 96% to friends who mock their state, while Odia and Meghalayan viewers send the 5% as 'why is ours so low?'.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** State-data paradox plus identity. A Bihari viewer shares it as pride ('96%, ahead of Gujarat and Tamil Nadu'), an Odia or Meghalayan viewer shares it as a question ('why is ours 5%?'), and both land in the comments. Expected comments: 'coverage of villages is not coverage of people', 'my village has no signal', 'NFHS is survey, not network data'. Share trigger: the split frame of Bihar 96% against Meghalaya 5%.
- **Why it could flop:** This is the one idea built on the measured state-data paradox (FactTechz Bihar internet, 4.6M in 9 days), but that hook is a Short-length reveal stretched to 14 minutes. Phones per 100 by telecom circle (Delhi 277) and NFHS women's internet use dilute it into a development tour. Counting villages treats a 100-person hamlet like a large village, so the top comment will be 'coverage of villages is not coverage of people'. Bihar is not even the top of the table (Haryana 96.8%, Delhi and Puducherry 100%), so the 'Bihar wins' framing is selective. Long-form Bihar videos hit 3x only 17% of the time (n=6). The idea works better as a Short first, then a long video if the Short travels.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Villages with 5G (28 Feb 2026): India 367,156 of 644,131 (57.0%); Bihar 42,902 of 44,888 (95.6%); Meghalaya 341 of 7,100 (4.8%); Odisha 5.9%; Haryana 96.8%; Punjab 93.1%; Delhi and Puducherry 100%. Villages without any mobile signal: 8,748 in total; Odisha 1,177, Arunachal Pradesh 1,176 of 5,993, Madhya Pradesh 930, Maharashtra 868, Chhattisgarh 752. Phones per 100 people (T149, end-March): India 7.1 (2004), 84.36 (2025); Bihar 56.27; Delhi 277. Women who have used the internet (NFHS-5 to NFHS-6): India 33.3% to 64.3%; Bihar 20.6% to 58.4%; Karnataka 57.9%; Kerala 87.3%. Women with their own mobile phone: India 53.9% to 63.6%.
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. All DoT figures recompute from mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json (India 367,156 of 644,131 = 57.0%; Bihar 42,902 of 44,888 = 95.6%; Meghalaya 341 of 7,100 = 4.8%; Odisha 5.9%, A and N 6.3%, Mizoram 8.5%, Haryana 96.8%, Punjab 93.1%; 8,748 uncovered; sums tie to the published total row), and NFHS-6 matches (women online India 33.3 to 64.3, Bihar 20.6 to 58.4, Karnataka 57.9 = 31st of 35, Kerala 87.3; own phone India 53.9 to 63.6, Bihar 62.8). RBI T149's column '2025 #' is data to 30 Nov 2024, so 84.36 is a Nov 2024 figure, not 2025, and Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand, Uttarakhand and A and N are missing.
- **Corrected numbers:** T149: India 84.36 phones per 100 is as of 30 Nov 2024 (column labelled 2025); 7.1 is end-March 2004; Bihar 56.27 and Delhi 277.0 are the same Nov 2024 point.
- **Source:** public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json (DoT, Lok Sabha Starred Q470 of 25 Mar 2026; earlier snapshot 31 Jan 2026 from Unstarred Q3122); RBI Handbook T149; nfhs6_states.json indicators 14, 15, 93; NPCI UPI statistics (SOURCE_MAP, browser only) for an optional payments chapter. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Launch slot 7. The state-data paradox is the cleanest repeatable hook in the Shorts research: FactTechz's Bihar internet Short reached 4.6M in 9 days (research_shorts_india.md). Odisha, Meghalaya and the Northeast are named in the lowest 5G numbers, which makes people in those states curious about their own place. The Bihar-vs-others contrast is the sort of fact people forward with 'why?'.
- **Risks:** Do not title it as 'Bihar has the fewest phones': T149 is by telecom service area (Bihar's row may include Jharkhand, Uttar Pradesh and the Northeast are not in it, and 2025 is missing for four states), and NFHS-6 shows Bihar's women's phone ownership (62.8%) close to India's (63.6%). Explain that '5G in a village' counts villages, not people or square kilometres. Bihar is not the top state (Delhi, Puducherry, Dadra, Haryana are higher). Delhi's 277 phones per 100 is a circle effect and must carry a footnote. NFHS fieldwork period is 2023-24 while DoT is Feb 2026.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. A Short-length reveal stretched to 14 minutes on Bihar, whose long-form record is weak (17% of 6 videos at 3x), so the one proven hook loses its punch.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. The DoT facts recompute and a 14-minute length allows the caveats, but fix 84.36 phones per 100 (a Nov 2024 tele-density of connections by telecom circle, not people, with Bihar's circle possibly including Jharkhand), confirm the NFHS-5 to NFHS-6 internet question is comparable, and re-check the typed DoT table against the PDF.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. It is a Short-length reveal stretched to 14 minutes, a 'why is ours 5%?' chapter lists Meghalaya, Mizoram and Arunachal as the laggards, and an Assam viewer would see the Northeast as the butt.

### L056. South vs North India: 12 Official Numbers, Zone by Zone

- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 7 (virality 7, appeal 8, distinct 5, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Rivals
- **Template:** new: Scoreboard Duel 16:9 on a shared Long Kit v1 (reuse StateMap.tsx on the official boundary; ChapterCard; SourceFooter that keeps publisher, table, period and unit on screen; RankDots for all 35 states; PauseGuess card). build_long.py reads one JSON config of rounds [{source, column, label, higher_is_better}], computes leaders, ties and ranks itself, and prints 'level' whenever a lead is under 1 point or about 3 percent, so the tally cannot be edited by hand.
- **Hook:** Everyone says the South is ahead. We scored India's six official zones on 12 numbers, and no zone won them all.
- **Outline:** Target 16 min. Ch1 Who picks the teams (1.5 min): India's six zone groupings from the Zonal Councils and the North East Council, typed from the Home Ministry list, so nobody cherry-picks. Ch2 Money, rounds 1-3 (3 min): income per person 2022-23 (RBI T019), credit-deposit ratio 2025 (T153), unemployment rate 2025 (PLFS). Ch3 Lives, rounds 4-7 (3 min): infant mortality 2023 (T004); stunted children, fully vaccinated children and homes with health insurance (NFHS-6); life expectancy 2019-23 (T007) as an unscored context card. Ch4 Women, rounds 8-11 (3.5 min): women in the labour force 2023-24 (PLFS), women paid in cash for work, women who have used the internet, women with their own mobile (NFHS-6). Ch5 Connected, round 12 (1.5 min): villages with 5G (DoT, 28 Feb 2026). Ch6 The surprise cards (1.5 min): Karnataka 31st of 35 for women online, Telangana 1st for women paid in cash. Ch7 Scoreboard (1.5 min): wins, ties and 'level' calls per zone, then a dot for every state so each viewer finds their own. Ch8 Your state, pause-and-guess close (0.5 min). Cut three Shorts from ch2, ch5 and ch6.
- **Who shares it and why:** A Chennai or Hyderabad colleague sends it to a Lucknow or Jaipur colleague with 'we lead on these', the reply cites the rounds the South lost, and Northeast and Eastern viewers share because they finally appear on the board.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Friendly rivalry plus identity and pride, with a surprise that overturns a belief (the South does not win every round). A Chennai or Hyderabad viewer sends it to a Lucknow or Jaipur colleague in the office group, and the reverse; Northeast and Eastern viewers share because they finally appear on the board. Comments to expect: 'UP is Central, not North?', 'Karnataka 31st? Bengaluru is full of women online', 'where is my state?'. Share trigger: the six-row scoreboard frame with a coloured dot for every state, and the Karnataka 31st-of-35 card.
- **Why it could flop:** Six zones water down the two-sided rivalry that made North-vs-South the strongest bucket (63% at 3x+), and 'no zone won them all' removes the winner viewers want. The zone typing (Uttar Pradesh and Bihar are not in the Northern zone) starts an argument about method instead of the numbers, and zone medians over 1 to 4 states are fragile. The measured hits were opinionated presenter-led takes ('South is 100x better') that our neutral rules forbid, so we may land nearer the 1x to 2.6x that big channels got. It also needs the heaviest engine.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Women who have used the internet (NFHS-6, 2023-24): Kerala 87.3%, Karnataka 57.9% (31st of 35 states/UTs), India 64.3%. Women paid in cash for work: Telangana 53.2% (1st), Andhra Pradesh 48.0%, Tamil Nadu 48.0%, India 30.8%. Villages with 5G (DoT, 28 Feb 2026): India 57.0%, Bihar 95.6%, Meghalaya 4.8%, Odisha 5.9%. Infant mortality 2023 (T004): Kerala 5, Madhya Pradesh 37, Uttar Pradesh 37, Chhattisgarh 37 per thousand. Unemployment rate 2025 (PLFS): India 3.1%, Gujarat 0.9%, Goa 8.3%. Life expectancy 2019-23 (T007): Kerala 75.1, Chhattisgarh 64.6 years.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Every key number recomputes from nfhs6_states.json (Kerala 87.3 internet, Karnataka 57.9 = 31st of 35, Telangana 53.2 cash pay = 1st by 5.2 points, AP and TN 48.0, India 30.8), RBI T004/T007 (IMR Kerala 5 vs 37 for MP, UP, Chhattisgarh; life expectancy 75.1 vs 64.6), plfs_annual.json (UR 2025 India 3.1, Gujarat 0.9, Goa 8.3) and the coverage file. Using standard Zonal Council and North East Council membership (typed from memory, not the MHA list) and my reconstruction of the 12 rounds (outline truncated), unweighted zone medians give the South 5 wins of 12 and no zone wins all; but the credit-deposit round (T153) has no clear 'better' direction, and Manipur, Tripura and DNH and DD are missing from some tables.
- **Source:** public/datayt/nfhs6/nfhs6_states.json (indicators 7, 14, 44, 69, 91, 93); RBI Handbook T004, T007, T019 (column 2022-23), T153; public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (LFPR female 2023-24, UR 2025); public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json (Lok Sabha Starred Q470, DoT). Zone membership: Zonal Councils and North East Council, Ministry of Home Affairs / Inter-State Council Secretariat (about 40 lines to type and check). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Launch slot 1 of the first ten long-forms. North-vs-South is the strongest measured bucket in out/research_long_india.md: 19 videos on 8 channels, 63% reached 3x their channel median, median 4.4x, and channels of 5K to 28K subscribers got 380K to 1.1M views. Southern states over-index (Tamil Nadu 75% at 3x+ on 16 videos). Searchers type 'north india vs south india development' and 'what if south india became a country'. The official-zone design lets us make the same video under our neutrality rule, and the loud versions ('100 times better') are banned for us.
- **Risks:** Zone membership must be checked against the Home Ministry list; UP, Bihar and Uttarakhand are not in the Northern zone, which will start arguments, so show the by-state dot plot to defuse it. Zone medians are unweighted and some zones have only 1 to 4 reporting states per round; the builder must print n on screen. Income year 2022-23 is used because Gujarat, Goa and Sikkim have no 2024-25 figure. Delimitation politics: no seats, parties or votes, no 'better' wording; South and West income medians are within about 3%, so call that level. The measured North-vs-South hits were presenter-led and this is faceless with an English voice (most viral Indian state videos are Hindi), so the opening must carry the tension.
- **Effort:** high
- **Red team (growth):** drop. Six zones dilute the two-sided rivalry that makes North vs South work, 'no zone won them all' withholds the verdict viewers want, and it is the highest-effort engine build.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. Zone crowns rest on unweighted medians of 1-4 states, zone membership typed from memory, mixed periods, and value-laden 'winners' like unemployment and credit-deposit ratio, and the sibling L012 recompute shows the women's labour-force winner flips between 2023-24 and 2025.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. Six zones with 'no zone won them all' waters down the North-vs-South fight people click for, and typing UP into 'Central' will start arguments; if one North-vs-South video is made it should be L012.

### L063. Telangana vs Andhra, Jharkhand vs Bihar: The 4 States That Split

- **Original title:** India Split 4 States. How Do Both Halves Compare Today?
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.6 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 6, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Template:** new: Twin-State Scoreboard 16:9, the Scoreboard Duel engine with a 'split date' timeline strip and a gap-over-time line (child state divided by parent state) for each pair.
- **Hook:** Jharkhand earns 1.7 times more per person than Bihar. But Bihar has fewer infant deaths: 23 per thousand to 29.
- **Outline:** Target 13 min. Ch1 How the four splits happened (2 min): Chhattisgarh from Madhya Pradesh, Uttarakhand from Uttar Pradesh, Jharkhand from Bihar (2000), Telangana from Andhra Pradesh (2014); dates and Acts cited from the Reorganisation Acts, official boundary map. Ch2 Income, round 1 (2.5 min): income per person 2011-12 vs 2024-25 and the ratio of child to parent: Uttarakhand/UP 3.07x to 2.52x, Chhattisgarh/MP 1.43x to 1.07x, Jharkhand/Bihar 1.90x to 1.68x, Telangana/AP 1.32x to 1.46x (T019). Ch3 Babies, round 2 (2.5 min): infant mortality 2004 to 2023 (T004). Ch4 Women, round 3 (2 min): women in the labour force 2017-18 to 2023-24 (PLFS). Ch5 Families, round 4 (1.5 min): children per woman 2023 (T006). Ch6 What the numbers cannot say (1 min): they do not show that a split caused any of it. Ch7 Your pair: pause-and-guess which half is ahead before each reveal (1.5 min).
- **Who shares it and why:** Residents of each parent and child state send their pair's card to the friend in the other half to settle who did better after the split.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** How a place came to be, plus friendly rivalry between parent and child. Residents of all eight states have a stake; a Jharkhand viewer shares the income card, a Bihar viewer shares the infant-mortality card, a Chhattisgarh and Madhya Pradesh pair argue who caught up. Expected comments: 'splitting was worth it', 'it was the same people', 'Telangana vs Andhra: Hyderabad effect'. Share trigger: the four-pair gap-over-time lines on one screen and the Bihar 23 vs Jharkhand 29 card.
- **Why it could flop:** Four pairs times four measures makes a tour with no verdict. Three of the four splits happened in 2000 and the data starts in 2004 or 2011-12, so the video cannot answer 'was the split worth it', which is exactly what the title and the comments will ask. Telangana and Andhra Pradesh 2011-12 figures are a back-cast, and the 2014 split is still politically live. Only the Telangana-Andhra pair has measured pull (Angel One's duel did 494K, 61x on a 28K-sub channel). Chhattisgarh-Madhya Pradesh and Jharkhand-Bihar are low-profile pairs, and mid-profile duels have fallen below 0.5x elsewhere. The hook's numbers check out (116,663 / 69,321 = 1.68), but write '1.7 times as much', not 'more'.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Income per person, current prices (T019), 2011-12 to 2024-25: Uttar Pradesh 32,648 to 1,08,572; Uttarakhand 1,00,314 to 2,74,064; Madhya Pradesh 38,497 to 1,52,615; Chhattisgarh 55,177 to 1,62,870; Bihar 21,750 to 69,321; Jharkhand 41,254 to 1,16,663; Andhra Pradesh 69,000 to 2,66,240; Telangana 91,121 to 3,87,623. Infant mortality (T004), 2004 to 2023: Uttar Pradesh 72 to 37, Uttarakhand 42 to 20; Madhya Pradesh 79 to 37, Chhattisgarh 60 to 37; Bihar 61 to 23, Jharkhand 49 to 29; Andhra Pradesh 19 and Telangana 18 in 2023. Children per woman 2023 (T006): Uttar Pradesh 2.6, Uttarakhand 1.7; Madhya Pradesh 2.4, Chhattisgarh 2.2; Bihar 2.8, Jharkhand 2.1; both Andhra Pradesh and Telangana 1.5. Women in the labour force 2023-24: Bihar 30.5, Jharkhand 49.8; Chhattisgarh 59.5, Madhya Pradesh 52.3.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from T019, T004, T006 and plfs_annual.json: the four child/parent ratios (3.07x to 2.52x, 1.43x to 1.07x, 1.90x to 1.68x, 1.32x to 1.46x) and all infant mortality, fertility and women's LFPR figures match. Cautions: Bihar 23 vs Jharkhand 29 is a 2023-only reversal (Jharkhand was 25 vs Bihar 26-27 in 2020-22), Telangana has no T004 series before 2014, say '1.7 times as much' not '1.7 times more', and split dates and Acts are not in SOURCE_MAP.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T019, T020, T004, T006, T007; plfs_annual.json (LFPR female); split dates and Acts from the Reorganisation Acts (Ministry of Home Affairs / India Code, SOURCE_MAP: Press Information Bureau and Census entries) to be cited at edit. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Launch slot 8. 'How a place came to be' is the strongest Shorts hook: state formation, merger and what-if stories reach 1M+ in 26.7% of cases against a 12.4% baseline (60 Shorts, 22 channels), and neutral 'how X became' Shorts have a median of 383K (research_shorts_india.md). In long-form, a named pair in a verdict frame is the over-indexing shape (Telangana vs Andhra Pradesh 494K, 61x). The gap in Bihar vs Jharkhand on babies versus income is a belief-overturner.
- **Risks:** Causation: the series starts at 2004 (babies) or 2011-12 (income), so it cannot show the split's effect; say 'compare', not 'pay off'. The Telangana and Andhra Pradesh 2011-12 figures are a back-cast to the 2014 boundary. Political charge around the 2014 split: use dates and numbers only, no commentary on motives, parties or the capital. Infant-mortality gaps of a few points are small-number noise, so show them as level when thin. Check Jharkhand's separate rows against Bihar's before use.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** drop. A four-pairs-by-four-measures tour with no verdict that cannot answer 'was the split worth it' and duplicates L026.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. The gap-over-time line uses current-price ratios that bake in each state's different deflator (Chhattisgarh/Madhya Pradesh 1.07x nominal vs 1.32x at constant prices; Jharkhand/Bihar 1.68x nominal vs 1.88x constant), so it overstates catch-up and contradicts L026's constant-price tie.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. Four parent-child pairs times four measures is a tour with no verdict that Bihar, UP and MP viewers see as 'our state is the poorer half' again, and it duplicates L026.

Ch2 Size and income, rounds 1-2 (2 min): GSDP 2024-25 (T021), income per person (T019). Ch3 What each state makes, rounds 3-5 (3 min): manufacturing value added (T033), services value added (T049), exports (T181). Ch4 Lives, rounds 6-8 (2.5 min): infant mortality 2023 (T004), life expectancy 2019-23 (T007), stunted children (NFHS-6). Ch5 Women and wages, rounds 9-10 (2 min): women in the labour force 2023-24 (PLFS), rural daily wage (T112). Ch6 The budget, round 11 (1.5 min): interest as a share of revenue spending (T170 over T166). Ch7 The 'level' round (1 min): phones per 100 (T149) and women online (NFHS-6) too close to call. Ch8 Final tally; viewers pick their winner before the reveal (1 min). SERIES 'Rivals', first ten in order: 1 South vs North, 2 this video, 3 Tamil Nadu vs Kerala, 4 Maharashtra vs Gujarat, 5 Telangana vs Andhra Pradesh (after the twin-states video), 6 Uttar Pradesh vs Bihar (small test: low-profile pairs under-performed), 7 Punjab vs Haryana, 8 West Bengal vs Odisha, 9 Madhya Pradesh vs Rajasthan, 10 'Is Telangana the next Karnataka?' (verdict frame).
- **Who shares it and why:** Bengaluru and Chennai colleagues forward it to each other with 'told you', and the Hosur and Mysuru border crowds argue in the comments about which round matters.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Friendly rivalry. Bengaluru and Chennai colleagues forward it to each other with 'told you'; the Hosur/Krishnagiri and Mysuru/Coimbatore crowd argues in comments about which round matters. Expected comments: 'services is not real economy', 'Bengaluru roads vs Chennai floods' (we will not engage), 'you forgot Kerala'. Share trigger: the opening split frame (Karnataka higher income, Tamil Nadu 2.7x factories) and the 'level' cards that stop either side claiming victory.
- **Why it could flop:** The exact pair already exists with proven winners (Angel One 1.1M, Adarsh Gupta 473K), so we would be the third version competing against the proof that justifies the idea. That 136x may be a one-off helped by the 2026 delimitation news and survivorship. A twelve-metric scoreboard can read like a spreadsheet, the Cauvery and language flame-bait is a moderation burden, and it depends on the engine built for L056.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Income per person 2024-25 (T019, current prices): Karnataka 3,80,906; Tamil Nadu 3,61,619. Factories 2023-24 (T116): Tamil Nadu 40,121; Karnataka 14,984. GSDP 2024-25 (T021): Tamil Nadu 3.11859e+08 lakh rupees; Karnataka 2.88390e+08 lakh. Manufacturing value added (T033): Tamil Nadu 5.27293e+07 lakh; Karnataka 3.24418e+07 lakh. Services value added (T049): Karnataka 1.79102e+08 lakh; Tamil Nadu 1.5504e+08 lakh. Exports 2024-25 (T181): Tamil Nadu USD 52,074 million; Karnataka USD 30,481 million. Infant mortality 2023: Tamil Nadu 12, Karnataka 14. Women in the labour force 2023-24: Tamil Nadu 43.2%, Karnataka 38.0%. Phones per 100 (T149, 2025): Karnataka 103.95, Tamil Nadu 102.09. Women who have used the internet (NFHS-6): Tamil Nadu 59.5%, Karnataka 57.9%.
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. Recomputed from RBI T019, T021, T033, T049, T116, T181, T004, T007, T112, plfs_annual.json and nfhs6_states.json (ids 14, 69): every key number and the 2.7x factory ratio (40,121 / 14,984 = 2.68) match. The one wrong figure is in the risks line: T168 own tax revenue 2024-25 (BE) is Tamil Nadu 1,95,173 vs Karnataka 1,89,893 crore (not 3,95,173), a 2.8% gap on a budget estimate, so show it as level.
- **Corrected numbers:** T168 own tax revenue 2024-25 BE: TN 1,95,173 vs KA 1,89,893 crore (2.8% gap); 2022-23 actuals: TN 1,50,223 vs KA 1,43,702 (4.5%). Near-level rounds: phones per 100 (T149) 102.09 vs 103.95 (1.8%), NFHS-6 internet 59.5 vs 57.9 (1.6 points).
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T019, T021, T033, T049, T116, T181, T004, T007, T112, T149, T153, T166, T170; plfs_annual.json (LFPR female 2023-24); nfhs6_states.json (indicators 14, 69). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Launch slot 2. The same pair is the best-measured duel: Angel One Economics 'Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu: Whose Model is Winning?' reached 1.1M views, 136x its channel median, on a 28K-subscriber channel; Telangana vs Andhra Pradesh got 494K (61x) while its single-state profiles got only 34K to 55K (research_long_india.md). Tamil Nadu vs Karnataka is also a top typed comparison in search. The data is genuinely split, which is what keeps a duel honest.
- **Risks:** Depends on the Scoreboard engine being built for idea 1. The rule on thin leads applies: own tax revenue (3,95,173 vs 1,89,893 crore is not a typo risk, check T168 values before use) and phones per 100 are close, so show them as level. Do not use any party, chief minister or 'model' wording; use the neutral 'who leads' only. Revenue spending and interest are 2024-25 budget estimates, not actuals. Higher power use per person (T138) is not 'better', so keep it unscored.
- **Effort:** low
- **Red team (growth):** keep. The only long-form with proven demand for the exact pair (Angel One 1.1M on a 28K channel, Adarsh Gupta 473K) and a verdict-ready hook, but cut 12 rounds to about 6, commit to a verdict, and note the Scoreboard engine is a hidden cost.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. Two-entity comparisons recompute (income, factories 2.68x, GSDP, wages) and the engine reports thin leads as level; fix the wrong own-tax figure (Tamil Nadu 1,95,173 vs Karnataka 1,89,893 crore, 2.8% gap), label exports as goods only, treat phones and internet as level, and publish no overall winner beyond the tally.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu on 12 numbers is a Bengaluru-Chennai argument with zero stake for a viewer in UP, Assam or Punjab, and it is a third version of a pair already done by others.

### L026. Telangana vs Andhra, Jharkhand vs Bihar: Who Grew Faster?

- **Original title:** Did Splitting a State Work? Telangana, Jharkhand and More
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.5 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 7, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Place Stories
- **Template:** existing: Vox explainer engine (16:9), plus a new split-pair scene (parent and child outlines, two-line chart, scoreboard).
- **Hook:** Four states were cut out of older ones. Did the new state beat the one it left behind?
- **Outline:** Target 13 min. 1) Cold open and the four splits: Chhattisgarh from Madhya Pradesh, Uttarakhand from Uttar Pradesh, Jharkhand from Bihar (all November 2000), Telangana from Andhra Pradesh (2014); dates from the Acts, to verify. 2) Income per person, 2011-12 to 2024-25 (T019): levels, for example Telangana ₹387,623 vs Andhra Pradesh ₹266,240. 3) Price-adjusted growth (T020): the child grew faster only in Telangana (2.11x vs 2.05x); Madhya Pradesh beat Chhattisgarh (1.83x vs 1.69x) and Uttar Pradesh beat Uttarakhand (1.71x vs 1.58x); Jharkhand and Bihar are a tie (1.66x vs 1.67x). 4) Babies and mothers: infant deaths and fertility, 2023 (T004, T006). 5) Power at home: kWh per person, 2024-25 (T138). 6) The starting-point twist: Chhattisgarh's income lead over Madhya Pradesh shrank from 43% to 7%. 7) What the tables cannot tell: causes, recast boundaries, series breaks. 8) Scoreboard and the viewer vote: 'Which split would you judge?'
- **Who shares it and why:** Telangana and Andhra viewers, Jharkhandis and Biharis, and Chhattisgarh and Madhya Pradesh viewers each send it to the other side to settle an old argument.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: a strong but fair question plus friendly rivalry. Telangana and Andhra people, Jharkhandis and Biharis, Chhattisgarh and Madhya Pradesh viewers share it to settle arguments. Expect 'Telangana obviously', 'Bihar beat Jharkhand?', mining and IT as explanations. Trigger frame: 'In three of the four pairs, the parent state kept up or grew faster.'
- **Why it could flop:** The title asks 'did splitting work?' but the data cannot answer it. Three of the four splits happened in November 2000, about 11 years before the 2011-12 base of T020, so the series only shows who grew faster after the split, not what the split did. Only Telangana (2.11x vs 2.05x, a gap of about 3%) beats its parent, and Jharkhand/Bihar is a tie, so the verdict is muddy: children are richer per person on current prices yet three of four parents grew faster. That is a good argument but a confusing thumbnail. Evidence says mid-profile duels fail (Maharashtra vs Gujarat 22K, Gujarat vs Rajasthan 23K, Bihar vs Rest of India 0.3x) while Telangana vs Andhra did 61x, so three of the four pairs may dilute the one pair that travels. Statehood is emotive; the comments will turn political however neutral the script is, and the rule against saying a split was good or bad collides with the title.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Income per person, constant prices, 2011-12 to 2024-25 (T020): Telangana 91,121 to 192,128; Andhra Pradesh 69,000 to 141,609; Jharkhand 41,254 to 68,357; Bihar 21,750 to 36,342; Chhattisgarh 55,177 to 93,161; Madhya Pradesh 38,497 to 70,434; Uttarakhand 100,314 to 158,819; Uttar Pradesh 32,648 to 55,990. Current prices 2024-25 (T019): Telangana 387,623; Andhra Pradesh 266,240; Jharkhand 116,663; Bihar 69,321; Chhattisgarh 162,870; Madhya Pradesh 152,615; Uttarakhand 274,064; Uttar Pradesh 108,572. Infant deaths 2023 (T004): 18 vs 19; 29 vs 23; 37 vs 37; 20 vs 37. Fertility 2023 (T006): 1.5 vs 1.5; 2.1 vs 2.8; 2.2 vs 2.4; 1.7 vs 2.6. Power per person 2024-25 (T138): 2,306.1 vs 1,481.6; 378.6 vs 343.9; 1,414.6 vs 1,190.6; 1,423.2 vs 692.2.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed T020 2011-12 to 2024-25 multiples (Telangana 2.108 vs Andhra Pradesh 2.052, Madhya Pradesh 1.830 vs Chhattisgarh 1.688, Uttar Pradesh 1.715 vs Uttarakhand 1.583, Jharkhand 1.657 vs Bihar 1.671) and every T019, T004, T006 and T138 figure matches, so the 'three of four pairs' trigger holds. The series starts 11 to 14 years after the 2000 splits and Telangana's 2011-12 base is back-cast, so the data cannot answer 'did splitting work'; retitle it as a comparison.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook tables T019, T020, T004, T006, T138 under public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ (original agencies from the table notes); formation dates from the Reorganisation Acts (India Code, not in SOURCE_MAP). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Place-based long-form videos reach 3x their channel median 37% of the time against 13% for other topics (235 videos, 21 channels); 'who is winning' and 'next' verdict frames hit 43.5%. Angel One's 'Telangana vs Andhra Pradesh: Who is Winning?' did 494K (61x) and 'Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu: Whose Model is Winning?' 1.1M (136x) on a 28K-subscriber channel. Narrated place videos of 12 to 20 minutes hit 3x in 45% of cases. Timeliness: the 26th anniversaries of Chhattisgarh, Uttarakhand and Jharkhand fall on 1, 9 and 15 November 2026 (dates from memory; verify).
- **Risks:** Statehood movements are political: never credit parties or leaders and never say a split was good or bad. Jharkhand and Bihar differ by about 1%: call it a tie. Telangana's pre-2014 values are recast; the base is 2011-12 and the series are revised, so check table notes. All four children started richer per person, so growth multiples carry a catch-up effect: say so. The November window is tight (about three weeks); if missed, tie it to 2 June 2027.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. The title promises an answer the data cannot give (three of four splits predate the 2011-12 base), the topic is politically charged under our neutrality rules, and the origin-lane pull is a Shorts finding rather than a long-form one.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. 'Did splitting a state work?' asks a causal question the data cannot answer (the T020 base year is 2011-12, 11 years after three of the four splits), and the trigger line 'the parent kept up or grew faster in three of four' hands viewers a verdict on a political issue.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. The title asks 'did splitting work?' but the data cannot answer it, so I would feel baited, and Bihar and UP are again the lower half of the comparison.

### L049. Tamil Nadu's Economy Nearly Equals UP's With a Third of the People

- **Original title:** Tamil Nadu vs Uttar Pradesh: Similar Economies, 3.3x Per Person
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.5 (virality 6, appeal 6, distinct 5, fit 9, risk medium)
- **Series:** Rivals
- **Template:** new: DK Duel Long (shared template) with a 'one economy, shared by how many people' scale card (a block of rupees divided among two crowds of people)
- **Hook:** Spoken: 'Tamil Nadu's economy is within 5% of Uttar Pradesh's in size. A person in Tamil Nadu produces 3.3 times as much.' On screen: two equal blocks of rupees, then two very different crowds under them.
- **Outline:** Target 14 min. 1. Cold open (1 min). 2. Size (2): GSDP 2024-25 at current prices Tamil Nadu 31.19 vs Uttar Pradesh 29.78 lakh crore rupees (Uttar Pradesh is 95.5% of it; 91.4% at constant prices). 3. People (2): implied by RBI's own totals, about 77 million vs 240 million (derived; not a census count). 4. Per person (2): current prices 361,619 vs 108,572; constant prices 197,747 vs 55,990 (3.5x). 5. Where each is moving (2.5): per-person growth 5.96% vs 4.24% a year since 2011-12; if Uttar Pradesh kept 4.24% it would need about 30 years to reach Tamil Nadu's 2024-25 level even if Tamil Nadu stood still (arithmetic, not a forecast). 6. What the gap looks like (2): infant mortality 12 vs 37; fertility 1.3 vs 2.6; factory workers 2.48 million vs 1.28 million. 7. Where Uttar Pradesh leads (2): sugarcane 220.8 million tonnes, milk 38.8 million tonnes, wheat 35.65 million tonnes, domestic tourist visits 646.8 million vs 306.8 million. 8. Verdict (1.5).
- **Who shares it and why:** A Tamil Nadu viewer sends the 3.3x line to a Uttar Pradesh friend or colleague as a brag, and the Uttar Pradesh friend answers with the population and tourism rebuttals, which keeps the thread going.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Surprise plus friendly rivalry. Uttar Pradesh viewers will share the 'where Uttar Pradesh leads' chapter; Tamil Nadu viewers the per-person chapter. Comments: 'Uttar Pradesh has three times the people', 'land and history', 'tourism is not income'. Share trigger frame: two equal blocks of rupees over two very different crowds, and the line 'about 30 years, even if Tamil Nadu stood still'.
- **Why it could flop:** The paradox is mostly arithmetic: Uttar Pradesh has about three times the people (our own derived 240 vs 77 million), so 'similar economy, 3.3x per person' is the population ratio restated, and the first comment will say so. Uttar Pradesh place videos in the sample sit near 1.0x the channel median (38% at 3x+) and its audience is mostly Hindi, so the Uttar Pradesh side may not show up. Any wording that reads as 'Uttar Pradesh is less productive' breaks the no-mockery rule.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** GSDP 2024-25 current prices (T021, rupees lakh): Tamil Nadu 3.11859e8, Uttar Pradesh 2.97822e8 (95.5%); constant prices (T022): 173,218,858 vs 158,263,609 (91.4%). Per-person NSDP 2024-25 (T019): 361,619 vs 108,572 (3.3x); constant (T020): 197,747 vs 55,990 (3.5x). Implied population (NSDP divided by per-person NSDP, derived): about 77.2 vs 239.5 million. Per-person growth since 2011-12 (T020, my CAGR): Tamil Nadu 5.96%, Uttar Pradesh 4.24%. Infant mortality 2023: 12 vs 37; fertility 2023: 1.3 vs 2.6; factory workers 2023-24: 2,475,675 vs 1,280,760; tourist visits 2024 (T013): 646.81 vs 306.84 million; sugarcane 220,801 thousand tonnes (T065).
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. T021/T022/T023/T019/T020/T004/T006/T122/T013/T065 recompute exactly: GSDP 95.5% current and 91.4% constant, per person 3.33x and 3.53x, implied population 77.2M vs 239.5M, per-person CAGR 5.96% vs 4.24%, and 30.4 years for UP to reach Tamil Nadu's constant-price level. Cautions: 'within 5%' holds only at current prices (8.6% at constant), the 30-year sum is constant-price only (current-price UP growth would give about 13 years), and the tourist pair 646.81 vs 306.84 is UP first, the reverse of the other pairs.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ T021, T022, T023, T019, T020, T004, T006, T122, T065, T091, T059, T013. 'Implied population' is T023 divided by T019, our derivation. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Tamil Nadu vs Uttar Pradesh is one of the typed comparisons in YouTube search, and Tamil Nadu is the best-performing named state (75% at 3x+). The 'same size, very different per person' framing is a data paradox, the hook type behind FactTechz's 4.6M-view Bihar Short; and 'Why West UP is Rich but East UP is Poor?' reached 780K (11x).
- **Risks:** 'Implied population' is our derivation from RBI totals, not a census figure; label it. The size gap depends on the price basis (4.5% vs 8.6%). Do not suggest people in Uttar Pradesh are less productive; the gap reflects structure and capital, which the tables cannot separate. The 30-year line is arithmetic on past growth, not a forecast. Tourist visit counts are compiled differently by states, and tourism is not income. Keep the tone respectful to both.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. 'Similar economy, 3.3x per person' is mostly the population ratio restated (77 vs 240 million by its own derivation), so the paradox is arithmetic.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. The hook says a Tamil Nadu 'person produces 3.3 times as much', which turns a per-capita total into worker productivity and mocks Uttar Pradesh; 'within 5%' holds only at current prices (8.6% at constant), the 30-year line is an extrapolation, and the 2024 tourist figures are flagged as estimates elsewhere.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. The punchline is that a Tamil Nadu person produces 3.3 times a UP person, which is mostly UP's larger population restated and makes UP the butt of a Tamil Nadu brag.

### LG13. North vs South India: Gap Inside North 3.2x, Inside South 1.5x

- **Original title:** North vs South India: What the 12 Official Numbers Don't Show
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.4 (virality 7, appeal 7, distinct 5, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** North vs South hub
- **Template:** new: 16:9 two-zone spread chart on the Vox engine (a dot-strip per indicator, one dot per state); the hub spokes reuse Ranking, Face-off and chart posts
- **Hook:** Delhi earns 3.2 times what Jammu and Kashmir does. Telangana earns 1.5 times what Andhra Pradesh does. So where exactly is the North-South gap?
- **Outline:** Target 13 min. 1 Recap (1 min): the 12 numbers from the hub video in 60 seconds. 2 The average trap (2.5): income per person (T019, 2024-25, current prices) North zone Delhi 493,024 to J&K 154,826 = 3.18x; South zone Telangana 387,623 to Andhra Pradesh 266,240 = 1.46x; at constant prices 3.47x vs 1.44x (T020); in 2011-12 it was 3.57x vs 1.73x, so both narrowed. 3 Women at work (2): labour force participation 2023-24 North from Delhi 18.5% to Himachal Pradesh 67.6%; South from Puducherry 34.2% to Telangana 46.7%. 4 Where the North leads (2.5): power per person Punjab 2,503.4 kWh beats Telangana 2,306.1 and every other southern state (T138); life expectancy Himachal Pradesh and J&K 74.4 and Delhi 74.2 beat Tamil Nadu 73.4 and Karnataka 70.0 (T007); wheat yield Punjab 5,123 vs Telangana 1,973 and Karnataka 1,255 kg per hectare (T081); lowest fertility Delhi 1.2 vs Tamil Nadu 1.3 (T006). 5 Where the South leads (2): rural construction wage Kerala 922.6 vs J&K 584.5 (T112); life expectancy Kerala 75.1. 6 Where the South is the wider zone (1.5): power per person Telangana 2,306.1 vs Kerala 880.3 = 2.62x vs North 1.81x. 7 Whose 'North'? (1): zones follow the statutory Zonal Councils; Uttar Pradesh, Uttarakhand, Bihar and others sit in other zones; show how adding Bihar's 69,321 changes the spread. 8 Make your own map (0.5): viewer picks states; quick Higher-or-Lower. HUB PLAN (one research pull becomes different videos, not near-duplicates): anchor = the existing zone-by-zone long; spokes = 12 single-number 'round' Shorts (each a different number from this video), 1 Higher-or-Lower, 1 paradox Short ('Punjab out-powers every southern state'), 1 your-zone quiz, 1 chart post of the dot-strips, plus this follow-up; every spoke ends on the long.
- **Who shares it and why:** Viewers from both North and South forward the two dot-strips to the other side ('we lead here too'), and the 'whose North?' definition fight keeps the comments going.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A surprise that overturns a belief, plus identity. Viewers on both sides get a fact to quote (the North out-powers every southern state; the South is far tighter on income), so each side forwards it to the other: 'see, we lead here too'. Likely comments: 'whose North?' (a useful definition fight), 'what about Bihar and UP', 'now do East vs West'. Share trigger: the two dot-strips side by side, wide in the North and tight in the South, with '3.18x vs 1.46x'.
- **Why it could flop:** It is a sequel to a hub video that is not yet made, so as a standalone it opens with a recap that cold viewers did not ask for. North vs South is the strongest measured bucket (63% at 3x+) but also the most crowded long-form lane (19 videos on 8 channels, plus Dhruv Rathee's 12.9M), every measured hit was presenter-led, and many used a rage-bait frame ('100 times better') that this balanced framing deliberately drops. The 3.2x gap compares a city (Delhi) with a state (J&K), and the zone definition is from memory and will be disputed; part of the lift was the 2026 delimitation news cycle (an assumption).
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Income per person (T019, 2024-25): North zone Delhi 493,024, Haryana 353,182, Himachal Pradesh 256,137, Punjab 221,197, Rajasthan 185,053, J&K 154,826 (spread 3.18x); South zone Telangana 387,623, Karnataka 380,906, Tamil Nadu 361,619, Kerala 308,338, Puducherry 281,478, Andhra Pradesh 266,240 (1.46x). Constant prices (T020): 3.47x vs 1.44x. 2011-12: 3.57x vs 1.73x. Women's labour force 2023-24 (PLFS): Delhi 18.5%, Himachal Pradesh 67.6%; Puducherry 34.2%, Telangana 46.7%. Power per person (T138, 2024-25): Punjab 2,503.4, Telangana 2,306.1, Rajasthan 1,386.3, Kerala 880.3 kWh. Life expectancy (T007, 2019-23): Kerala 75.1, Himachal Pradesh 74.4, J&K 74.4, Delhi 74.2, Tamil Nadu 73.4, Karnataka 70.0. Wheat yield (T081, 2024-25): Punjab 5,123, Telangana 1,973, Karnataka 1,255 kg/ha. Fertility (T006, 2023): Delhi 1.2, Tamil Nadu 1.3. Rural construction wage (T112, 2024-25): Kerala 922.6, J&K 584.5.
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. Income spreads recompute exactly from T019/T020 (3.18x vs 1.46x current, 3.47x vs 1.44x constant, 3.57x vs 1.73x in 2011-12), and the PLFS 2023-24 LFPR, T138, T007, T081, T006 and T112 numbers all match, but only for Northern Zonal Council states (Chandigarh and Ladakh have no 2024-25 income). The line 'the North out-powers every southern state' is false: only Punjab (2,503.4) beats Telangana (2,306.1), Telangana beats Haryana (2,293.5), and Puducherry (2,109.3) beats Delhi; also L012 defines North as Northern plus Central, which would make the income spread 4.54x (Delhi vs Uttar Pradesh), and Zonal Council membership is not in SOURCE_MAP.
- **Corrected numbers:** Say 'Punjab out-powers every southern state' (T138 2024-25: Punjab 2,503.4 vs Telangana 2,306.1, 8.6% lead). If North = Northern + Central, income spread is 4.54x (Delhi 493,024 / UP 108,572) and women's LFPR 2023-24 runs from Delhi 18.5 to HP 67.6 unchanged.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook T019, T020, T006, T007, T081, T112, T138; public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (LFPR 2023-24, female). Zonal Council membership from the Ministry of Home Affairs / Inter-State Council Secretariat (not in SOURCE_MAP.md; verify). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 9 (hub-and-spoke). North vs South is the strongest measured bucket (19 videos on 8 channels, 63% at 3x+, 37% at 10x+, median 4.4x, 380K to 1.1M views on channels of 5K to 28K subscribers). The list holds 48 long ideas that collapse to about 30 distinct videos, so a plan prevents near-duplicates: repeats of 'Top 10 Richest States' decayed from 62K to 2K, while event-style hooks on a recurring visual win. This follow-up gives the hub a different hook (what zone averages hide) and each Short spoke its own hook. Shorts views do not count toward the 4,000 watch hours, so every spoke ends on the long.
- **Risks:** The zone definition is the whole claim: Zonal Council membership is from memory and must be checked, and the hub's original zone-by-zone video may use other groupings, in which case reuse them and recompute. Not every state has every indicator (life expectancy covers 22 states; Chandigarh and Ladakh have no 2024-25 income). Power and other spreads go the other way, so show them. No 'South is better' framing (neutrality), no ranking by religion or caste. J&K is the northern low in income: word it as a number, not a verdict. Thin leads: Himachal Pradesh and J&K vs Tamil Nadu on life expectancy is about 1 year, so say 'above', not 'best'.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. A sequel to a hub video that does not exist, opening with a recap cold viewers did not ask for.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. The 3.2x vs 1.5x 'internal gap' compares a max/min that Delhi (a city-state) drives against a southern set with none, and the result depends on the zone definition (4.54x if North includes Central), with fertility numbers feeding delimitation politics.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. It is a sequel to a hub video that does not exist yet and opens with a recap cold viewers did not ask for; the dot-strip idea is good but belongs inside L012.

### L013. Richest States of India 2024-25: Big Economy vs Rich People (RBI)

- **Original title:** Richest States in India: Big Economy vs Rich People (RBI Data)
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.4 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 4, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** The Annual State Table
- **Template:** new: 16:9 two-rank slope chart plus ranked bars on the official map, with a year-stamped data card
- **Hook:** In the RBI's 2024-25 table, Uttar Pradesh is 3rd by size and 24th per person. Both are true.
- **Outline:** Target 13 minutes. 1. Two ways to rank a state (1.5): size (GSDP) against per person (NSDP per capita), and why the two lists differ. 2. The size list (2): Maharashtra Rs 45.32 lakh crore, Tamil Nadu 31.19, Uttar Pradesh 29.78, Karnataka 28.84; the five largest are 49.4% of the 25 states and UTs' total. 3. The per-person list (2.5): Delhi Rs 4,93,024; Telangana 3,87,623; Karnataka 3,80,906; Tamil Nadu 3,61,619; Haryana 3,53,182; Bihar 69,321, a 7.1x gap. 4. The rank swap (2): Uttar Pradesh 3rd to 24th, Maharashtra 1st to 6th, Telangana 7th to 2nd, Delhi 11th to 1st, Bihar 13th to 25th. 5. Who is missing (2): Gujarat (last figure 2022-23), Goa, Sikkim, Chandigarh, Manipur, Mizoram, Nagaland, Ladakh, Andaman and Nicobar; on 2023-24 values Sikkim Rs 5,87,743 and Goa 5,85,953 sit above Delhi's 4,59,408. 6. Prices matter (1.5): the constant-price list (Delhi 2,83,093; Karnataka 2,04,605; Tamil Nadu 1,97,747; Haryana 1,94,285; Telangana 1,92,128) and growth since 2011-12 (Telangana 4.25x, Karnataka 4.22x, Bihar 3.19x, Delhi 2.66x, nominal). 7. Your state's card and what changes when RBI and MoSPI update (1.5).
- **Who shares it and why:** Telangana, Karnataka and Tamil Nadu viewers send the per-person list, UP and Maharashtra viewers the size list, and friends from Gujarat or Goa forward the 'where is my state?' gap to start the 'Delhi is not a state' argument.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: identity and pride with a surprise that overturns a belief. Uttar Pradesh and Tamil Nadu viewers share the 'size' half with pride, Karnataka and Telangana viewers the 'per person' half, and everyone forwards the 'who is missing' reveal to friends from Gujarat and Goa ('where is my state?'). Expected comments: 'Delhi is not a state', 'cost of living', 'Gujarat?'. Share trigger: the slope chart where Uttar Pradesh falls 21 places.
- **Why it could flop:** Gujarat is missing from the 2024-25 table (the idea's own 2022-23 figures put it 5th by size), so 'UP 3rd', 'UP 24th', 'Maharashtra 6th' and the 49.4% top-five share are true only 'in RBI's table' and can flip when Gujarat's figure lands; the top comment will be 'where is Gujarat?'. The query is owned by 4-5 minute non-official charts with 3M views, a zero-subscriber 13-minute video has no search authority, and Mohak Mangal's 33-minute version reached only 1.4x his own median. The decay of the 'Top 10 Richest States' series (62K down to 2K) shows no second chance.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** GSDP 2024-25 current prices (Rs lakh): Maharashtra 453,151,842; Tamil Nadu 311,859,030; Uttar Pradesh 297,822,351; Karnataka 288,390,323; West Bengal 181,500,991. Income per person 2024-25 (Rs): Delhi 493,024; Telangana 387,623; Karnataka 380,906; Tamil Nadu 361,619; Haryana 353,182; Maharashtra 309,340; Kerala 308,338; Uttar Pradesh 108,572; Bihar 69,321 (7.1x gap to Delhi is 7.1x for the top and bottom of the table). Constant-price per person 2024-25: Delhi 283,093; Karnataka 204,605; Tamil Nadu 197,747; Haryana 194,285; Telangana 192,128; Bihar 36,342. 2023-24 current prices: Sikkim 587,743; Goa 585,953; Delhi 459,408; Chandigarh 453,457; Gujarat's latest figure is 272,451 (2022-23). Per-person 2011-12 to 2024-25 multiples (nominal) computed from the table. Rank pairs (size rank, per-person rank) computed from the 25 states with 2024-25 data.
- **Data editor:** numbers re-checked. Recomputed from T019, T020, T021: all GSDP, per-person, constant-price and 2023-24 values, the 49.4% top-five share, the 7.1x gap and every rank pair (UP 3rd to 24th, Maharashtra 1st to 6th, Telangana 7th to 2nd, Delhi 11th to 1st, Bihar 13th to 25th) match. All ranks are among the 25 states and UTs with 2024-25 data (Gujarat, Goa, Sikkim and five others are absent), which the outline already plans to say; the MoSPI release dates come from the digest calendar and the December Handbook timing is unverified.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook: T019 (per capita NSDP, current prices), T020 (constant prices), T021 (GSDP, current prices), base 2011-12, columns 2011-12 to 2024-25. Timing hooks for the refresh: MoSPI Q2 GDP estimates 30 Nov 2026, First Advance Estimates 7 Jan 2027, RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States usually in December (not re-verified). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Proven demand for exactly this query: Globe's 'Richest States Of INDIA 2025' 3.0M (118x its median), Data Vids' 'Richest India States 2024' 3.27M, Mohak Mangal's 33-minute 'India's Richest vs Poorest States' 2.8M, and income/GDP/'richest' is the largest theme in YouTube autocomplete (563 suggestions). The research recommends one definitive official-source version, updated annually, because near-identical repeats decayed from 62K to 2K. The 'big versus rich' twist and the 'who is missing' chapter are the differentiators.
- **Risks:** Crowded query: earlier winners were 4-5 minute non-official chart videos (lottery tickets), while 4-8 minute videos have lower medians than a channel's longer ones, so keep 12-14 minutes. The table has holes: never say 'richest state' without 'among states with 2024-25 data', and note Goa and Sikkim lead on 2023-24 values (and Sikkim leads Goa by only 0.3%). Telangana is ahead of Karnataka by only 1.8% in 2024-25, so no '#2' claim. Delhi is a Union Territory with a legislature, say so. Current-price per-person income is not purchasing power or inequality. Do not release variants of this video; update it yearly.
- **Effort:** high
- **Red team (growth):** drop. The most crowded query in the set (Globe 3.0M, Data Vids 3.27M, Mohak Mangal 2.8M) with table holes (no Gujarat in 2024-25) that undercut a 'definitive' trust claim, and it duplicates L070.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. 'Uttar Pradesh 24th per person' and 'Delhi 1st' are ranks within a 25-of-34 table that omits Goa, Sikkim and Gujarat (the actual top per-person states), so the headline reads as a national rank it is not; L070 is the corrected version of the same video.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. It duplicates L070 on the same richest-states query, and its hook ('UP 3rd by size, 24th per person') puts the UP viewer's state at the bottom within the first sentence.

### LG14. Telangana or Karnataka? The RBI's Own Tables Give Two Answers

- **Original title:** Telangana or Karnataka? India's No. 2 Richest State Has Two Answers
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.3 (virality 5, appeal 5, distinct 8, fit 8, risk low)
- **Series:** Find It Yourself
- **Template:** new: 16:9 redrawn-table walkthrough (RBI, MoSPI and NFHS tables rebuilt in code, highlight on the cell with its unit and period; no filming)
- **Hook:** Ask the RBI's own tables for India's second-richest state per person, and the answer changes with one word: current or constant.
- **Outline:** Target 9 min. 1 The question on two tables (1.5 min): income per person at current prices (T019, 2024-25) Delhi 493,024, Telangana 387,623, Karnataka 380,906, Tamil Nadu 361,619; at constant 2011-12 prices (T020) Delhi 283,093, Karnataka 204,605, Tamil Nadu 197,747, Telangana 192,128: Telangana slides from 2nd to 4th. 2 Trap 1, current vs constant prices (1.5). 3 Trap 2, total vs per person (1.5): Maharashtra has the largest GSDP (453,151,842 rupees lakh, 2024-25, T021) but 309,340 rupees per person. 4 Trap 3, which year and which kind of number (1.5): financial year vs calendar year (PLFS 2025 vs 2023-24), actual vs budget estimate (T164 shows 2024-25 BE). 5 Trap 4, units (1): rupees lakh vs crore, thousand tonnes (Uttar Pradesh rice 20,758.7 thousand tonnes = 20.76 million tonnes, T058). 6 Trap 5, definitions and flags (0.5): PLFS 'usual status, age 15+'; NFHS cells marked '*' and '(x)'. 7 Find it yourself in 3 steps (1): RBI Handbook table number, MoSPI eSankhyiki, IIPS NFHS fact sheet. 8 How we stay honest (0.5): a corrections log in the description and a pinned comment, a 'this number changed' post when a source revises (PLFS annual report 25 Mar 2027, RBI Handbook each December), a pinned 'request your state' thread and a poll for the next comparison. COMPANION SHORTS (near-zero engineering): 'Your State Next' (the most-requested state) and 'This number changed'.
- **Who shares it and why:** Hyderabad and Bengaluru tech workers send it to each other to settle who is richer, and exam aspirants forward the current-versus-constant-prices lesson.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Friendly rivalry plus a belief overturn. Telangana and Karnataka (Hyderabad and Bengaluru) fans each send it to the other side to settle the argument; exam aspirants and finance students forward it as a 'read data properly' guide. Likely comments: 'constant prices is the real measure', 'it is a near tie anyway', 'do my state', which the pinned request thread turns into episodes. Share trigger: the rank animation where Telangana slides from 2nd to 4th as 'current' flips to 'constant'.
- **Why it could flop:** It is a trust piece, not a share piece: a 'find it yourself' table walkthrough teaches a nerdy concept, and the honest answer is 'a near tie, two ways', which can feel like a non-answer after a title that promises one. The comment-and-community mechanics rely on features a brand-new channel may not have, and comments alone do not create reach (Data Duck had 3,400 comments on 233K views). Before claiming 'No. 2' it must be checked that the 2024-25 column includes every state and UT (for example Goa and Sikkim), and the 1.8% gap has to be called a near tie.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Income per person, current prices (T019, 2024-25): Delhi 493,024, Telangana 387,623, Karnataka 380,906, Tamil Nadu 361,619 rupees; Telangana and Karnataka are 1.8% apart. Constant 2011-12 prices (T020, 2024-25): Delhi 283,093, Karnataka 204,605, Tamil Nadu 197,747, Telangana 192,128. Maharashtra GSDP 453,151,842 rupees lakh (T021, 2024-25) vs 309,340 rupees per person (T019). Uttar Pradesh rice 20,758.7 thousand tonnes (T058, 2024-25).
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. Current-price numbers (T019), Maharashtra GSDP (T021) and UP rice (T058) match, and Telangana vs Karnataka is 1.76% apart. At constant prices (T020) Haryana (194,285) sits between Tamil Nadu and Telangana, so Telangana falls from 2nd to 5th, not 4th, and 'second-richest' holds only among the 25 states and UTs reporting 2024-25 (Sikkim, Goa, Chandigarh, Gujarat absent; in 2023-24 Telangana is 5th of 33 at current prices, and Delhi is a UT, not a state).
- **Corrected numbers:** T020 2024-25 order: Delhi 283,093; Karnataka 204,605; Tamil Nadu 197,747; Haryana 194,285; Telangana 192,128 (5th of 25). Current prices T019 2024-25: Telangana 2nd of 25, 5th of 33 in 2023-24. At constant prices Karnataka leads Telangana by 6.5%, so only the current-price pair is a near tie.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook T019, T020, T021, T058, T164; public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json; public/datayt/nfhs6/nfhs6_states.json. Steps cite SOURCE_MAP entries: RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States, MoSPI eSankhyiki, NFHS (IIPS). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 10 (interactive and trust formats). The stated goal is attention and trust, and primary-source citation is the only differentiator against chart channels (Visual Capitalist Shorts median 2,300, Statista 644). Personalised and argue-about-it hooks pull 0.09% to 0.15% comment rates vs a 0.013% median. The list has 12 quiz and game ideas but no viewer-driven loop (requests, polls, a corrections log). State-rivalry videos drew 1.1M (Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu) and 494K (Telangana vs Andhra Pradesh) on small channels, so a Telangana-vs-Karnataka hook makes a how-to watchable.
- **Risks:** Thin lead: Telangana and Karnataka are 1.8% apart at current prices, so say 'near tie' and never name a winner. Comments alone do not create reach (Data Duck had 3,400 comments on only 233K views). Whether Community posts and polls are available to a brand-new channel is unverified; fall back to pinned comments. Moderation takes time. Name no individuals; the corrections log must carry dates. A how-to risks low retention, so keep the rivalry hook and the rank animation inside the first 60 seconds.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. A table-reading lesson that ends in 'a near tie, two ways' is a trust piece, not a share piece, and a no-winner answer is a retention risk.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. A trust piece whose core claim holds (Telangana and Karnataka 1.8% apart at current prices, order changes at constant prices), provided the hook stops saying 'India's second richest' (Goa, Sikkim and Chandigarh are higher in 2023-24; Telangana is 2nd only of 25 reporting) and the constant-price order is shown as Delhi, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu, Haryana, Telangana (5th).
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. 'Current versus constant prices' for Telangana vs Karnataka is a data-nerd lesson with a 'near tie, two ways' answer that feels like a non-answer to anyone not in Hyderabad or Bengaluru.

### L070. Richest States in India: Maharashtra Is Biggest, Not Richest

- **Original title:** Richest States in India Per Person: The Official Ranking (RBI)
- **Status:** Shortlisted, survived the red team. **Score** 6.1 (virality 6, appeal 8, distinct 3, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** Handbook Day (annual, refreshed each December when the RBI Handbook is published)
- **Template:** new: DK Long Map Explainer (16:9, 12-18 min, narrated; official-boundary India map as the spine, one map or chart on screen at a time, source/period/unit tag on every frame; includes a pause-and-guess beat borrowed from Guess the State). This is the flagship that defines the long-form engine.
- **Hook:** Maharashtra has India's biggest economy. Per person, it ranks 10th of 33 states and UTs.
- **Outline:** Target 13 minutes. 1) Two lists, one question: GSDP 2024-25 (T021) vs income per person. 2) Pause and guess: viewer ranks 8 states before the reveal. 3) Per person 2023-24, the one year all 33 states/UTs have (T019): Sikkim and Goa 0.3% apart, shown as co-leaders; then Delhi, Chandigarh; Gujarat shown separately at its latest year, 2022-23. 4) The big-state ladder 2024-25 (T019): Delhi, Telangana, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu, Haryana, Maharashtra ... Bihar, a 7.1x gap. 5) Change the price basis, change the podium (T020 constant prices): Karnataka 2nd, Tamil Nadu 3rd, Telangana falls to 5th. 6) Who is catching up: real income per person 2011-12 to 2024-25 (T020): Karnataka 2.27x, Tripura 2.25x, Tamil Nadu 2.12x, Telangana 2.11x, Delhi 1.53x (growth folded in here so it is not a second ranking video). 7) What the number is and is not: average output per person (NSDP), not household income; Delhi and Chandigarh are UTs; Sikkim and Goa are tiny. 8) Find your state, and what the next Handbook will add (Gujarat, Goa, Sikkim for 2024-25).
- **Who shares it and why:** It gets sent to the one friend who insists their state is richest (Telangana vs Karnataka, Kerala vs Tamil Nadu, Goa and Sikkim fans) in state WhatsApp groups.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Identity and pride plus a surprise that overturns a belief (the biggest economy is not the richest people) plus a guess-first quiz. Shared in state WhatsApp/Telegram groups and sent to the one friend who insists their state is richest (Telangana vs Karnataka, Kerala vs Tamil Nadu, Goa and Sikkim fans). Predictable comments: 'Where is Gujarat?' (answered on screen), 'Delhi is not a state', 'Goa is tiny', 'show PPP'. The share frame is a split screen, Biggest economy | Richest people, where Maharashtra drops from first to tenth.
- **Why it could flop:** This is the most crowded lane in the set: Globe, Data Vids, Mohak Mangal's 33-minute version, eight near-identical World In Details videos decaying from 62K to 2K, and 82% of richest-state Shorts under 1,000 views. 'Official source' is not a thumbnail hook for a zero-subscriber channel. The headline claim cannot be verified as written. Gujarat has no 2023-24 figure, and its 2022-23 value (Rs 2,72,451) is only 2.3% below Maharashtra's 2023-24 (Rs 2,78,681), so Gujarat very probably belongs above Maharashtra, which would make it 11th, not 10th (inference, check when Gujarat reports). The outline also says all 33 have 2023-24 while the risk note says Gujarat does not, so 'of 33' needs fixing. The best twist (constant prices reorder the podium) sits in chapter 5, not the title.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Income per person, current prices, 2023-24 (T019): Sikkim 5,87,743; Goa 5,85,953; Delhi 4,59,408; Chandigarh 4,53,457; Telangana 3,47,714; Karnataka 3,39,813; Maharashtra 2,78,681 (10th of 33); Bihar 62,201. 2024-25 (25 states/UTs reported): Delhi 4,93,024; Telangana 3,87,623; Karnataka 3,80,906; Tamil Nadu 3,61,619; Maharashtra 3,09,340 (6th of 25); Bihar 69,321 (Delhi 7.1x Bihar). Constant prices 2024-25 (T020): Delhi 2,83,093; Karnataka 2,04,605; Tamil Nadu 1,97,747; Telangana 1,92,128 (5th); Bihar 36,342 (7.8x). GSDP 2024-25 (T021): Maharashtra Rs 45.32 lakh crore; Tamil Nadu 31.19; Uttar Pradesh 29.78; Karnataka 28.84. Real income per person 2011-12 to 2024-25 (computed from T020): Karnataka 2.27x, Tripura 2.25x, Tamil Nadu 2.12x, Telangana 2.11x, Delhi 1.53x. Gujarat latest is 2022-23: 2,72,451.
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. T019/T020/T021 figures all match (Maharashtra 10th of 33 in 2023-24 and 6th of 25 in 2024-25, Delhi 7.1x Bihar, Telangana 5th at constant prices), but 2023-24 has only 33 of 34 entries (no Gujarat) and 2022-23 is the only year with all 34. In 2022-23 Maharashtra is 12th of 34 with Gujarat 9th, so '10th of 33' needs 'of the 33 reporting' and Gujarat's absence stated; Karnataka 2.267x and Tripura 2.251x real growth (T020) are 0.7% apart and must be shown as level.
- **Corrected numbers:** 2022-23 (T019, all 34): Goa 532,854, Sikkim 520,466, Gujarat 9th at 272,451, Maharashtra 12th at 252,289. Real growth 2011-12 to 2024-25 (T020): Karnataka 2.267x, Tripura 2.251x (co-leaders), Tamil Nadu 2.124x, Telangana 2.108x, Delhi 1.530x.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook of Statistics on Indian States, tables T019, T020, T021 (T022 and T023 for context), in public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/. Underlying producers: MoSPI and state statistics bureaus. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** The exact query is proven: Globe's 'Richest States Of INDIA 2025' got 3.0M views (118x its channel median, 4.7 min), Data Vids' 'Richest India States 2024' 3.27M, Mohak Mangal's 33-min version 2.8M, and GDP/income/richest is the largest search theme (563 suggestions). The research also says repeats decay (eight near-identical 'Top 10 Richest States' videos fell from 62K to 2K), so we publish ONE definitive version and update it yearly. All the winners used non-official numbers; an official-data version with a price-basis twist is the open gap.
- **Risks:** Gujarat has no 2023-24 or 2024-25 figure yet (latest 2022-23), so say so on screen. Use 2023-24 as the common year and 2024-25 only as an update; do not mix years in one ranking. Sikkim vs Goa is a 0.3% gap, so no '#1' claim; Telangana vs Karnataka is 1.8% in 2024-25, say 'close'. Per-person NSDP is average output, not household income. Verify whether a new GDP base year has replaced 2011-12 before publishing. Do not mock Bihar or UP: pair the 7.1x gap with their growth. First long-form means the engineering cost of the new engine lands here.
- **Effort:** high
- **Red team (growth):** drop. Same crowded richest-states lane against much bigger channels, with data gaps in the headline year, as a high-effort flagship for a channel with no audience.
- **Red team (accuracy):** keep. Best-scoped income video (common year, co-leaders for the 0.3% Sikkim-Goa gap, Gujarat shown separately), but fix 'the one year all 33 have' (2023-24 lacks Gujarat; only 2022-23 has all 34) and phrase the hook as 'of the 33 reporting'.
- **Red team (viewer):** keep. Richest states is the one long-form query where a viewer from any state is guaranteed to find their own row and a friend to send it to, and pause-and-guess keeps it from being a lecture, but only if the price-basis and catch-up chapters are short and the all-states ladder comes first (this is the weakest of my keeps).
