### L072. Why Is Kerala Different? 10 Official Numbers, 3 Puzzles

- **Original title:** Why Is Kerala Different? What 10 Official Numbers Actually Show
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (3 of 3 said no). **Score** 6.1 (virality 6, appeal 8, distinct 5, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** State Files (one state, ten numbers; vary the structure each time)
- **Template:** new: DK Long Map Explainer (16:9, 12-15 min) with a 'records vs puzzles' split screen.
- **Hook:** Kerala pays India's highest rural construction wage: Rs 922.6 a day. Yet 46 in every 1,000 rural men there are unemployed.
- **Outline:** Target 14 minutes. 1) The reputation: what people ask when they type 'why is Kerala so developed'. 2) Health records: infant mortality 5 per 1,000 (Manipur is lower at 3), life expectancy 75.1, multidimensional poverty headcount 0.55% (T004, T007, T010). 3) An older state: 60+ share 20.7% vs India 12.9%, fertility 1.5 (NFHS-6, T006). 4) Pay: rural construction wage Rs 922.6 vs Tamil Nadu 583.2 and Madhya Pradesh 307.8 (T112). 5) Puzzle one: pay is highest, but rural male unemployment is 46 per 1,000 vs India 27 (T008). 6) Puzzle two: income per person is only 7th of 25 in 2024-25 and 9th of 33 in 2023-24 (T019). 7) Puzzle three: net fixed capital formation in registered factories is negative (T129). 8) What the numbers cannot tell us: causes, and which data would be needed (RBI remittances survey as an optional chapter, to be read). 9) Your state's version: three records and three puzzles for any state.
- **Who shares it and why:** Malayalis at home and in the Gulf send it for pride and to argue causes in the comments, and friends in other states forward it as 'is this true?'.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Pride plus a paradox people want to explain. Shared by Malayalis and the Gulf diaspora, and forwarded to friends in other states as 'is this true?'. Comments will argue causes (remittances, education, politics): invite 'what would explain this?' rather than offering a cause. The frame that triggers the share is the split screen, Highest wages | Not the most jobs.
- **Why it could flop:** 'Why is Kerala...' is among the most-answered questions on Indian explainer YouTube, and our rules bar us from giving causes, so the video promises a 'why' and withholds it, which risks a click-gap and weak retention. The hook paradox (top wage, higher rural male unemployment) is explainable in one line by any commenter, which deflates the mystery. '46 in every 1,000 rural men' may misstate the denominator if T008 is per 1,000 in the labour force; check before use. The wage is a construction wage, not 'pay'. The '9th of 33' income rank has the same Gujarat flaw as L070: Kerala's Rs 2,81,269 is only 3.2% above Gujarat's 2022-23 figure, so Kerala is probably 10th. The life-expectancy lead is under 1%, so no 'highest' claim. Ten numbers is a list, and the strongest Kerala audience prefers Malayalam.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Rural construction wage 2024-25 (Rs/day): Kerala 922.6; J&K 584.5; Tamil Nadu 583.2; Madhya Pradesh 307.8; India 441.1. Rural male unemployment 2023-24 (per 1,000): Kerala 46; India 27. Infant mortality 2023: Kerala 5 (Manipur 3; Goa and Sikkim 6). Life expectancy 2019-23: Kerala 75.1; J&K and Himachal Pradesh 74.4; India 70.3. SDG score 2023: Kerala 79, level with Uttarakhand. Multidimensional poverty headcount NFHS-5: Kerala 0.55%; India 14.96%. Income per person: Rs 3,08,338 (2024-25, 7th of 25); Rs 2,81,269 (2023-24, 9th of 33). Net fixed capital formation in registered factories 2023-24: Kerala -Rs 15,647 lakh, the lowest (Sikkim -9,775). Population aged 60+ (NFHS-6): Kerala 20.7%, India 12.9%. Telephones per 100 (2025): 118.99.
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. T112, T004, T007, T010, T106, T129, T149, T019 and nfhs6 indicator 3 all match (wage 922.6, IMR 5 vs Manipur 3, LE 75.1, MPI 0.55%, SDG 79 level with Uttarakhand, NFCF -15,647 lowest, 60+ share 20.7% vs 12.9%, income 7th of 25 and 9th of 33). But T008's 46 is a rate per 1,000 in the labour force (PLFS rural male UR 4.6% vs India 2.7%), not 46 in every 1,000 rural men, and Kerala's wage is the highest only of the 20 states in T112.
- **Corrected numbers:** Hook: 'Kerala's rural unemployment rate for men is 4.6% (46 per 1,000 in the labour force) vs India 2.7%'; wage is highest of the 20 states in T112 (2024-25) while unemployment is 2023-24.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T004, T006, T007, T008, T010, T019, T106, T112, T129, T149; NFHS-6 public/datayt/nfhs6/nfhs6_states.json (indicator 3, population aged 60+). Optional: RBI Remittances Survey (not individually mapped; needs reading). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** 'Why is Kerala ...' is the richest why-cluster in YouTube search and Kerala is the most typed state (179 data-relevant suggestions). A state-data paradox is a measured breakout hook (FactTechz 'Bihar is No. 1 in internet use while having the lowest penetration' reached 4.6M in 9 days), and single-state 'why is X' videos on small channels reached 4.6x-6.4x (Adarsh Gupta). The counter-evidence is that plain single-state profiles on big channels sit near 1x (Mohak Mangal's Tamil Nadu 0.8x), so the paradox structure is what carries it.
- **Risks:** The data cannot carry causes: do not say what 'caused' any record, and keep out the political 'Kerala model' and 'communist' framing entirely. Do not say Kerala has the lowest infant mortality (Manipur is 3); life expectancy lead is 0.7 years (0.9%), so 'top, narrowly'. The wage series is rural construction workers only. Unemployment here is 'usual status (adjusted)' from the older series, not PLFS 2025. Do not repeat this shape for every state without changing the story.
- **Effort:** medium
- **Red team (growth):** drop. It promises the 'why' of Kerala and then withholds it under the no-causes rule, which is a retention and let-down risk, and its Shorts cousins (S007, S019) are weak.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. The hook misreads the denominator (46 per 1,000 people in the labour force, 4.6%, not per 1,000 rural men), puzzle three leans on a single noisy year of negative net fixed capital formation that invites a political 'Kerala model' reading, and a 'why' title with causes withheld will read as clickbait.
- **Red team (viewer):** drop. It is a Kerala explainer that promises 'why is Kerala different' and by its own rules withholds the why, so a far-state viewer gets a list of puzzles and no payoff.

### L012. Is South India Really Ahead of North India? 12 Official Numbers

- **Original title:** North vs South India: Who Leads on What? 12 Official Numbers
- **Status:** Shortlisted, dropped by the red team (2 of 3 said no). **Score** 5.9 (virality 7, appeal 7, distinct 4, fit 5, risk medium)
- **Series:** Who Leads on What
- **Template:** new: 16:9 narrated map-and-dot-plot scoreboard (one dot per state, coloured by region), built on the Vox explainer engine
- **Hook:** Is the South really ahead of the North? We checked 12 official numbers, and the answer changes with the number.
- **Outline:** Target 16 minutes. 1. Cold open and the rules (1.5): regions follow the Home Ministry's Zonal Councils (South = Southern Zonal Council, North = Northern plus Central), every state a dot, no winner declared. 2. Money (2.5): income per person 2024-25 (Delhi, Telangana, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu, Haryana lead; South has 3 of the top 5) and the rural construction wage (Kerala 922.6 a day against Jammu and Kashmir 584.5). 3. Health (2): infant deaths (Kerala 5; Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh 37), life expectancy (Himachal Pradesh, Jammu and Kashmir, Delhi sit between Kerala and Tamil Nadu), SDG score (Kerala and Uttarakhand tied at 79). 4. Work (2.5): women in the workforce, PLFS 2025 (top five are Sikkim, Himachal Pradesh, Nagaland, Meghalaya, Arunachal; best Southern state Tamil Nadu 47.3, 13th) and unemployment (Gujarat 0.9, Madhya Pradesh 1.5, Karnataka 2.3 against Kerala 4.4, Telangana 5.0). 5. Farms, power, phones (3): rice yield (Punjab 4,428, Andhra Pradesh 3,928), electricity per person (Goa 3,417.6, Punjab 2,503.4, Telangana 2,306.1, Haryana 2,293.5), 5G villages (Haryana 96.8, Bihar 95.6, Kerala 94.4 against Tamil Nadu 56.9, Telangana 19.1). 6. Factories and banks (2): factories (Tamil Nadu 40,121; no Northern state in the top five), credit-deposit ratio (Andhra Pradesh 151.1, Tamil Nadu 115.4, Telangana 111.6). 7. The scoreboard (1.5): the dots overlap, who leads where, why no single number settles it. 8. Caveats and your state's row (1): years differ, missing states, 'name the round you would add'.
- **Who shares it and why:** A Southern engineer and a Northern colleague each forward the rounds their side won, and the comments fill with 'you left out X' and each viewer's own state row.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: regional identity and rivalry. A Southern engineer sends it to Northern colleagues and the reverse; each side finds rounds to quote. Expected comments: 'you left out X', 'population-weighted?', 'delimitation', and each viewer's own state row. Share trigger: the moment the Southern dots lose a round (women in work, 5G) and the Northern dots lose another (factories, banks). Clip each round as a Short.
- **Why it could flop:** Every measured North-vs-South hit (63% at 3x+) came from a presenter-led channel, and a faceless 16-minute video read by a synthetic voice has no precedent; retention across 12 indicators is the main risk to watch hours. Big-channel versions reached only 0.8x-2.6x their median, and the 2026 delimitation news may have inflated the sample. The 'no winner' structure removes the verdict tension that the viral titles ('South is ahead') run on, and the rules bar that wording. The topic is crowded (Dhruv Rathee 12.9M), and defining 'North' through Zonal Councils will itself start an argument. It is the most expensive build (effort high) and needs a new long-form format.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Income per person 2024-25 (Rs): Delhi 493,024; Telangana 387,623; Karnataka 380,906; Tamil Nadu 361,619; Haryana 353,182. Rural construction wage 2024-25 (Rs/day): Kerala 922.6; Jammu and Kashmir 584.5; Tamil Nadu 583.2; Himachal Pradesh 557.9; Andhra Pradesh 553.9. Infant mortality 2023: Kerala 5; UP, MP, Chhattisgarh 37. Life expectancy 2019-23: Kerala 75.1; Himachal Pradesh 74.4; Jammu and Kashmir 74.4; Delhi 74.2; Tamil Nadu 73.4. SDG 2023: Kerala 79, Uttarakhand 79, Tamil Nadu 78. PLFS 2025 women's LFPR: Himachal Pradesh 64.7; Tamil Nadu 47.3; Delhi 13.1. Unemployment 2025: Gujarat 0.9; Madhya Pradesh 1.5; Karnataka 2.3; Kerala 4.4; Telangana 5.0; India 3.1. Rice yield 2024-25 (kg/ha): Punjab 4,428; Andhra Pradesh 3,928. Power per person 2024-25 (kWh): Goa 3,417.6; Punjab 2,503.4; Telangana 2,306.1; Haryana 2,293.5. 5G villages: Haryana 96.8%; Bihar 95.6%; Kerala 94.4%; Punjab 93.1%; Tamil Nadu 56.9%; Telangana 19.1%. Factories 2023-24: Tamil Nadu 40,121. Credit-deposit ratio 2025 (%): Andhra Pradesh 151.1; Tamil Nadu 115.4; Telangana 111.6.
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. Income, wage, infant mortality, life expectancy, SDG, PLFS 2025 LFPR and unemployment, rice yield, power, factories, credit-deposit ratio (T019, T112, T004, T007, T106, T080, T138, T116, T153) and 5G (mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json) all match. The share trigger that Southern dots 'lose' 5G is not supported (South median 61.3% vs North plus Central 54.0%, Puducherry 100%), women's LFPR flips by year (2025 median 43.0 vs 42.0; 2023-24 42.0 vs 48.2), and the Zonal Council definition is not in SOURCE_MAP and differs from LG13's Northern-only zone.
- **Corrected numbers:** 5G village share (DoT, Feb 2026): South median 61.3% (Kerala 94.4, Puducherry 100, Telangana 19.1) vs North plus Central median 54.0%. Women's LFPR South median 2025 43.0, 2023-24 42.0; North plus Central 42.0 and 48.2. Rural wage J&K 584.5 vs TN 583.2 is a 0.2% tie.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T019, T112, T004, T007, T106, T080, T138, T116, T153; public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (LFPR and UR 2025); public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json. Region definition: Zonal Councils of the Ministry of Home Affairs / Inter-State Council Secretariat (not in SOURCE_MAP.md; to be read and cited). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** North-vs-South is the strongest single bucket in the long-form research: 19 videos on 8 channels, 63% at 3x+ their channel median, 37% at 10x+, median 4.4x, with 380K-1.1M views on channels of only 5K-28K subscribers; search demand is proven (Dhruv Rathee's 'Why is South India more Developed than North India?' 12.9M; 'north india vs south india development comparison' is a typed query). A verdict frame ('who leads on what') lifted place titles to 43.5% at 3x+. Our edge is an official-source scoreboard where the South does not win everything.
- **Risks:** The topic touches delimitation and language politics: no party, no language, no 'better' or 'best' wording, and no population-growth or fertility round. Big-channel versions only reached 0.8x-2.6x their median; expect less than the small-channel results. Delhi, Telangana and Karnataka are within 2% on income per person (Telangana is 1.8% ahead of Karnataka), so no '#2' claim. Gujarat has no 2024-25 income figure, indicators use different years, and dots are unweighted by population: say all three on screen. Verify Zonal Council membership at publication. Hindi-belt viewers may feel targeted, so show the North's wins first in two rounds.
- **Effort:** high
- **Red team (growth):** drop. Right bucket, wrong build: 12 indicators with no declared winner, a 16-minute faceless synthetic-voice cut with no precedent, high effort, and a zone definition that is itself an argument; re-cut as a verdict-led video after the first duel proves out.
- **Red team (accuracy):** drop. Its own recompute contradicts the storyline: South's median 5G (61.3%) beats North plus Central (54.0%) and the women's labour-force winner flips between 2023-24 and 2025, so 'the South loses 5G and women in work' would need correcting; it also depends on a typed zone list in the delimitation-politics zone.
- **Red team (viewer):** keep. North vs South is the one long-form fight people from my side will forward ('we lead on these'), UP is counted as North so it is in the game, and no winner is declared; open with the rounds the North wins, never single out UP or Bihar as the foil, and make sure Assam and the Northeast appear as dots.

### L028. Tamil Nadu Has the Most Factories, Gujarat the Most Output. How?

- **Original title:** Tamil Nadu Has the Most Factories. Gujarat Has More Factory Output.
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5.9 (virality 6, appeal 6, distinct 7, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Place Stories
- **Template:** existing: Vox explainer engine (16:9), plus a new three-state scoreboard scene (count, jobs, output, exports).
- **Hook:** Tamil Nadu has the most factories in India. Gujarat's factories produce more. How can both be true?
- **Outline:** Target 13 min. 1) Cold open: three numbers, three different leaders (factories, workers, output; T116, T122, T126). 2) What the table counts: the Annual Survey of Industries, what a factory is, gross output vs value added (T127). 3) Size of a factory: output per worker, derived: Gujarat ₹1.33 crore, Maharashtra ₹1.20 crore, Tamil Nadu ₹62.6 lakh. 4) Jobs: Tamil Nadu's 24.8 lakh factory workers are 15.9% of India's. 5) Exports: Gujarat, Maharashtra, Tamil Nadu (T181). 6) Beyond the factory gate: bank lending ₹115 per ₹100 of deposits (T153) and renewable capacity of 19,983 MW (T143). 7) The ranking depends on the question: count, jobs, output or exports. 8) Viewer pick and next state pair.
- **Who shares it and why:** Tamil Nadu professionals and the diaspora share 'we have the most factories and workers' while Gujarat business groups reply with the output number, and both camps argue in the comments.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: identity and pride plus friendly rivalry plus a belief overturn. Tamil Nadu professionals and diaspora share it, Gujarat business groups argue back. Expect 'refineries inflate Gujarat', 'Tamil Nadu is labour-intensive', 'per worker', 'what about Maharashtra', and a pull for the next pair. Trigger frame: three leader cards, three different states.
- **Why it could flop:** The hook promises an answer the tables cannot give. Why a Gujarat factory produces about twice what a Tamil Nadu factory does (capital-intensive refining and chemicals) is not in T116-T127, and the rules forbid attributing causes, so the 'how can both be true?' payoff is just 'factories differ in size'. The cold open is also wrong as written: factories (T116) and workers (T122) are both led by Tamil Nadu, so the 'three numbers, three leaders' frame only works if you add net value added (T127), where Maharashtra leads Gujarat. That is the honest and better measure, and it undercuts the headline, because Gujarat's gross output includes input costs. Thus 'Gujarat has more output' is true only for gross output. It rides on the best-performing place (Tamil Nadu: 16 videos, 75% at 3x+), but a 13-minute scoreboard of one explainable gap can feel like one idea stretched out.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Factories 2023-24 (T116): Tamil Nadu 40,121 (15.4% of India's 260,061); Gujarat 33,311; Maharashtra 26,539. Workers (T122): Tamil Nadu 2,475,675 (15.9% of 15,519,957); Gujarat 1,978,830; Maharashtra 1,849,633. Value of gross output (T126, ₹ lakh): Gujarat 263,949,936 (17.2% of 1,532,716,609); Maharashtra 221,850,214; Tamil Nadu 154,939,545 (10.1%). Net value added (T127): Maharashtra 3.4146e+07, Gujarat 2.90993e+07, Tamil Nadu 2.13956e+07. Exports 2024-25 (T181, USD million): Gujarat 116,332; Maharashtra 65,855; Tamil Nadu 52,074. Bank credit-deposit ratio 2025 (T153): Tamil Nadu 115.4, India 80.1. Renewable capacity 2024 (T143): Tamil Nadu 19,983 MW.
- **Data editor:** numbers corrected by the data editor. T116, T122, T126, T127, T181, T153 and T143 recompute exactly (TN 40,121 factories and 2,475,675 workers, Gujarat gross output 263,949,936 lakh, output per worker Rs 1.33 crore, 1.20 crore and 62.6 lakh), but TN's worker share is 15.95% (16.0%, not 15.9%). The cold open 'three numbers, three leaders' is wrong for factories, workers and output (Tamil Nadu, Tamil Nadu, Gujarat is two leaders); use net value added (T127), where Maharashtra leads Gujarat by 17%, and say 'Gujarat produces more' holds for gross output (19% over Maharashtra) and against Tamil Nadu on both measures.
- **Corrected numbers:** Leaders: factories TN 40,121; workers TN 2,475,675 (16.0% of India); gross output Gujarat 263,949,936 lakh; net value added Maharashtra 34,145,981 lakh vs Gujarat 29,099,304 vs TN 21,395,650; exports Gujarat 116,332 USD m.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T116, T122, T126, T127, T181, T153, T143 under public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ (Annual Survey of Industries, MoSPI; exports and other agencies per table notes). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Tamil Nadu is the most reliable place in the long-form data: 16 videos, 75% at 3x+, median 6.6x. Lookk's 'How Tamil Nadu Became India's Fastest Growing State?' did 426K (294x), The Indian Insight's Tamil Nadu factory video 307K (9.9x), and Angel One's 'Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu: Whose Model is Winning?' 1.1M (136x). Verdict and question frames hit 3x in 43.5% of cases. The count-versus-output twist adds a belief-overturn to a place story.
- **Risks:** Do not attribute causes (policy 'models', parties or governments). Why the counts and outputs differ (capital-intensive industries such as refining and chemicals) is not in these tables: say so, or add an industry-wise ASI source (mapped, not collected). Lead checks: Tamil Nadu over Gujarat on factories is 2.6 points of India's total and on workers 3.2 points (passes); manufacturing value added Maharashtra vs Tamil Nadu is only about 2.7% apart (do not name a leader there). The Annual Survey of Industries covers registered factories only. Label each year (2023-24 for the survey, 2024-25 for exports).
- **Effort:** medium

### L014. When Did Your State Become Your State? India's Map, Year by Year

- **Original title:** How India's Map Was Drawn: Every State's Birthday, Year by Year
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5.5 (virality 6, appeal 6, distinct 7, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Template:** new: 'every year' animated map on today's official boundary with a big year counter; each state is painted the year it reached its present form and stays neutral grey before that
- **Hook:** India's states were not all here in 1950. Watch the map fill in, one birthday at a time.
- **Outline:** Target 12 minutes (all years and appointed days to be confirmed against each Act before scripting). 1. Cold open (1): today's official outline, every state grey, the counter at 1950. 2. 1950 and the Constitution's states (1.5): which areas are painted, which stay grey and why. 3. 1956 States Reorganisation Act (1.5): the language-based redraw, with the source cited on screen. 4. 1960 to 1972 (2): Bombay State into Maharashtra and Gujarat, Nagaland, Punjab and Haryana, Himachal Pradesh, Manipur, Meghalaya and Tripura. 5. 1975 to 1987 (1.5): Sikkim, then Mizoram, Arunachal Pradesh and Goa, each with its enabling Act. 6. 2000 and 2014 (1.5): Chhattisgarh, Uttarakhand, Jharkhand, then Telangana. 7. 2019 and 2020 (1): the Union Territory changes, one neutral line each taken from the Acts. 8. What the birthdays do and do not tell us (2): birth year against income per person (RBI T019) and infant deaths (T004) as a scatter, saying plainly if there is no pattern. 9. Your state's birthday card (0.5) and the question 'Which birthday surprised you?'
- **Who shares it and why:** Viewers wait for their own state's year, then post 'my state turned N in year X' and tease friends from older or newer states in family and college groups, and the video cuts naturally into one Short per era.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Mechanism: how a place came to be, plus identity. People wait for their own state's birthday and post it ('my state turned N in year X'); older-state and newer-state friends tease each other; the video is cut into one Short per era. Expected comments: arguments about dates and the 'appointed day', and requests for district or city versions. Share trigger: the instant the viewer's own state is painted.
- **Why it could flop:** It is 12 minutes of Acts and dates with no number or conflict, so retention probably drops once each viewer's own state has been painted; the 1956 chapter lands many states at once and turns into a data dump. The 2000, 2014 and 2019 chapters (Chhattisgarh, Telangana, and J&K and Ladakh if included) are hard to narrate neutrally, there is no search-demand evidence for the topic in long form, every year and appointed day is still unread (needs_reading), and high effort against a 5-7 hour week is a real production risk.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** 
- **Data editor:** source exists, numbers not re-checked. No numbers are cited, and the formation dates and Acts are in no local file: SOURCE_MAP.md lists only the Home Affairs annual report and Census, not the Reorganisation Acts, India Code or the Gazette, so they must be added and read before scripting. The dates are public record and the 'now' chapter (T019, T004) is in hand, but the demand evidence is Shorts-only (research_shorts_india.md) and the long-form 'every year' precedent uses historical borders we cannot copy.
- **Source:** States Reorganisation Act 1956 and the later state and Union Territory Acts, from India Code and the Gazette of India (primary documents not in SOURCE_MAP.md; to be added and read); Ministry of Home Affairs for the current list of states and Union Territories. The 'now' chapter uses RBI Handbook T019 and T004, in hand. (answer PDF still to read)
- **Evidence it could work:** Origin hooks are the most reliable Indian map hook: 15 'how X became part of India' Shorts had a median of 383K with 67% at 100K+, Yash Rajput's 21 history-of-borders Shorts had a median of 1.3M against a channel median of 139K, and state formation/history/what-if Shorts reached 1M+ in 26.7% of cases against 12.4%. In long-form, Ollie Bye's 'History of India: Every Year' got 9.4M (45x its median) and broad-subject 'every year' maps have a 587K median; 12-20 minute place videos hit 3x+ in 45-48% of cases.
- **Risks:** Only the present official boundary may be shown: never draw historical borders (the official-boundary rule is why Ollie Bye's version is not copyable). Painting by year of the present state, with grey before, must not imply the land was empty. Episodes like Goa, Sikkim, Hyderabad and Jammu and Kashmir involve force or disputes: give the legal date and the Act only, no commentary. 'Born' dates differ between assent, appointed day and commencement: pick one rule and say it on screen. No religion, caste or party content. The count of states and Union Territories must be re-checked against Ministry of Home Affairs at publication.
- **Effort:** high

### L058. Tamil Nadu: India's Most Factories, but 27th of 35 on One Chart

- **Original title:** Tamil Nadu in 20 Charts: Where It Leads, Where It Doesn't
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5.5 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 4, fit 7, risk low)
- **Series:** [State] in 20 Charts
- **Template:** new: State Report Card 16:9 on Long Kit v1. 12 fixed 'spine' charts plus 8 'surprise' charts chosen by report_card.py: for a state S it ranks S on every indicator in the RBI Handbook, NFHS-6 and PLFS, subtracts S's rank on income per person, and keeps the 4 biggest positive and 4 biggest negative gaps that have at least 20 states reporting and no thin lead. The surprise block makes every episode different, which answers the template-factory risk. One JSON config per state.
- **Hook:** Tamil Nadu has India's most factories, a birth rate half of Bihar's, and one chart where it ranks 27th of 35.
- **Outline:** Target 18 min. Ch1 Money (3 charts, 3 min): income per person and growth since 2011-12 (T019), size of the economy (T021), exports (T181). Ch2 Make (3 charts, 3 min): what the economy is made of (T029, T041, T049), factories and workers (T116, T122). Ch3 People (3 charts, 3 min): birth rate and children per woman (T002, T006), infant deaths 2004 to 2023 (T004), life expectancy (T007). Ch4 Work and women (2 charts, 2 min): women in the labour force 2017-18 to 2023-24 (PLFS), rural wage (T112). Ch5 The state budget (1 chart, 1.5 min): interest and pensions as a share of revenue spending (T166, T170, T171). Ch6 The 8 surprise charts, four each way (5.5 min), ending on the one where the state ranks near the bottom. Ch7 Your turn: pause-and-guess on where your own state would rank. FIRST TEN in order: 1 Tamil Nadu, 2 Kerala (largest 'why is Kerala' search cluster), 3 Karnataka, 4 Maharashtra, 5 Gujarat, 6 Uttar Pradesh, 7 Bihar, 8 West Bengal, 9 Telangana, 10 Rajasthan; then the other states and UTs, about 36 in all.
- **Who shares it and why:** A Tamil viewer sends the 'where it leads' half to family and the '27th of 35' chart to a friend to argue about.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Identity and pride plus 'your state' personalisation. A Tamil viewer shares the 'leads' half with family and the 'doesn't' half with a friend to argue; each later state brings its own audience, and people ask for theirs in comments ('do Kerala next', 'Odisha when?'), which becomes the content calendar. Share trigger: the opening three-fact line and the final ranked strip of all 35 states with the home state highlighted.
- **Why it could flop:** The one natural experiment shows single-state profiles lose to duels (34K to 55K against 494K to 1.1M for head-to-heads), and 20 charts in 18 minutes with no opponent and no verdict is a long watch. A fixed '[State] in 20 Charts' series is exactly the template-factory pattern, the automated rank-gap logic may surface dull charts, and many Tamil Nadu searchers want Tamil-language videos.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Birth rate 2023 (T002): Tamil Nadu 12.0, Bihar 25.8 per thousand. Factories 2023-24 (T116): Tamil Nadu 40,121, Gujarat 33,311. Factory workers (T122): Tamil Nadu 2.47568 million, Gujarat 1.97883 million. GSDP 2024-25 (T021): Maharashtra 4.53152e+08 lakh rupees, Tamil Nadu 3.11859e+08 lakh, Uttar Pradesh 2.97822e+08 lakh. Women in the labour force: Tamil Nadu 33.7% to 43.2%, India 23.3% to 41.7% (2017-18 to 2023-24). Women who have used the internet (NFHS-6): Tamil Nadu 59.5% (27th of 35), India 64.3%. Infant mortality: Tamil Nadu 41 (2004) to 12 (2023). Interest as a share of revenue spending 2024-25 BE: Tamil Nadu 17.9% (computed from T170 over T166). Domestic tourist visits 2024 (T013): Tamil Nadu 306.84 million, Karnataka 304.56 million, Uttar Pradesh 646.81 million. Exports 2024-25: Tamil Nadu USD 52,074 million.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T002, T004, T006, T007, T019, T021, T029, T041, T049, T112, T116, T122, T166, T170, T171, T181, T013; plfs_annual.json (LFPR); nfhs6_states.json (indicator 14 and a pool of 101 for the surprise charts). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Launch slot 3. Tamil Nadu is the best-performing place in the long-form sample (75% of 16 videos reached 3x their channel median, research_long_india.md; Lookk 'How Tamil Nadu Became India's Fastest Growing State?' 426K, 294x) and 116 data-relevant YouTube suggestions mention it. A fixed state-by-state series has the highest floor seen in Shorts (Geo Study state series: 24 Shorts, median 982K, minimum 212K), and every new state is a fresh 'my state' landing page.
- **Risks:** Template-factory risk is the main one: keep the surprise block automatic, vary the cold open per state, and never reuse the same script skeleton word for word. Avoid '#1' claims on thin leads: Tamil Nadu and Maharashtra industry value added are within about 2% (T041), the birth rate gap to Kerala is only 0.3 per thousand, and Tamil Nadu and Karnataka tourist visits are within 1%, so none of those may be called first. Bihar, West Bengal and Punjab videos under-performed in the sample, so run them as trust-building search landings, not as breakout bets. Chart data from different years must carry the year on screen.
- **Effort:** medium

### L043. Tamil Nadu Has More Factories Than Gujarat. Gujarat Exports 2.2x More

- **Original title:** Tamil Nadu vs Gujarat: Who Employs More, Who Exports More?
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5.4 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 6, fit 6, risk medium)
- **Series:** Rivals
- **Template:** new: DK Duel Long (the template built for idea 1, built first here if this ships first): split screen, two state outlines, one question per chapter, running tally, source and period tag on every number
- **Hook:** Spoken: 'Tamil Nadu has more factories and more factory workers than Gujarat. Gujarat exported 2.2 times as much.' On screen: 40,121 vs 33,311 factories, then US$52 billion vs US$116 billion.
- **Outline:** Target 15 min. 1. Cold open and the three claims (1 min): factories, exports, income. 2. Income per person (2): latest year both have is 2022-23; current prices Tamil Nadu 275,272 vs Gujarat 272,451 (1.0% apart, treat as level); constant prices Gujarat 181,963 vs Tamil Nadu 163,205. 3. Factories and jobs (3): 40,121 vs 33,311 factories; 2,475,675 vs 1,978,830 workers; shares of India's 15,519,957 factory workers 16.0% vs 12.8%; Tamil Nadu's factory lead shrank from 14,776 (2011-12) to 6,810 (2023-24). 4. Machines and output (3): fixed capital 3.74 vs 9.03 lakh crore rupees; gross value added 2.52 vs 3.49 lakh crore; value added per worker about 10.2 vs 17.6 lakh rupees (derived). 5. Pay (2): total factory emoluments are almost identical, 94,315 vs 94,059 crore rupees, split across 25% more workers in Tamil Nadu. 6. Exports (2): US$ million 52,074 vs 116,332 in 2024-25 and 43,556 vs 134,403 in 2023-24; what the table does not say is what is exported. 7. Verdict and what data cannot prove (1.5): Tamil Nadu employs more, Gujarat invests and exports more, per-person income is level. 8. Which should we compare next? (0.5)
- **Who shares it and why:** Tamil Nadu and Gujarat alumni, engineering and MBA groups send the 40,121 vs 33,311 factories line to a rival-state friend, who answers with the US$116 billion export line.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Friendly rivalry plus a surprise. Shared in Tamil Nadu and Gujarat family and alumni groups and among engineering and MBA students. Comments will be 'Gujarat exports are mostly petroleum', 'Tamil Nadu jobs matter more', 'where is Maharashtra?'. Share trigger frame: the split screen 'Factories: Tamil Nadu 40,121, Gujarat 33,311. Exports: US$52B vs US$116B' followed by 'income per person: 1% apart'.
- **Why it could flop:** The export half of the surprise is exposed. The idea itself expects the comment 'Gujarat exports are mostly petroleum', and the commodity mix is not collected, so the video looks naive on its own best number. The 2.2x holds only for 2024-25; 2023-24 was US$134.4 billion vs 43.6 billion, about 3.1x. Tamil Nadu vs Gujarat is a live party-politics argument, so comments will turn political despite neutral wording, and the owner is in Tamil Nadu, which invites a bias charge. Mid-profile duels have flopped (Infra Talks: Maharashtra vs Gujarat 22K, Gujarat vs Rajasthan 23K). The 15-minute script stacks seven or more tables on registered factories only, and the per-person tie uses stale 2022-23 data because Gujarat's series stops there.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Factories 2023-24 (T116): Tamil Nadu 40,121, Gujarat 33,311. Workers (T122): 2,475,675 vs 1,978,830; India 15,519,957. Fixed capital (T117, rupees lakh): 37,427,225 vs 90,327,908; India 462,409,035. Gross value added (T128): 25,217,514 vs 34,920,541 lakh. Total emoluments (T124): 9,431,535 vs 9,405,891 lakh. Exports (T181, USD million): 2024-25 Tamil Nadu 52,074, Gujarat 116,332; 2023-24 43,556 vs 134,403. Per-person NSDP 2022-23, current prices (T019): 275,272 vs 272,451. Factory counts 2011-12: Tamil Nadu 36,996, Gujarat 22,220.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ T116, T117, T122, T123, T124, T126, T128, T181, T019, T020, T021. Factory tables are the Annual Survey of Industries as compiled by RBI (original publisher MoSPI, SOURCE_MAP: MoSPI eSankhyiki). Export commodity mix would come from Commerce NIRYAT / Tradestat (SOURCE_MAP, not collected). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Tamil Nadu is the best-performing named state in the long-form research (16 videos on 8 channels, 75% at 3x+, median 6.6x). Head-to-heads between big economies matched one channel's best (Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu 473K, Maharashtra vs Gujarat 474K). A 'people vs capital' paradox with a 1% per-person tie is a surprise that overturns the belief that industrial Gujarat simply outranks Tamil Nadu.
- **Risks:** Gujarat's GSDP and per-person series end at 2022-23 in the RBI tables, so compare both states in 2022-23. The factory tables cover registered factories only (no informal or small units) and 'workers' differs from 'persons engaged'. Gujarat exports fell between 2023-24 and 2024-25, so show both years; check whether T181 is goods only and what is in it before claiming anything about the mix. Duels between mid-profile states flopped in research (Maharashtra vs Gujarat 22K), so Tamil Nadu is the draw. Do not claim 'better'. Related NFHS Shorts exist (S054, S055, S060, S064); link, do not repeat.
- **Effort:** high

### L075. Which Country Is Your State's Economy as Big as?

- **Original title:** If Indian States Were Countries: Where Would Each One Rank?
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5.4 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 6, fit 6, risk medium)
- **Series:** State vs World (refreshed when the World Bank and RBI publish new data)
- **Template:** new: DK Country Ladder (16:9, 12-14 min; a ranked world bar ladder where a state's bar slides into place among countries, with the state lit on the official-boundary map).
- **Hook:** Maharashtra's economy is Rs 45.32 lakh crore a year. Which country is that size? Pause.
- **Outline:** Target 13 minutes. 1) The rules: GSDP at current prices for 2024-25 converted at the World Bank's official exchange rate, against World Bank GDP in current US dollars; economy-sized comparison only, not statehood. 2) The big four: Maharashtra, Tamil Nadu, Uttar Pradesh, Karnataka. 3) The middle: West Bengal, Rajasthan, Telangana, Andhra Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh, Kerala. 4) The small: Goa, Sikkim, Arunachal Pradesh. 5) People instead of money: state populations vs country populations. 6) Per person: state vs country income per person. 7) Why the comparison has limits: nominal vs PPP, no foreign policy, no trade of its own. 8) Your state's economic twin.
- **Who shares it and why:** State-group members post 'my state is the size of X' and forward it to a rival state's group with 'see where yours lands'.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Pride and scale made literal. Shared in state groups as 'my state is the size of X', then forwarded to rivals ('see where yours lands'). Comments: 'PPP would change this', 'my state is bigger than X', 'where is Gujarat'. The share frame is the state's bar landing between two countries, with the state's twin named on screen.
- **Why it could flop:** The proven hits used the comparators the risk note drops. Tamil Nadu vs Pakistan (135K, 9x) and Delhi vs Dhaka (111K, 7x) carried the pattern. The same channel's later copies fell to 10K and 6.7K, and Data Duck's flat country comparisons sit at 15K-41K. Nobody types 'indian state vs country' (autocomplete returned nothing). It is a one-trick reveal stretched to 13 minutes, so viewers may leave after their own state's bar lands. The World Bank numbers are not collected, and ranks move with the exchange-rate choice, so the first comment will be 'PPP would change this'. A new ladder template is high effort. It would probably work better as Shorts, with the long video as a compilation.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** GSDP 2024-25 (T021, current prices): Maharashtra Rs 45.32 lakh crore; Tamil Nadu 31.19; Uttar Pradesh 29.78; Karnataka 28.84; West Bengal 18.15; Rajasthan 17.04; Telangana 16.41; Andhra Pradesh 15.93; Madhya Pradesh 15.03; Kerala 12.49. Uttar Pradesh implied population 2023-24: about 237.1 million (computed from T023 / T019; replace with Census or UN number). US-dollar and country numbers must come from the World Bank.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T021 (GSDP 2024-25), T019, T023 (local). NOT YET COLLECTED: World Bank API GDP (current US$), GDP per capita, population and official exchange rate (SOURCE_MAP: World Bank API); Census or UN WPP for populations. (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** Scale made literal is a measured pattern: World In Details' 'Tamil Nadu vs Pakistan | State vs Country' got 135K (9x its median) and 'Delhi vs Dhaka' 111K (7x); Map Motion's 'This Indian State Has a Country's Population' reached 1.6M; Wawamu's 'India States vs China Provinces GDP' got 600K (2.7x). The same channel's later copies decayed (Gujarat vs Pakistan 10K, UP vs Pakistan 6.7K = 0.4x), which is why this is one definitive ladder, not a series of pairs, and Data Duck's flat country comparisons sit at 15K-41K, so execution matters.
- **Risks:** Avoid the secession framing the what-if hits use ('became an independent country'): say 'economy-sized' and show it is not statehood. Skip Pakistan and Bangladesh as comparators so it cannot read as mockery. Currency conversion choices move ranks, so show the rate used and flag any near-tie (no '#N' claim on a thin gap). GSDP and national GDP are close but not identical concepts. Gujarat 2024-25 is missing. Needs World Bank data collected and re-checked.
- **Effort:** high

### L079. Richer Delhi: 18.5% of Women in the Labour Force. Meghalaya: 71.8%

- **Original title:** Women in India's Labour Force: Meghalaya 71.8%, Delhi 18.5%
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5.4 (virality 5, appeal 7, distinct 7, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Template:** Mapped (existing, re-cut to 16:9) plus Change (existing), inside the new DK Long Map Explainer.
- **Hook:** In Delhi, 18.5% of women are in the labour force. In Meghalaya, 71.8%. Delhi is more than three times richer per person.
- **Outline:** Target 12 minutes. 1) What the number means: women aged 15+ working or looking for work, usual status (PLFS definition; read the MoSPI note). 2) India's climb: 23.3% (2017-18) to 41.7% (2023-24); calendar 2025 is 40.0%, a different basis. 3) The map 2023-24: Meghalaya, Sikkim, Himachal Pradesh, Arunachal Pradesh, Nagaland lead; Delhi, Haryana, Goa, Bihar at the bottom. 4) Biggest risers: Arunachal Pradesh 14.7 to 66.5, Nagaland 16.7 to 64.3, Assam 12.7 to 50.2. 5) Richer is not more: the rank correlation between income per person and women's participation is about -0.36 across 33 states and UTs. 6) The gap with men: Punjab 31.1 vs 79.8, Meghalaya 71.8 vs 82.3. 7) What counts as work: the mix of self-employed, regular wage and unpaid family helpers (to read in the MoSPI PLFS report). 8) Your state's story, and the next PLFS annual release (25 March 2027).
- **Who shares it and why:** North-east and hill-state viewers share it as 'see where we stand', women's workplace and college groups circulate it around 8 March, and Delhi viewers argue about why the city is so low.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A surprise that overturns a belief (richer does not mean more women in the labour force) plus north-east pride. Shared by north-east and hill-state audiences ('see where we stand') and in women's workplace and college groups on 8 March. Comments: 'why is Delhi so low', 'this is unpaid work', 'safety'. The trigger frame is the income bar and the participation bar flipping for Delhi and Meghalaya.
- **Why it could flop:** Labour force participation is not paid work. The high states are likely driven by farm and unpaid family work, so the 'north-east women lead' pride share gets answered by 'this is unpaid work', which the idea itself predicts. Arunachal Pradesh moving from 14.7 to 66.5 and Nagaland from 16.7 to 64.3 in six years looks like a survey artefact a sharp viewer will call out. The topic alone is average: PolyMatter's women-unemployment video got 540K, 0.6x its median. The rank correlation of about -0.36 is modest, so 'richer is not more' is a soft claim. The 2023-24 and calendar-2025 figures use different bases (Delhi is 13.1 in 2025), which invites 'you mixed data' comments. Delhi's low number pulls the thread to safety and culture.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Women's labour force participation, 15+, usual status: India 23.3% (2017-18) to 41.7% (2023-24); 40.0% in calendar 2025. 2023-24: Meghalaya 71.8; Sikkim 68.2; Himachal Pradesh 67.6; Arunachal Pradesh 66.5; Nagaland 64.3; Delhi 18.5; Haryana 24.2; Goa 29.3; Bihar 30.5; Lakshadweep 17.1. Changes 2017-18 to 2023-24: Arunachal Pradesh 14.7 to 66.5; Nagaland 16.7 to 64.3; Assam 12.7 to 50.2; Tripura 12.5 to 47.1; Jharkhand 15.4 to 49.8; Goa 30.9 to 29.3; Delhi 14.3 to 18.5. Men: Punjab women 31.1 vs men 79.8; Meghalaya women 71.8 vs men 82.3. Income per person 2023-24: Delhi 4,59,408 vs Meghalaya 1,41,195 (3.25x). Rank correlation of income and women's participation: about -0.36, n=33 (computed here; re-check).
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** PLFS annual JSON public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (LFPR by sex, sector, state, 2017-18 to 2025); RBI T019 for income; out/data_leads_extra.txt for the 2017-18 to 2023-24 changes. Status-in-employment tables need reading in the MoSPI PLFS annual report (SOURCE_MAP: MoSPI reports). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Honest read: the topic alone is average (PolyMatter's 'Why 75% of Indian Women are Unemployed' got 540K, 0.6x its median). The lift has to come from the data surprise and 'your state' pride: state-data paradox hooks are a measured breakout type (FactTechz 4.6M in 9 days) and personalised hooks drew 7-12x the median comment rate. Timing: Women's Day (8 March) and the PLFS annual release on 25 March 2027.
- **Risks:** Labour force participation is not 'working for pay': it includes job-seekers and unpaid family workers, so the title and narration must say 'labour force'. The 2023-24 and calendar-2025 figures are on different bases, and Delhi is 13.1 in 2025: never mix them in one ranking. Lakshadweep (17.1) is lower than Delhi, so do not say Delhi is lowest; small UTs are volatile. No explanation of 'why' without the MoSPI source; avoid blame or stereotypes about any state or community.
- **Effort:** medium

### L044. Karnataka Grew Faster for 11 Years. Then Tamil Nadu Did for 2

- **Original title:** Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu: Who Grew Faster? Depends on the Years
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5.3 (virality 6, appeal 6, distinct 5, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Rivals
- **Template:** new: DK Duel Long (shared template) with a 'pick your window' growth-race chart: three time windows on one line chart, a switch between windows
- **Hook:** Spoken: 'Karnataka beat Tamil Nadu on growth for eleven years. Then in two years Tamil Nadu grew 10.2% a year, and Karnataka 6.7%.' On screen: a growth-rank ladder that flips when the window changes.
- **Outline:** Target 16 min. 1. The question: whose model is winning? (1.5 min). 2. Per person, then and now (2): constant prices, Tamil Nadu 93,112 vs Karnataka 90,263 in 2011-12; Karnataka 204,605 vs Tamil Nadu 197,747 in 2024-25 (3.5% apart, level). 3. Eleven years, 2011-12 to 2022-23 (3): GSDP growth per year at constant prices, Karnataka 7.77% (3rd of 33 states and UTs), Tamil Nadu 5.99% (13th). 4. The last two years (3): Tamil Nadu 10.22% (2nd of 25), Karnataka 6.67% (20th). 5. Two models (3): manufacturing value added 2024-25 Tamil Nadu 5.27 vs Karnataka 3.24 crore-lakh-rupees per T033; services Karnataka 1.79 vs Tamil Nadu 1.55; factory workers 2.48 million vs 0.97 million. 6. Is Tamil Nadu India's fastest-growing state? (1.5): rank depends on the window; level with Bihar and Meghalaya over two years. 7. Verdict and the line to watch next December (1).
- **Who shares it and why:** Bengaluru and Chennai professionals send it to each other with their own window ('my eleven years' versus 'your last two'), because the winner flips with the window chosen.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A strong but fair question whose answer depends on the viewer's chosen window, which invites argument. Shared between Bengaluru and Chennai professionals and by each side's fans. Comments: 'which window is fair?', 'Bengaluru has the startups', 'Tamil Nadu has the factories'. Share trigger line: 'Pick your window and you pick your winner.'
- **Why it could flop:** The exact pair and verdict frame already exist as Angel One's 1.1M-view 'Whose Model is Winning?' (plus Adarsh Gupta's 473K), so we arrive second. The current title gives away the non-answer ('depends on the years'), which removes the curiosity gap, and viewers who click for a verdict get 'it depends'. Tamil Nadu's 10.22% over two years rests on 2024-25 values that may be provisional and later revised, and the windows are our own CAGRs, so 'you picked those windows' is the obvious attack even with all three shown. The per-person lead is only 3.5%, so no 'richer' claim is available. The owner being in Tamil Nadu raises the bias question.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** GSDP growth per year, constant prices (T022, my CAGR): 2011-12 to 2022-23 Karnataka 7.77%, Tamil Nadu 5.99%; 2022-23 to 2024-25 Tamil Nadu 10.22%, Karnataka 6.67%, Bihar 10.28%, Meghalaya 10.15%. Per-person NSDP constant prices (T020): 2011-12 Tamil Nadu 93,112, Karnataka 90,263; 2024-25 Karnataka 204,605, Tamil Nadu 197,747. Manufacturing GSVA 2024-25 (current prices, rupees lakh): Tamil Nadu 5.27293e7, Karnataka 3.24418e7. Services GSVA: Karnataka 1.79102e8, Tamil Nadu 1.5504e8. Factory workers 2023-24: Tamil Nadu 2,475,675, Karnataka 968,922.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ T022, T020, T019, T033, T049, T116, T122 (GSDP at constant prices, per-person NSDP, sector value added, factories). Annual refresh from the RBI Handbook (usually December). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Angel One's 'Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu: Whose Model is Winning?' got 1.1M views (136x its channel median) on a 28K-subscriber channel, and Lookk's 'How Tamil Nadu Became India's Fastest Growing State?' got 426K (294x). Verdict frames ('who is winning', 'whose model') hit 3x+ in 43.5% of cases in the place cluster. Ours adds a real twist the others lack: the answer flips with the years you choose.
- **Risks:** The two-year Tamil Nadu figure rests on 2024-25 values that may be provisional or advance estimates and can be revised; say so. Show all three windows, not the flattering one. The per-person lead is 3.5%, so no 'richer' claim. Sector shares computed from T029, T041 and T049 do not add to 100% of total value added in our files (categories missing), so read the table definitions before showing shares. Growth is not welfare: do not call a winner on wellbeing. Keep party and government credit out of it.
- **Effort:** medium

Telangana's 2012-13 numbers are a back-cast onto the 2014 state boundary. Keep language neutral on farm policy and protests; this is a production map only.
- **Effort:** medium

### L074. India vs China: 10 Rounds, Same Source, Same Year

- **Original title:** India vs China: 10 Rounds on the Economy and the People
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5.1 (virality 6, appeal 8, distinct 3, fit 5, risk medium)
- **Series:** India vs the World
- **Template:** new: DK Long Scoreboard (16:9, ten rounds, 'leads' badge per round, nominal and PPP both shown, no overall winner); chapters on the DK Long Map Explainer engine.
- **Hook:** India has more people than China. Does it lead in anything else? Ten rounds, same source, same year.
- **Outline:** Target 17 minutes. 1) Rules: only World Bank, UN and IMF numbers, same year for both, nominal and PPP shown side by side. 2) People: population, median age, working-age share (UN WPP). 3) Size: GDP nominal and PPP (World Bank, IMF October edition). 4) Per person: GDP per capita. 5) Growth since 1990. 6) Lives: life expectancy, fertility, infant mortality. 7) Power: electricity use per person. 8) Connected: internet use, mobile subscriptions (ITU). 9) Cities: urban share. 10) School and skills: literacy, years of schooling. 11) Scoreboard, who leads on what, and what each source cannot tell us (revisions, definitions). Optional closer: India's richest states against China's provinces only if an official provincial source is mapped.
- **Who shares it and why:** Indians forward it to friends in India-China arguments and quote the running scoreboard in the comments of other India-China videos, but only the rounds India wins invite a pride share.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** National pride and rivalry with a surprise per round: the viewer predicts the winner of each round and some answers flip. Shared with friends and in the comment sections of other India-China videos; comments will be 'PPP!', 'per capita!', 'their data is not reliable' (a risk to steer around). The frame that triggers the share is the running scoreboard that flips on a round the viewer did not expect.
- **Why it could flop:** The surprise may not exist. Most viewers already expect India to win population and China to lead most other rounds. None of the data is collected, so nobody knows the scoreboard. If China sweeps, it is a downer nobody forwards. If India's wins are thin, our no-'#1' rule forbids the headline. The 2.6x-median evidence comes from established channels (Economics Explained, Money & Macro) with authority a zero-subscriber channel lacks. The lane is crowded: 28 channels have a 1M+ India-geopolitics Short, and Dataverse's India-vs-X videos have a 33.5K median. 'No overall winner' removes the argument that fuels shares, and the comments will be 'PPP!' and 'their data is fake'. It needs three international sources cross-checked, 17 minutes of runtime and high effort.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** 
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** NOT YET COLLECTED: World Bank Open Data and API (SOURCE_MAP: World Bank Open Data, World Bank API), UN World Population Prospects (SOURCE_MAP), IMF World Economic Outlook and DataMapper (SOURCE_MAP; browser only), ITU DataHub (SOURCE_MAP), IEA or World Bank for electricity. No local numbers; an India-side check can use RBI T006/T007 but cross-country rounds must use one source for both countries. (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** India-plus-China is the strongest India hook in the global sample: median 2.6x its channel baseline across 20 titles in 15 channels, 8 of them at 3x+ (Economics Explained 'India Will Not Be The Next China' 2.9M = 4.3x; Money & Macro 1.7M = 7.0x; Wawamu 'China vs Japan vs Korea vs India: Everything Compared' 2.4M = 10.7x). Typed demand: 114 India-vs-country suggestions, mostly China ('india vs china economy comparison'). The IMF's October World Economic Outlook is a natural timing hook (verify the release date).
- **Risks:** Needs three international sources to be collected and cross-checked; use the same year and definition for both countries and show nominal and PPP. No '#1' or 'biggest' claim on a thin lead. Keep economic and demographic only: no border, military or political content, no mocking China, no comments on the reliability of Chinese statistics beyond noting source revisions. Verify the UN population-crossover statement before using it in the hook. A flat 'comparison chart' version performs poorly on Shorts (Dataverse median 33.5K), so the narration and round-by-round tension are essential.
- **Effort:** high

### L046. Bihar Is Poorer Than UP, but Fewer Babies Die There: 23 vs 37

- **Original title:** Bihar vs Uttar Pradesh: Poorer, Yet Healthier? Official Numbers
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 7, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Rivals
- **Template:** new: DK Duel Long (shared template) with a 'rate of change' scoreboard: each round shows level and change, so both states win some rounds
- **Hook:** Spoken: 'Bihar is poorer than Uttar Pradesh. But fewer babies die in Bihar: 23 per thousand against 37.' On screen: the two numbers, India's 25 as a line between them.
- **Outline:** Target 15 min. 1. Cold open: poorer yet healthier (1 min). 2. Money (2): per-person NSDP constant prices 2024-25 Uttar Pradesh 55,990 vs Bihar 36,342; poverty headcount NFHS-5 Bihar 33.76% vs Uttar Pradesh 22.93%. 3. Babies and lives (3): infant mortality 2023 23 vs 37 (2011: 44 vs 57); life expectancy 2019-23 69.3 vs 68.0 (India 70.3); fertility 2.8 vs 2.6. 4. Power (2): per head 2024-25 343.9 vs 692.2 kWh; the gap closed from 3.0x (2005-06) to 2.0x. 5. Banks and wages (2): credit-deposit ratio 2025 51.4 vs 50.9; rural construction wage 388.6 vs 389.9 rupees. 6. Growth (2): last two years Bihar 10.28% vs Uttar Pradesh 8.96%; 13-year growth 6.07% vs 6.05% (a tie). 7. Women at work (1.5): 4.1 to 30.5 vs 13.5 to 34.5 (2017-18 to 2023-24). 8. Verdict (1.5): scoreboard of levels vs rates of change, and what a sample survey cannot prove.
- **Who shares it and why:** Bihar viewers and the large Bihar diaspora send the 23-vs-37 pair to Uttar Pradesh friends as a rare 'we lead on this' stat, and Uttar Pradesh viewers reply with milk, tourism and scale.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Pride and a belief-overturning surprise. Shared by Bihar viewers and the large Bihar diaspora, and answered by Uttar Pradesh viewers with their own rounds (milk, tourism, scale). Comments: 'now compare incomes', 'Uttar Pradesh is far bigger', 'what about literacy?'. Share trigger frame: the 23 vs 37 pair with India's 25 between them.
- **Why it could flop:** Bihar-named long-form videos mostly do not over-index (17% hit 3x on 6 videos; 'Bihar vs Rest of India' got 0.3x), and mid-profile state duels flopped on Infra Talks. 'Healthier' rests on two sample-registration indicators (infant mortality, life expectancy). Bihar is worse on poverty headcount (33.76% vs 22.93%), and NFHS-6, which is in hand but not in the plan, may show Bihar worse on stunting or anaemia, which would make the claim look cherry-picked. Narrow it to 'fewer babies die' unless the wider data agrees. A Bihar vs Uttar Pradesh comment section also drifts toward stereotype and caste jabs, which the sensitivity rules oblige us to moderate.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Infant mortality 2023 (T004): Bihar 23, Uttar Pradesh 37, India 25; 2011: 44 vs 57. Life expectancy 2019-23 (T007): Bihar 69.3, Uttar Pradesh 68.0, India 70.3. Fertility 2023 (T006): 2.8 vs 2.6, India 1.9. Per-person NSDP constant prices 2024-25 (T020): Uttar Pradesh 55,990, Bihar 36,342. Power per head (T138): 2005-06 Bihar 87.0, Uttar Pradesh 264.9; 2024-25 343.9 vs 692.2. Credit-deposit ratio 2025 (T153): 51.4 vs 50.9. Rural construction wage 2024-25 (T112): 388.6 vs 389.9, India 441.1. GSDP growth (T022, my CAGR): 2022-23 to 2024-25 Bihar 10.28%, Uttar Pradesh 8.96%. MPI headcount NFHS-5 (T010): Bihar 33.76%, Uttar Pradesh 22.93%. Female LFPR (PLFS): Bihar 4.1 to 30.5, Uttar Pradesh 13.5 to 34.5.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ T004, T006, T007, T010, T020, T022, T112, T138, T153; public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (female LFPR 2017-18 and 2023-24). Original SRS tables via Census of India SRS (SOURCE_MAP). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Search shows people type 'Bihar vs UP'. But research says Bihar-named videos mostly do not over-index (17% at 3x+, 6 videos) and 'Bihar vs Rest of India' got 0.3x, while a data paradox does: FactTechz's Bihar internet-versus-penetration Short got 4.6M views in 9 days, and 'Why West UP is Rich but East UP is Poor?' got 780K (11x). So this is the small, honest test of a low-profile pair, built on a paradox and on rates of change rather than 'who is backward'.
- **Risks:** Infant mortality and life expectancy come from sample registration with sampling error; poverty headcount is from an older survey (NFHS-5, 2019-21). Frame it as a fair test, never as mocking either state. Cherry-picking risk: show the rounds Uttar Pradesh wins (income, power, scale). Women-at-work numbers need the series caveat from idea 9. Low-profile pairs may underperform (Bihar 17% hit rate). A related Short exists (S066, Uttar Pradesh vs Bihar teenage mothers); keep distinct.
- **Effort:** medium

### L054. Which Country's Economy Matches Your State's?

- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 5 (virality 6, appeal 7, distinct 4, fit 5, risk medium)

- **Hook:** Spoken: 'Tamil Nadu's economy is about 31 lakh crore rupees. Which country does that match?' On screen: one rupee bar and an empty ranked column of country names.
- **Outline:** Target 15 min. 1. Cold open: guess the match (1 min). 2. The method (2): state GSDP at current prices (T021) converted at the RBI reference rate for the same fiscal year, set beside World Bank GDP in current US dollars for that year; why dollars and rupees differ from purchasing power. 3. The big four (3): Maharashtra 45.32, Tamil Nadu 31.19, Uttar Pradesh 29.78, Karnataka 28.84 lakh crore rupees, each matched to peer economies within about 10% (countries to be pulled). 4. The mid-size and small states (2.5): Telangana 16.41, Bihar 9.92, Himachal Pradesh 2.32 lakh crore. 5. Per person (2.5): Delhi 493,024 and Bihar 69,321 rupees against countries at similar income levels. 6. The five southern states together are 104.8 and the eight northern ones 88.2 lakh crore rupees (size only; grouping stated). 7. Why a state is not a country (1.5): no currency, borders or trade policy; this is a scale comparison only. 8. Your state's match (1).
- **Who shares it and why:** A viewer sends 'my state is as big as this country' to friends in other states to start a pride contest, then comments asking for their own state.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A what-if built on pride in scale. Each state's fans share 'my state is bigger than X' with friends in other states; comments: 'compare with my state', 'PPP would change this', 'Gujarat is missing'. Share trigger frame: the Tamil Nadu bar sliding into a named country's slot, then a pick-your-state call.
- **Why it could flop:** State-versus-country is a recycled social-media meme, and repeats decay fast (Tamil Nadu vs Pakistan 135K, Gujarat vs Pakistan 10K, Uttar Pradesh vs Pakistan 6.7K). Matches may land on mid-size countries that give no pride, and Data Duck's flat country comparisons sit at 15K to 41K. We hold no World Bank GDP or exchange-rate data, and the nominal-versus-PPP choice changes every match. The Map Motion 8M to 12M hits were Shorts with a secession frame we cannot use.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** GSDP 2024-25 current prices (T021, lakh crore rupees, derived from rupees lakh): Maharashtra 45.32, Tamil Nadu 31.19, Uttar Pradesh 29.78, Karnataka 28.84, Telangana 16.41, Bihar 9.92, Himachal Pradesh 2.32; five southern states together 104.8, eight northern states together 88.2 (my sums). Per-person NSDP 2024-25 (T019): Delhi 493,024; Bihar 69,321. No country figures yet: they are not in our files.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** Local: public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T021, T019 (state GSDP and per-person NSDP; Gujarat ends 2022-23). Not collected: World Bank GDP (current US$), SOURCE_MAP rows 'World Bank Open Data' and 'World Bank API' (NY.GDP.MKTP.CD), and the RBI reference exchange rate (RBI Database on Indian Economy, SOURCE_MAP). (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** Map Motion's state what-ifs reached 12M ('What If Tamil Nadu Never Existed') and 8.4M ('...Became an Independent Country'). Long-form state-vs-country worked when well built: 'Tamil Nadu vs Pakistan | State vs Country' 135K (9x), 'Delhi vs Dhaka' 111K (7x), and Wawamu's 'India States vs China Provinces GDP' 600K (2.7x). Flat country comparisons did not (Data Duck 15K to 41K), so the hook has to be the guess-the-match.
- **Risks:** Exchange-rate and nominal-versus-PPP choices change matches; fix the method before scripting. The framing must stay a scale comparison, never secession (neutral wording; avoid 'become independent'). Do not pair states with hostile neighbours for mockery (that template decayed: Gujarat vs Pakistan 10K). Gujarat's latest year is 2022-23. Pull and cite the World Bank and RBI numbers before any country is named in the title or thumbnail.
- **Effort:** high
