Hook chapter order puts the farmland-and-groundwater twist (chapter 1 to 2) first.
- **Who shares it and why:** Geography teachers and exam aspirants might forward the cropland-versus-groundwater reveal, but it is a weak trigger and nothing in the other chapters gives a reason to send it.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A quiz the viewer can fail plus surprise that overturns a belief. Shared by teachers, students and exam aspirants and by geography-minded friends ('guess before you watch'). Likely comments: 'I thought X was top', 'where is my state', 'do districts', score-sharing from the quiz chapter. Share trigger: the reveal that the state with the most cropland is also second in groundwater use (Rajasthan 149.86%), with the map lighting both facts at once.
- **Why it could flop:** It copies the title shape of FactSpark's 2.5M 'The Geography of India Explained' but swaps its narrative for a ten-table ranking tour, and there is no tension, verdict or rivalry. A quiz has no measured Indian lift, and ranking-chart repeats decayed (62K to 2K). Several chapters cannot name a clean leader (rain tie between Kerala and Andaman and Nicobar, heat-wave tie at 22 days, budget estimates rather than outcomes). Its best chapter, Rajasthan's biggest cropland with 150% groundwater use, is the same fact as SG03, so the Short would spoil the long.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Net sown area (T054, 2023-24 p): Rajasthan 18,198, Maharashtra 16,571, Uttar Pradesh 16,365 of India 138,992 thousand ha. Cropping intensity (T057): Punjab 201.1%, Haryana 196.3%, India 156.8%. Gross irrigated area (T055): Uttar Pradesh 24,653 of 130,548 thousand ha. Groundwater extraction (T107, 2024): Punjab 156.87%, Rajasthan 149.86%, Haryana 135.96%, India 60.5%. Rainfall (T102, 2024): Kerala 2,795.6 mm, Andaman and Nicobar 2,794.2, Arunachal Pradesh 2,169.0, Punjab 391.7. Forest cover (T099, 2023): Madhya Pradesh 77,073, Arunachal Pradesh 65,882, Chhattisgarh 55,812 of 715,343 sq km. Tree cover (T101): Maharashtra 14,525 of 112,014 sq km. Heat-wave days 2024 (T104): Punjab 22, Uttarakhand 22, Haryana 21. Cold-wave days 2024 (T103): Jammu and Kashmir 12, Bihar 7, Delhi 7.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook T053-T057, T099, T101-T105, T107. Not collected (SOURCE_MAP): Census of India area figures, Survey of India, IMD district data for the follow-up district lane. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 3 (physical-geography flagship). Measured: FactSpark's 'The Geography of India Explained' reached 2.5M (50x its median; 12 of its 66 long videos are 10x+), The Graphic Earth's 'Crazy Geographical Borders of India' 3.5M, and the geography/maps tag hits 3x+ in 27.3% of videos vs 14.7% (n=110). The unused state-level physical tables (forest, rain, heat and cold, calamity relief, irrigation) are already in hand. The research warns that heat and air-quality videos had medians under 6K and that state-level is weaker than district hits, so heat is one chapter, not the hook, and the closing chapter sets up the district lane.
- **Risks:** T102 covers only 14 IMD sub-divisions, and Kerala and Andaman and Nicobar are a tie (1.4 mm apart), so no rank claim there; heat-wave days tie at 22 (Punjab, Uttarakhand). T105 figures are budget estimates, not disaster outcomes, and one value (Tamil Nadu 1.9) looks like a reporting quirk, so leave it out. Totals favour big states, so frame as 'share of India'. Official boundary only on every map. Reuses the State Files engine, so build it after the Assam video. Past heat and air-quality videos did poorly, so do not title around them.
- **Effort:** medium

### L060. Delhi 18.5%, Arunachal 66.5%: Women in India's Labour Force

- **Original title:** India's Women Joined the Workforce: 23% to 42% in 6 Years
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.8 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 5, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** The Long Series
- **Template:** new: Long Series documentary on Long Kit v1 with a two-map 'flip' (2017-18 map dissolves into the 2023-24 map, every state numbered, text never on the map) and a men-vs-women gap strip.
- **Hook:** Arunachal Pradesh: 14.7% of women in the labour force in 2017-18. 66.5% in 2023-24. Delhi: 18.5%.
- **Outline:** Target 14 min. Ch1 The jump (2 min): women 15+ in the labour force 23.3% to 41.7%, at work 22.0% to 40.3% (PLFS usual status). Ch2 The map flips (3 min): biggest rises Arunachal Pradesh 14.7 to 66.5, Nagaland 16.7 to 64.3, Assam 12.7 to 50.2, Tripura 12.5 to 47.1, Jharkhand 15.4 to 49.8, Bihar 4.1 to 30.5. Ch3 The states that did not move (2 min): Delhi 14.3 to 18.5, Goa 30.9 to 29.3, Lakshadweep 18.4 to 17.1, Haryana 14.3 to 24.2. Ch4 Village and city (1.5 min): rural 24.6 to 47.6, urban 20.4 to 28.0. Ch5 The gap with men (2 min): Meghalaya women 71.8 vs men 82.3, Delhi 18.5 vs 70.1, Lakshadweep 17.1 vs 74.7. Ch6 What kind of work? (2 min): share of self-employed and unpaid family workers by state, from the MoSPI PLFS annual report (to collect), plus women paid in cash for work (NFHS-6). Ch7 The 2025 reading (1 min): 40.0% on the calendar-year series, explained as a different survey frame. Ch8 Pause-and-guess: where does your state sit. Cut three Shorts: 'Delhi 18.5%', 'Bihar 4.1 to 30.5', 'Arunachal passes Himachal'.
- **Who shares it and why:** Women and mothers forward 'Delhi is 18.5%?' in family and alumni groups, and Northeast viewers share Arunachal Pradesh and Nagaland at the top.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A surprise that overturns a belief, shared by women and by mothers, sisters and colleagues in family and alumni groups ('Delhi is 18.5%?'). Northeast viewers share pride (Arunachal, Nagaland, Meghalaya at the top), Bihar and Jharkhand viewers share the rise. Expected comments: 'is it paid work or unpaid family work?', 'what about domestic work', 'rural jobs scheme effect'. Share trigger: the map flip from 2017-18 to 2023-24 and the Delhi-versus-Arunachal split frame. Chapter 6 answers the top comment before it is typed.
- **Why it could flop:** It uses the same PLFS table as L050 and L051, so the three cannibalise each other. 'Joined the workforce' claims more than participation measures, since much of the rise may be unpaid family work or self-employment, and the 2025 calendar-year reading (40.0) already dips. Chapter 6 needs PLFS annual-report tables we have not collected, and a survey-method change may break the 2017-18 comparison. Women's-employment explainers are common among Indian economics channels.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Women in the labour force, age 15+, usual status: India 23.3% (2017-18) to 41.7% (2023-24); calendar-year 2025 reading 40.0%. Worker-population ratio, women: 22.0% to 40.3%. Rural women 24.6% to 47.6%; urban 20.4% to 28.0%. Arunachal Pradesh 14.7 to 66.5; Nagaland 16.7 to 64.3; Assam 12.7 to 50.2; Tripura 12.5 to 47.1; Jharkhand 15.4 to 49.8; Bihar 4.1 to 30.5; Goa 30.9 to 29.3; Delhi 14.3 to 18.5; Lakshadweep 18.4 to 17.1; Haryana 14.3 to 24.2. 2023-24 women vs men: Meghalaya 71.8 vs 82.3; Delhi 18.5 vs 70.1; Lakshadweep 17.1 vs 74.7; Punjab 31.1 vs 79.8. Women paid in cash for work (NFHS-6): India 30.8%, Telangana 53.2%.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (LFPR, WPR, UR; female, male, rural, urban; 2017-18 to 2025); nfhs6_states.json indicator 91; chapter 6 needs the MoSPI PLFS Annual Report 2023-24 status-in-employment tables (SOURCE_MAP: MoSPI reports and publications). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Launch slot 5. A state-data paradox plus 'your state' personalisation, the two Shorts hooks that pulled 4.6M (FactTechz Bihar internet paradox, 9 days) and 7 to 12 times the median comment rate (research_shorts_india.md). It is the largest single jump on the long RBI/MoSPI series, and unlike a tax or caste title it is neutral. Honest counter-evidence: PolyMatter's 'Why 75% of Indian Women are Unemployed' did 540K, only 0.6x its median, so the topic is not a sure thing and the map flip has to carry it.
- **Risks:** Largest risk is the claim the numbers cannot carry: a rise in participation is not a rise in paid or formal jobs, and much of it may be self-employment or unpaid family work, so chapter 6 must be built before publishing and the title says 'workforce' (counts those working or seeking work), not 'earning'. The 2025 calendar-year reading uses a different frame from the July-June annual series; state it and do not chart them as one line. Small UTs (Lakshadweep) have small samples. Keep out of any government-scheme credit or blame; no party names.
- **Effort:** medium

### L045. Kerala's Rural Construction Wage: Rs 923 a Day, Up 17% Since 2014-15

- **Original title:** Why Is Kerala So Different? The Official Numbers, Good and Bad
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.7 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 4, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Why Desk
- **Template:** new: DK Why Desk Long (16:9, reuses the Duel template's chart cards): one state against the India row and the state median, a green/amber verdict chip per table, official India map highlight
- **Hook:** Spoken: 'In Kerala a rural construction worker earns 923 rupees a day. In Madhya Pradesh, 308. But since 2014-15 Kerala's wage rose just 17%.' On screen: the two wages, then '+17%' in amber.
- **Outline:** Target 15 min. 1. Cold open: the wage gap and the 17% (1.5 min). 2. Lives (3): infant mortality 5 per thousand (only Manipur is lower, at 3), life expectancy 75.1 (Himachal Pradesh and J&K 74.4), multidimensional poverty headcount 0.55% vs India 14.96%, SDG score 79 (tied with Uttarakhand). 3. Pay (2): rural construction wage 922.6 vs India 441.1; slowest growth since 2014-15. 4. Growth (2.5): GSDP growth 4.72% a year over 2011-12 to 2022-23 (25th of 33); per-person income 7th of 25 at both price bases. 5. Factories (2): net fixed capital formation 2023-24 is -15,647 lakh rupees, the lowest in the table. 6. Prices and jobs (2.5): consumer inflation 2024-25 5.9% vs India 4.6%; rural male unemployment 46 per thousand vs India 27; PLFS 2025 unemployment 4.4% vs India 3.1%. 7. An older state (1.5): 60+ share 20.7% (NFHS-6). 8. What the tables cannot explain (1): migration, remittances, history; where to look.
- **Who shares it and why:** Malayalis, including the Gulf diaspora, send the Rs 923 vs Rs 308 wage line as pride and sceptics send back the '+17% in ten years' line; the pair of lines is the share unit.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Identity and pride plus a paradox. Shared by Malayali viewers (including the Gulf diaspora) and by people who say 'the Kerala model' in debates, and sent back by sceptics. Comments: 'remittances!', 'wages are high because...', 'what about the 2025 numbers?'. Share trigger frame: 923 vs 308 rupees, then the amber '+17%' that makes the leader look slow.
- **Why it could flop:** 'Why is Kerala so different?' promises a cause, and the tables cannot show causes (migration, remittances and public-spending history are outside the data). The video either speculates, which breaks the accuracy line, or ends on 'we can't show why', and the top comment will be 'remittances!'. It is the most-typed state and the richest why-cluster, which means every Malayalam and English explainer already targets it, and a balanced 'good and bad' take ignites neither fans nor critics. 'Kerala model' is politically loaded. The wage series covers men construction workers only and is easy to over-read. Mohak Mangal's 'Tamil Nadu: India's Best State?' reached only 0.8x his median.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Rural construction wage 2024-25 (T112): Kerala 922.6, J&K 584.5, Madhya Pradesh 307.8, India 441.1; growth since 2014-15 Kerala +17% (slowest), Andhra Pradesh +133% (fastest). Infant mortality 2023 (T004): Kerala 5, Manipur 3, India 25. Life expectancy 2019-23 (T007): Kerala 75.1, India 70.3. MPI headcount NFHS-5 (T010): Kerala 0.55%, India 14.96%. SDG 2023 (T106): Kerala 79, Uttarakhand 79, India 71. GSDP growth 2011-12 to 2022-23 (T022, my CAGR) 4.72%. Net fixed capital formation 2023-24 (T129): -15,647 lakh rupees. Inflation 2024-25 (T108): Kerala 5.9, India 4.6. Rural male unemployment 2023-24 (T008): Kerala 46 per thousand, Punjab 51, Uttarakhand 48. NFHS-6 aged 60+: Kerala 20.7%, India 12.9%.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ T004, T007, T010, T106, T112, T022, T020, T019, T129, T108, T008; public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json; public/datayt/nfhs6/nfhs6_states.json (indicator 3). Migration and remittance data are not in SOURCE_MAP.md and would need a source (for example the RBI remittance survey, Tier 1, to be mapped). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Kerala is the most-typed state with data intent (179 YouTube suggestions, the most of any state), and 'why is Kerala so developed / different / the best state' is the richest why-cluster in the search research. Single-state 'why is X' videos reached 4.6x to 6.4x their channel median in the place research. Giving both the good and the bad is what makes it trustworthy and arguable.
- **Risks:** The tables cannot show causes (migration, remittances, public spending history); say what is not shown and do not guess. 'Kerala model' is politically loaded, so never credit or blame any party or government. The wage series is men construction workers in rural areas at nominal prices, not all pay. Avoid superlatives where the lead is thin: life expectancy lead is 0.7 years, SDG is a tie, Manipur beats Kerala on infant mortality. Net fixed capital formation covers registered factories only.
- **Effort:** medium

### L061. India's Richest States, Every Year: Who Climbed, Who Fell

- **Original title:** India's Richest and Poorest States, Every Year Since 2011
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.7 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 6, fit 6, risk medium)
- **Series:** India, State by State, Every Year
- **Template:** new: Every Year Map 16:9. every_year.py takes an RBI table id, the column-to-year map and a unit, and EveryYearMap.tsx draws the official-boundary map (reusing StateMap.tsx) with a big year counter, a live top-8 and bottom-8 leaderboard beside it (never over the map), hatched fill for a year a state has not published, auto-detected 'biggest climber' and 'biggest shock' captions, and a final 'your state' line chart. Works for any table with 10 or more periods.
- **Hook:** From the top state to the bottom state, the income gap fell from 11.9 times to 9.7 times. In rupees, it doubled.
- **Outline:** Target 13 min. Ch1 The rules (1 min): income per person (net state domestic product, current prices, base 2011-12), 33 states with a figure for every year 2011-12 to 2022-23 (T019). Ch2 The race (3 min): the map fills year by year with the rank counter running. Ch3 Climbers and fallers (2 min): Telangana 11th to 5th, Karnataka 12th to 6th, Gujarat 15th to 9th, Tripura 27th to 20th; Uttarakhand 7th to 15th, Puducherry 5th to 13th, Meghalaya 19th to 27th. Ch4 The COVID year (1.5 min): 2020-21 change, Chandigarh -12.2%, Mizoram -11.2%, Delhi -9.4% against Andhra Pradesh +4.8% and Tamil Nadu +1.7%. Ch5 Did the poor states catch up? (2 min): the ratio narrowed, Goa to Bihar 11.9x to 9.7x, while the gap in rupees went from 2,37,694 to 4,78,217. Ch6 Same race after inflation (1.5 min): T020 constant prices. Ch7 The latest two years (1 min): the 25 states with a 2024-25 figure, and who has not published. Ch8 Your state: pause-and-guess, then its line against India. FIRST TEN 'Every Year' episodes in order: 1 Income per person (T019, T020); 2 Babies per woman (T006, 2003-2023); 3 Infant deaths (T004, 2004-2023); 4 Electricity per person (T138, 2005-06 to 2024-25); 5 Bank branches (T152, 2004-2025); 6 Milk (T091); 7 Forest cover (T099, 1987-2023); 8 Factories (T116); 9 Women in the labour force (PLFS, 2017-18 to 2025); 10 Rural wages (T112).
- **Who shares it and why:** A viewer scrubs to their own state and sends the timestamp where it overtakes a rival state to the friend from that rival state.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A spectacle people rewatch, plus 'where was my state' pride. A viewer scrubs to find their state, then shares the timestamp where it overtakes a rival in the comments ('Telangana passes Karnataka at 2020!'). The twist (ratio narrowed, rupee gap doubled) gives the argument: 'so are poor states catching up or not?'. Share trigger: the leaderboard frame at the final year and the two-gap card.
- **Why it could flop:** The 'Every Year' spectacle is only 12 steps of a leaderboard where most ranks barely move, which is a bar-chart race with a map beside it; the research rates bar-chart races low-odds now, and the global Every Year hits ran on centuries of changing borders that we cannot use. The 'twist' (ratio narrowed, rupee gap doubled) is nominal-price arithmetic that any growing economy produces, so the first comment will be 'inflation'. The hook number also looks wrong: 532,854 / 54,637 = 9.75, which rounds to 9.8x, not 9.7x. Telangana's climb rests on a back-cast to the 2014 boundary, and 'top state' needs a lead check against Sikkim (the two were 0.3% apart in 2023-24). It also overlaps L070 on the same T019 table, and richest-state repeats decayed from 62K to 2K. This is the highest build cost in the set, and the data stops at 2022-23.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Income per person, current prices (T019): top-to-bottom gap Goa 2,59,444 vs Bihar 21,750 in 2011-12 (11.9x); Goa 5,32,854 vs Bihar 54,637 in 2022-23 (9.7x); rupee gap 2,37,694 to 4,78,217. Rank moves 2011-12 to 2022-23 among 33 states: Telangana 11 to 5, Karnataka 12 to 6, Gujarat 15 to 9, Tripura 27 to 20, Uttarakhand 7 to 15, Puducherry 5 to 13, Meghalaya 19 to 27. 2019-20 to 2020-21 change: Chandigarh -12.2%, Mizoram -11.2%, Delhi -9.4%, Andhra Pradesh +4.8%, Tamil Nadu +1.7%. 2024-25 (25 states reporting): Delhi 4,93,024; Telangana 3,87,623; Karnataka 3,80,906; Bihar 69,321 (7.1x).
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T019 (current prices) and T020 (constant prices), 14 periods 2011-12 to 2024-25; later episodes T006, T004, T138, T152, T091, T099, T116, T112 and plfs_annual.json. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Launch slot 6. The 'Every Year' shape is the best code-only format in the global sample when the subject is broad: Ollie Bye's broad 'Every Year' maps have a median of 587K with 49% at 3x+, against 202K for narrow ones, and 'History of India: Every Year' reached 9.4M (45x) (research_long_global.md). We cannot use historical borders, but the same shape works on official numbers; Wawamu's 'India States vs China Provinces GDP' got 600K (2.7x). 'Richest states of India' is proven demand (3.0M to 3.3M on 4 to 5 minute charts) and crowded, so we publish one definitive official-source version with a twist.
- **Risks:** Highest build cost of the set (new engine) and the narrow-subject penalty: later episodes must stay broad (income, babies, power, phones), since narrow subjects fell to about 200K even for the best channel. Missing data: 2024-25 is missing for Gujarat, Goa, Sikkim, Chandigarh, Manipur, Mizoram, Nagaland and Andaman and Nicobar (Gujarat also 2023-24), so Delhi is the top only among the 25 reporting states and Goa and Sikkim were above it in 2022-23; never call Delhi the richest. Goa leads Sikkim by only about 2% in 2022-23, so say 'Goa and Sikkim at the top'. Rank claims differ between current and constant prices (Puducherry falls further in real terms), so show both. Latest years may be provisional.
- **Effort:** high

### LG04. Assam Economy: Industry Up 245% in 13 Years. Real or Small Base?

- **Original title:** Assam Economy: Industry Output Up 245% Since 2011-12 (RBI Data)
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.7 (virality 5, appeal 5, distinct 6, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** State Files
- **Template:** new: 'State Files' 16:9 narrated map-and-chart profile on the Vox explainer engine (the channel's first long-form format; reused by the geography, best-state, NFHS and North-South longs)
- **Hook:** In 13 years Assam's industry output, with price rises removed, grew 245%. No other state in the table grew more. Is it real, or just a small base?
- **Outline:** Target 12-14 min. 1 'Assam economy' in one chart (1.5 min): GSDP at constant prices 14,317,491 to 34,981,426 rupees lakh (2011-12 to 2024-25), +144.3%, third among 25 states behind Tripura +161.7% and Karnataka +159.1%, so the headline is NOT 'fastest GDP'. 2 Where the growth came from (2.5): industry +245.0% vs Tripura +209.6% and Bihar +205.7% (T042); manufacturing +163.7% over 2014-15 to 2024-25 vs West Bengal +137.4% (T034); construction +293.2% vs Uttarakhand +201.1% (T038). 3 The small-base test (2): Assam's GSDP is 20.2% of Tamil Nadu's and its industry output 25.7%; income per person 41,142 to 159,185 rupees (T019) against Delhi 493,024 and Bihar 69,321. 4 'It depends on the start year' (1.5): the ranking flips between a 2011-12 start and a 2014-15 start (manufacturing is 3rd from 2011-12, 1st from 2014-15). 5 People (2): women in the labour force 12.7% to 50.2% (PLFS 2017-18 to 2023-24); phones per 100 people 46.51 to 72.82 (2013 to 2025, T149); bank offices 1,749 to 3,236 (T152); life expectancy 62.2 to 68.6 years (T007, second to Odisha's gain). 6 The cost side (1.5): gross fiscal deficit 5,430 to 22,534 crore rupees and capital outlay 3,912 to 26,596 crore (2014-15 to 2024-25 BE, T164 and T174). 7 Households (1): NFHS-6 women who have used the internet 28.2% to 53.5%; unemployment 3.7% vs India 3.1% (PLFS 2025). 8 What state tables cannot tell us (1): why, and which districts; point to the district lane. 9 Verdict and 'which state next' request (0.5).
- **Who shares it and why:** Assamese and Northeast viewers and APSC aspirants forward it in study groups as proof of an underdog surge, and viewers from other states argue the 'small base' point in the comments.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Pride and an underdog surprise with a strong but fair question built in ('is it only a small base?'). Shared by Assamese and Northeast viewers and APSC aspirants in study groups and with relatives elsewhere, and argued over by viewers from other fast-growing states. Likely comments: 'what about tea and oil', 'base effect!', 'do Meghalaya / Tripura next', 'districts?'. Share trigger: the chart where the Assam bar leads on industry growth and the narrator immediately asks whether it is only the small base, then answers with the 20.2% figure.
- **Why it could flop:** Single-state profiles are the weaker shape in the evidence: Angel One's state profiles got 34K to 55K while its two head-to-head episodes got 494K and 1.1M. '245%' is a number viewers cannot picture, and 'Industry' in the RBI table lumps mining, manufacturing, power and construction, so the video cannot say why Assam grew; the 2011-12 start year is the one that flatters Assam (GSDP growth is only third). It is the channel's first long-form engine at high effort inside a 5-7 hour week, and a title ending in '(RBI Data)' reads like exam notes. Assam is also not in the measured southern-state tilt, though 'Is Assam the Next Gujarat?' did 123K (51x) on a small channel.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Industry GSVA at constant prices (T042): Assam 4,385,874 to 15,130,438 rupees lakh, 2011-12 to 2024-25 = +245.0% vs Tripura +209.6%, Bihar +205.7% (25 states); 2014-15 to 2024-25 +215.4% vs Bihar +145.0%. Manufacturing, constant prices (T034): +163.7% (2014-15 to 2024-25) vs West Bengal +137.4%, but +208.7% from 2011-12 (3rd, behind Arunachal Pradesh +277.3% and Bihar +216.2%). Construction (T038): +293.2% vs Uttarakhand +201.1% (2011-12 to 2024-25). GSDP, constant prices (T022): +144.3%; Assam is 20.2% of Tamil Nadu's GSDP and 25.7% of its industry output. Income per person (T019, current prices): 41,142 to 159,185 rupees; Delhi 493,024; Bihar 69,321. PLFS women's labour force 12.7% to 50.2% (2017-18 to 2023-24); unemployment 3.7% vs India 3.1% (2025). Phones per 100: 46.51 to 72.82 (2013 to 2025). Bank offices 1,749 to 3,236 (2013 to 2025). Life expectancy 62.2 to 68.6 years (2007-11 to 2019-23). Gross fiscal deficit 5,430 to 22,534 crore rupees; capital outlay 3,912 to 26,596 crore (2014-15 to 2024-25 BE). NFHS-6 women who have used the internet 28.2% to 53.5%.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook T019, T022, T034, T038, T042, T007, T149, T152, T164, T174; public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json; public/datayt/nfhs6/nfhs6_states.json. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 2 (long State Files for high-demand states, titled the way people type). '[State] economy' searches give 263 suggestions, 82 with exam words (assam economy apsc), which also suits exam-prep sponsors. Measured: place-based India long-forms reach 3x their channel median 37% of the time vs 13% (235 videos); Uplearn's 'Is Assam the Next Gujarat?' reached 123K, 51x its median, on an 18K-subscriber channel; verdict and 'next' frames hit 3x+ 43.5% of the time. Because West Bengal, Bihar and Punjab long-forms hit 3x only 10-18% of the time, a low-profile state needs a data surprise as its hook; here it is a clear lead in industry growth.
- **Risks:** Industry in RBI tables is mining, manufacturing, electricity and construction together; the table cannot say why Assam grew. Ranks depend on the start year: GSDP growth is third from 2011-12 and first by under 2 points from 2014-15, and capital-outlay growth leads Manipur by only 33 points, so do not claim 'fastest GDP'. Auto-scan artifacts caught while checking: the leads file shows Assam life expectancy as +12% (61.2 to 68.6) by mixing the male 2007-11 column with the total 2019-23 column (correct is 62.2 to 68.6, second to Odisha), and Assam irrigated area +278% crosses a series break (163 in 2011-12, 378 in 2012-13); use neither. No politics, insurgency or flood framing. First long-form of the channel, so engineering is the real cost.
- **Effort:** high

### L066. Which Country Is as Big as Tamil Nadu's Economy? Guess First

- **Original title:** If Your State Were a Country: Its Economy Against the World
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.6 (virality 5, appeal 7, distinct 5, fit 5, risk medium)
- **Series:** If Your State Were a Country
- **Template:** new: Country Ladder 16:9. A vertical ladder of countries sorted by GDP in the same year; a state block 'drops in' to its rank. A PauseGuess card gives four country options before each reveal, and a running 'your score' counter makes the quiz failable. Config: state, year, exchange-rate source. Shares the kit with the other ideas.
- **Hook:** Tamil Nadu's economy is ₹31.2 lakh crore. Which country has an economy that size? Guess before we show you.
- **Outline:** Target 13 min. Ch1 The rule (1.5 min): state GDP in rupees converted at the year's average exchange rate, compared with World Bank GDP in current US dollars for the same year, with no pairing of states against political rivals. Ch2 The three giants (3 min): Maharashtra 45.3 lakh crore, Tamil Nadu 31.2, Uttar Pradesh 29.8 against the country ladder (T021 plus World Bank). Ch3 Quiz round (3 min): four countries per state, viewer guesses, reveal. Ch4 Per person (2 min): the same ranking by income per person, which reorders everything (T019). Ch5 The smallest states (1.5 min): where Goa and Sikkim land. Ch6 What a state is not (1 min): states do not conduct foreign trade, set currencies or hold reserves, so this compares only the size. Ch7 Your state's drop-in (1 min). SERIES first episodes: 1 the big five, 2 the South five, 3 the Northeast, 4 the smallest states and UTs, 5 'per person' edition.
- **Who shares it and why:** Viewers from Tamil Nadu, Gujarat or Maharashtra post 'my state is the size of ___' and their quiz score in family chats, and send it to the friend who insists their state is bigger.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Pride plus a quiz the viewer can fail. People post their score ('I got 1 of 4') and their state's rank in a family chat; a Gujarati shares 'Gujarat beats ___', a Tamilian shares the Tamil Nadu drop-in. Expected comments: 'compare with China provinces', 'what about PPP', 'do my state'. Share trigger: the drop-in frame where the state block lands between two countries.
- **Why it could flop:** State-versus-country is a worn lane: World In Details' Tamil Nadu vs Pakistan did 135K, but the same template on Gujarat and Uttar Pradesh fell to 10K and 6.7K, and flat country comparisons (Data Duck) sit at 15K to 41K. The 8.4M and 12M hits were secession what-ifs we cannot use. There is no Indian precedent for quizzes (35 quiz Shorts, median 63 views; 5.9% at 1M+ against a 12.4% baseline), and YouTube has no score card to post, so the 'I got 1 of 4' trigger is imagined. World Bank data is not yet collected, and ranks can flip on the exchange rate and the April-March versus calendar-year mismatch. It needs a new engine, so it is high effort, and country pairings risk reading as mockery.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** GSDP at current prices, 2024-25 (T021): Maharashtra 4.53152e+08 lakh rupees; Tamil Nadu 3.11859e+08 lakh; Uttar Pradesh 2.97822e+08 lakh; Himachal Pradesh 2.3169e+07 lakh (lowest of the larger states scanned).
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T021 (GSDP current prices, 2024-25) and T019; World Bank Open Data and API (GDP in current US$ NY.GDP.MKTP.CD, official exchange rate PA.NUS.FCRF; SOURCE_MAP 'World Bank API') not yet pulled. (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** A what-if plus a quiz the viewer can fail. Map Motion's 'What If Tamil Nadu Became an Independent Country' reached 8.4M and 'Never Existed' 12M on Shorts, but flat country comparisons (Data Duck) sit at 15K to 41K, so execution matters; World In Details' 'Tamil Nadu vs Pakistan' got 135K (9x) while repeating the formula on Gujarat and UP fell to 10K and 6.7K (research_long_india.md). India-plus-another-country titles run at 2.6x median globally (research_long_global.md). Use one definitive version per set of states and vary the quiz, not the template.
- **Risks:** Exchange-rate and fiscal-year mismatches (April-March state data against calendar-year World Bank data) can move a rank, so pair years explicitly and show the method. Not secession: wording must be economic scale only, no 'independent country' framing. Avoid pairing a state with Pakistan, China or any adversary (the sample shows that saturates and reads as mockery). Check World Bank figures at pull time; none are collected yet. GSDP at current prices is not PPP.
- **Effort:** high

### L042. Kerala vs Tamil Nadu in 10 Rounds: Who Leads on What? Official Data

- **Original title:** Kerala or Tamil Nadu? Guess Whose Number It Is (10 Rounds)
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.5 (virality 5, appeal 6, distinct 6, fit 5, risk medium)
- **Series:** Whose Number Is It?
- **Template:** new: 16:9 Guess-the-winner engine (the same engine as idea 6), switched to 'whose number is it?': two unlabelled numbers, viewer says which belongs to Kerala
- **Hook:** Two numbers. One is Kerala's, one is Tamil Nadu's. Which is which?
- **Outline:** Target 13 minutes. 1) Setup (1 min): why these two (the most-typed comparison pair; no mockery rule; each side wins rounds). 2) Money (1.5 min): income per person T019, Tamil Nadu Rs 3,61,619 vs Kerala Rs 3,08,338. 3) Health (2 min): infant mortality 5 vs 12 and life expectancy 75.1 vs 73.4. 4) Wages and work (2.5 min): rural construction wage Rs 922.6 vs Rs 583.2 a day; women in the labour force 41.2% vs 47.3%; unemployment 4.4% vs 3.4% (PLFS 2025). 5) Prices (1.5 min): CPI Dec 2025, Kerala 9.49% vs Tamil Nadu 2.62%, plus the 12-month line. 6) Signal and power (1.5 min): 5G villages 94.4% vs 56.9%; power per person 880.3 vs 1,691.7 kWh. 7) The tie (0.5 min): SDG score 79 vs 78, stamped 'too close to call'. 8) Scoreboard: each state wins several and loses some, plus 'which of your guesses were wrong'. 9) Vote for the next pair.
- **Who shares it and why:** A Malayali sends the wage round (Rs 922.6 vs Rs 583.2) to a Tamil friend, who replies with the inflation round (9.49% vs 2.62%), because each side owns a round it can brag about.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Two fan bases each get a round they can brag about. A Malayali viewer sends the wage reveal (Rs 922.6 vs Rs 583.2) to a Tamil friend; the reply is the inflation reveal (9.49% vs 2.62%). Comments: 'knew the wage one', 'you missed [indicator]', 'Karnataka next'. The share trigger is the scoreboard frame with both states holding real wins and a 'too close to call' stamp. The vote for the next pair feeds the Kadai Cup loop.
- **Why it could flop:** It is a 13-minute, 10-round quiz in a market where the measured lift comes from narrated place stories and verdict frames ('who is winning'), not games; 'X vs Y' as a title shape is neutral (16.0% hit 3x vs 14.7% base). Round after round of 'which number is Kerala's' risks a drop-off by round four, and the planned 'too close to call' stamp gives viewers no verdict to argue about. Kerala and Tamil Nadu is a friendly pair with less heat than North vs South. It needs the costliest new engine of the batch. Kerala's 9.49% CPI is a single month and invites a cherry-pick accusation, and part of the search demand wants Tamil or Malayalam videos. Comments can turn hostile in two languages.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** T019 2024-25 (Rs): Tamil Nadu 361,619; Kerala 308,338. T004 2023: Kerala 5; Tamil Nadu 12. T007 2019-23: Kerala 75.1; Tamil Nadu 73.4. T112 2024-25: Kerala Rs 922.6; Tamil Nadu Rs 583.2. PLFS 2025 women's LFPR: Tamil Nadu 47.3; Kerala 41.2; unemployment: Kerala 4.4; Tamil Nadu 3.4. CPI Dec 2025: Kerala 9.49; Tamil Nadu 2.62. 5G villages: Kerala 94.4%; Tamil Nadu 56.9%. T138 2024-25 kWh: Tamil Nadu 1,691.7; Kerala 880.3. T106 2023: Kerala 79; Tamil Nadu 78.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T019, T004, T007, T112, T138, T106; MoSPI PLFS (plfs_annual.json, 2025); MoSPI CPI (cpi_general.json, Dec 2025); DoT 5G villages via Lok Sabha Starred Q 470 (coverage JSON). SRS life tables (Registrar General) and Labour Bureau are the original publishers behind T007 and T112. (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Highest search demand of any state pair: Kerala is the most-typed state in data searches (179 suggestions), Tamil Nadu is second (116), 'Kerala vs Tamil Nadu' is a typed comparison and 'Why is Kerala...' is the richest why-cluster. Tamil Nadu-named long-form place videos hit 3x or more in 75% of 16 videos. Big-name duels carry: Angel One's Karnataka vs Tamil Nadu reached 1.1M (136x its median) and Adarsh Gupta's duels reached 473K-516K, whereas mid-profile pairs flopped (Infra Talks under 0.5x). The 'whose number' device adds a quiz the viewer can fail on a pair where each side has surprising wins.
- **Risks:** Comment sections between the two states can turn hostile in two languages; moderate and keep wording strictly neutral ('leads', never 'better'). Do not use the phones table (T149): data run only to 30 Nov 2024 and use telecom circles. Kerala's inflation spike is a fact for 2025 but the data cannot say why; check the base-year note on CPI. Totals (factories, GSDP) favour Tamil Nadu and should be left out in favour of per-person measures. The SDG tie (79 vs 78) must stay a draw. Reuses idea 6's engine, so build that first.
- **Effort:** high

### L067. Where Do Indians Actually Live? Every District on One Map

- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.5 (virality 5, appeal 7, distinct 6, fit 4, risk medium)
- **Series:** Why So Many, Why So Few
- **Template:** new: Density Map 16:9. A dot-density map (one dot per 1 lakh people) built from district population and area, drawn on the official boundary with a cumulative 'half of India lives in these districts' counter, a density colour mode and a zoom-in card per region. Dot placement is random within district polygons, which are not in hand.
- **Hook:** Draw a line across India. How many people live on each side? We put one dot for every one lakh people.
- **Outline:** Target 14 min. Ch1 One dot, one lakh people (2 min): the whole map filling. Ch2 Half of India (2.5 min): the smallest set of districts holding half the 2011 population, and the share of land they cover. Ch3 The emptiest places (2 min): lowest-density districts, tied to the 8,748 villages with no mobile signal (DoT). Ch4 The densest places (2 min): top 10 districts by people per square kilometre. Ch5 States compared (2 min): density by state, large against small. Ch6 Why: rivers, rain and rock (2 min), only with sources for each claim. Ch7 Census 2027 (1 min): what will be counted next and when; dates to verify before use. Ch8 Your district: pause-and-guess on how crowded it is. FIRST TEN episodes: 1 Where Indians live, 2 Why so few in Arunachal Pradesh, 3 Why so many in Bihar, 4 Why so few in the Thar, 5 Why Kerala is crowded along the coast, 6 Delhi, the densest, 7 The Himalayan states, 8 The Northeast hills, 9 The deltas, 10 The islands.
- **Who shares it and why:** Viewers forward the single frame showing how few districts hold half of India's people, with 'find your district', into hometown and district WhatsApp groups.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A surprise that overturns a belief plus a 'your district' search. The reveal of how few districts hold half the people gets forwarded as a single frame with 'look where you live'; the series repeats per region without looking like a factory (one place, one question each). Expected comments: 'my district is not on it', 'what about before 2011', 'Mumbai density?'. Share trigger: the half-of-India counter frame.
- **Why it could flop:** It uses Census 2011 in late 2026 with the new count under way, so the video is dated on release and the first comment will say so. 'Indians crowd the Gangetic plain and coasts' is already common knowledge, and the one new fact (half of India in N districts) is a one-frame stat, not 14 minutes. The strong global evidence (Geography By Geoff, Australia 95% empty) is about empty places; India's version is the crowded opposite, with no Indian precedent in the sample. Neither the Census district files nor district polygons are in hand, and a non-official district shapefile could break the official-boundary rule. 2011 districts differ from today's, so dots on a current outline can mislabel places. It is nearly the same video as L071, so make only one.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** 
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** Census of India 2011 Primary Census Abstract by district and district area (censusindia.gov.in, SOURCE_MAP 'Census of India', script can fetch); DoT village coverage in public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json for the emptiness chapter; Census 2027 gazette S.O. 4729(E), 5 Sep 2026 (dates unconfirmed). (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** This is the code-only format with the best evidence and no 8 to 15 minute India version in the sample: Geography By Geoff's 'Why So Few / Nobody Lives In' series has 68 videos with a median of 1.15M (2.4x its channel) and a worst video of 139K; density and 'empty' titles run at 1.46x with 27% at 3x+; Real Life Lore's 'Why Most Indians Live Above This Line' got 3.6M (research_long_global.md). Gaurav Thakur's 'Why AUSTRALIA is 95% EMPTY?' did 14M (8.8x) in India (research_long_india.md).
- **Risks:** Census 2011 is old; the 2027 count will update it, so the video must be dated and re-cut after 2027. District boundaries have changed since 2011 (new districts, Telangana, Ladakh), so a 2011 district map on today's official boundary needs a clear note. District polygons and area are not in hand. No religion, caste or language splits in this data: use only total population and area. Avoid 'overpopulated' judgements.
- **Effort:** high

### L076. Which State Is Top? 12 Questions, 5 Seconds Each (Official Data)

- **Original title:** Which State Has the Highest...? A 12-Question Quiz (Official Data)
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.5 (virality 4, appeal 6, distinct 5, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** Which State Has the Highest...? (annual edition; later one-topic editions)
- **Template:** new: DK Long Quiz (16:9, 12-14 min; question card, 5-second think timer, map reveal with runner-up, running score, end card); long-form version of the existing Guess the State logic.
- **Hook:** Which state has the most factories? Gujarat? You have five seconds. The answer is on the next screen.
- **Outline:** Target 13 minutes. Twelve questions, each a belief the answer overturns, with the runner-up on the reveal. 1) Most factories (T116). 2) Most wheat: Punjab is third (T059). 3) Most rice, and who is second (T058). 4) Most eggs (T092). 5) Highest exports (T181). 6) Most domestic tourist visits (T013). 7) Highest rural construction wage (T112). 8) Most women in the labour force (PLFS 2023-24). 9) Lowest unemployment (PLFS 2025). 10) Highest fertility (T006). 11) Most renewable capacity (T143). 12) Most villages with no mobile signal: Odisha by ONE village over Arunachal Pradesh, so we call it a tie, and Arunachal Pradesh is first as a share (DoT). Closing score card with 'your score' and a challenge to a friend.
- **Who shares it and why:** A student or friend sends it to the one who insists the answer is Punjab or Gujarat and people compare scores in the comments, but a video cannot record a score, so the 'I got 4/12' share is mostly imagined.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A quiz the viewer can fail. Shared as a score ('I got 4/12') in friend and family groups and class groups, and sent to the friend who insists the answer is Punjab or Gujarat. Comments: scores, 'UP wins half of these?', 'Punjab has the best yield' (answered in the video). The trigger line is 'you said Punjab, didn't you?' followed by the running score.
- **Why it could flop:** There is no Indian precedent for quiz-style state content. The 35 quiz-style state Shorts had a median of 63 views and a best of 31K, and quiz/guess titles reach 1M+ in 5.9% of cases against a 12.4% baseline. Twelve unrelated topics gather no single audience, because each viewer cares about one or two questions. Uttar Pradesh wins roughly half the answers, so the belief-overturned engine runs out by question 6. The superlative demand is real in aggregate (403 suggestions), but 'state with highest gdp' returned nothing, so people do not type it this way. Nothing here has a 'my state' pull beyond the reveal map. 'Highest' has to survive a check against all states and UTs, and the Odisha/Arunachal tie shows how thin some answers are.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Factories 2023-24: Tamil Nadu 40,121; Gujarat 33,311; Maharashtra 26,539. Wheat 2024-25 (thousand tonnes): Uttar Pradesh 35,652.9; Madhya Pradesh 24,509.1; Punjab 17,993.2. Rice: Uttar Pradesh 20,758.7; Telangana 17,445.7; West Bengal 16,019.4. Eggs 2023-24 (lakh): Andhra Pradesh 2,54,874; Tamil Nadu 2,23,325. Exports 2024-25 (USD million): Gujarat 1,16,332; Maharashtra 65,855; Tamil Nadu 52,074. Domestic tourist visits 2024 (million): Uttar Pradesh 646.81; Tamil Nadu 306.84; India total 2,948.19. Rural construction wage (Rs/day): Kerala 922.6; J&K 584.5; Madhya Pradesh 307.8. Women in labour force 2023-24: Meghalaya 71.8; Sikkim 68.2. Unemployment 2025: Gujarat 0.9; Madhya Pradesh 1.5. Fertility 2023: Bihar 2.8; Uttar Pradesh 2.6. Renewable capacity (MW): Rajasthan 26,693; Gujarat 25,472; Tamil Nadu 19,983. Villages with no signal: Odisha 1,177; Arunachal Pradesh 1,176.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T006, T013, T058, T059, T092, T112, T116, T143, T181; PLFS annual JSON; mobile coverage JSON public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json (all local). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Search demand is the point: 'which state has the highest...' superlatives are the second-largest theme (403 suggestions). Honest caveat: clue-quiz Shorts have no Indian precedent (35 quiz-style state Shorts, median 63 views), so the quiz is the wrapper and the engine is belief overturned; 'argue about it' hooks drew 7-12x the median comment rate (Georvix 'most loyal states' 5.7M views), and the paradox hook (FactTechz Bihar internet 4.6M in 9 days) shows data surprises travel.
- **Risks:** Absolute totals favour big states: label each question 'total' or 'per person' and keep the runner-up visible. Thin leads must be shown as ties (Odisha 1,177 vs Arunachal Pradesh 1,176). 'Highest' must be checked against all states and UTs (for example Goa leads power use per person, so ask 'among the larger states' or drop it). Some series are provisional. Pilgrimage-driven tourist counts should be shown neutrally. Quizzes can feel like exam GK, so keep the surprise and the story, not the facts-list tone.
- **Effort:** medium

### L051. Gujarat Unemployment 0.9%, Goa 8.3%, Yet Goa's Income Is 2x

- **Original title:** Gujarat 0.9%, Goa 8.3%: What India's Unemployment Rate Hides
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.4 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 6, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** Why Desk
- **Template:** new: DK Why Desk Long (shared) with a 'rate recipe' diagram (labour force, working, looking) and a bar for every state; two-panel compare for one pair
- **Hook:** Spoken: 'Gujarat's unemployment rate is 0.9%. Goa's is 8.3%. Goa's income per person is almost twice Gujarat's.' On screen: 0.9 and 8.3 side by side, then 532,854 vs 272,451.
- **Outline:** Target 12 min. 1. Cold open (1 min). 2. How the rate is made (2): unemployed divided by labour force; why people who give up or work in the family are not counted. 3. The 2025 league table (2.5): every state, India 3.1%, urban 4.8% vs rural 2.4%. 4. The twist (2.5): labour force participation and worker ratio, Gujarat 65.2 and 64.6, Goa 51.2 and 46.9, India 59.3 and 57.4. 5. Income per person (1.5): 2022-23, Goa 532,854 vs Gujarat 272,451 (current prices, 1.96x). 6. Men and women (1.5): Goa male 8.6 vs female 7.3; Gujarat 1.1 and 0.7; Kerala 3.1 and 6.4. 7. What low unemployment does not tell you (1): pay, job type; next data PLFS bulletin 15th of each month, quarterly 10 Nov 2026. 8. Your state (0.5): where does yours fall.
- **Who shares it and why:** Gujarat or Goa viewers and economics students forward the 0.9 next to 8.3 to friends who assume low unemployment means a better state.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Belief-overturning surprise plus state pride on both sides. Shared by Gujarat and Goa viewers and by exam and economics students. Comments: 'what about wages in Gujarat?', 'Goa is tiny', 'so is a higher rate better?'. Share trigger frame: 0.9 next to 8.3, then the income bar flipping the story.
- **Why it could flop:** It is an explainer on how a statistic is defined, and the research found zero search suggestions for 'state wise unemployment rate' and 91% of unemployment-by-state Shorts under 1,000 views. The 'low unemployment is not the same as doing well' twist is standard classroom material, there is no rivalry to argue over, and a 0.9% Gujarat figure invites 'the survey is fake' and Gujarat-politics comments. Small-state estimates (Goa, Andaman and Nicobar Islands) are noisy.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** PLFS unemployment rate calendar year 2025: Gujarat 0.9, Madhya Pradesh 1.5, Goa 8.3, Andaman and Nicobar Islands 10.0, India 3.1, urban 4.8, rural 2.4. Labour force participation (person, 2025): Gujarat 65.2, Goa 51.2, India 59.3; worker ratio: Gujarat 64.6, Goa 46.9, India 57.4. Female unemployment 2025: Gujarat 0.7, Goa 7.3, Kerala 6.4. Per-person NSDP 2022-23, current prices (T019): Goa 532,854, Gujarat 272,451.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (calendar year 2025: LFPR, WPR, UR by state and sex); public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/T019 (per-person NSDP 2022-23). Optional earnings and job-type data would come from the PLFS reports (MoSPI, SOURCE_MAP, not collected). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Jobs and unemployment are the third-biggest YouTube search theme in the research (302 suggestions), and a result that overturns 'low unemployment equals good' gives a reason to share it. Honest caveat from the same research: nobody types 'state wise unemployment rate' (zero suggestions), so the hook must be the paradox, not the topic.
- **Risks:** Gujarat's lead is thin (0.9 vs Madhya Pradesh 1.5, under 1 percentage point), so never say 'lowest'; Goa is highest among states but the Andaman and Nicobar Islands is higher. State estimates for small states are noisy. Calendar-year 2025 figures may use a revised survey design; verify comparability. Earnings and job type are not in hand, so the 'what it hides' chapter must say what we cannot show. Do not mock Goa or Gujarat.
- **Effort:** medium

Size (2): GDP, current US dollars, 1990, 2000, 2010, latest. 4. Per person (2): GDP per capita, current and PPP. 5. People (2): population and working-age share, fertility. 6. Lives (2): life expectancy, infant mortality. 7. Work (2): labour force participation overall and for women. 8. Power and connection (2): electric power use per person, internet users. 9. Where Indian states and Chinese provinces fit (2, optional): only if provincial data can be sourced from the National Bureau of Statistics of China; it is not in SOURCE_MAP.md, so drop the chapter if not. 10. Scoreboard: who leads on what (1.5).

Comments: 'compare PPP', 'what about 1960?', 'add Vietnam'. Share trigger frame: the 1990 starting bars, then the row where the answer surprises.

A zero-subscriber channel has no authority there, and seventeen minutes of ten bar-chart rows is a retention slog. No numbers are in hand, India-versus-X chart channels in our sample sit at a 33K median, and it moves Data Kadai away from the Indian-state data that is its only edge.

Optional China provincial data (National Bureau of Statistics of China) is not in SOURCE_MAP.md and would need adding. (source mapped, not collected)
- **Evidence it could work:** India-plus-China titles are the strongest global hook: median 2.6x channel median, 8 of 20 at 3x+. Wawamu's 'China vs Japan vs Korea vs India: Everything Compared' reached 2.4M (10.7x) and Economics Explained's 'India Will Not Be The Next China' 2.9M (4.3x). Search-sampled India-vs-China GDP videos had a 166K median and a 4.3M maximum. It also hedges the language risk: this audience is global and English-first.
- **Risks:** No numbers are in hand; pull and verify them before any claim. India-China is crowded (many strong channels). Keep to economics and demography, no borders, no politics. Use charts, not maps, so no disputed boundary appears (India maps only on the official boundary). Data definitions differ between countries; name the World Bank, not any government or news site. Provincial data would need a new source.
- **Effort:** high

### L069. Taps With No Water: What Parliament Was Told, State by State

- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.4 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 8, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Asked in Parliament
- **Template:** new: Parliament Table 16:9. The official answer's table is shown first (page image of the annexure with the question number, ministry and date), then rebuilt as a numbered official-boundary map and a top-and-bottom strip on Long Kit v1. read_answer.py extracts the state table from the PDF for a human check against the page image before any number is used.
- **Hook:** A household has a tap. The tap has no water. The Jal Shakti Ministry was asked how many, state by state.
- **Outline:** Target 9 min. Ch1 The question (1 min): Unstarred Question 3261, Jal Shakti, answered 12 March 2026, table as on 3 March 2026, 33 states and UTs. Ch2 The table as published (1.5 min): the annexure on screen. Ch3 The map (2.5 min): every state numbered, as a share of tap connections once the totals are read. Ch4 Top and bottom (1.5 min). Ch5 What the answer says and does not say (1.5 min): the ministry's own notes on definition. Ch6 Your state: pause-and-guess, then reveal (1 min). FIRST TEN in order: 1 Tap connections without water (U3261, 12.03.2026); 2 Closure of schools due to lack of students (U3669, 10.08.2026, 36 states); 3 Dropout rate by school level (U3603, 10.08.2026); 4 Malnutrition and anaemia among children (U2271, 31.07.2026, 36 states); 5 Women representatives in Panchayati Raj institutions (S333, 11.08.2026); 6 Vacancies of doctors and paramedical staff (U3449, 07.08.2026); 7 Assessment of housing shortage (S261, 06.08.2026); 8 Trends in real wages and job quality (S323, 16.03.2026); 9 Telecom black spots on national highways (U2763, 05.08.2026); 10 Functional dialysis units in public hospitals (U924, 24.07.2026).
- **Who shares it and why:** Viewers in the worst-ranked states would send the ministry's own table to a local journalist or friend ('my village has no water'), but the numbers are unread, so the trigger is unproven.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A strong but fair question ('does the tap in my state run?') with trust as the share reason. Local journalists, panchayat members and civic groups quote it, and viewers in the bottom states share it as a question to their representatives. Expected comments: 'my village has no water', 'JJM data is self-reported', 'what is the definition of functional'. Share trigger: the annexure page on screen followed by the numbered map.
- **Why it could flop:** The numbers are unknown, so the hook is a topic, not a finding. If the table shows little spread or is not comparable, the video gets shelved, and a title with no number claim is weak. The data is ministry self-reported and 'no water' is contested (connected versus functional), so constant attribution hedging slows the pace. The Jal Jeevan Mission is a flagship Union scheme, so state-by-state failure reads as Centre-versus-state politics even if we never name a party. A nine-minute single-PDF video has no search demand to carry a zero-subscriber channel. PDF extraction errors are a real accuracy risk even with the human check. The primary-source on-screen annexure is a real differentiator, but views for this format are unproven.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** 
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** Lok Sabha Unstarred Question 3261, Jal Shakti, 12.03.2026, https://sansad.in/getFile/lsapps/loksabhaquestions/annex/187/AU3261_nHrRqd.pdf?source=lsapps (listed in public/datayt/sources/loksabha/index.json, 1 table, 33 states, as on 03.03.2026). Companion U1868 (Tap Water Connections under JJM, 11.12.2025). (answer PDF still to read)
- **Evidence it could work:** Primary-source proof is the channel's differentiator and the Lok Sabha index holds 4,008 answers with state tables, so the supply never runs out. The Mapped Short built from Lok Sabha Starred Q470 (8,748 villages without signal) is the model, and 'your state' framing drew 7 to 12 times the median comment rate (research_shorts_india.md). Expect a small audience at first: no measured long-form benchmark exists for this format.
- **Risks:** Numbers are unknown until someone reads the PDF, so the title makes no number claim and the video can be shelved if the table is not comparable. Self-reported ministry data: attribute to the ministry and date every time, and mark totals that are not defined. Water and scheme topics carry party-political heat, so no scheme-credit or blame wording. Answers differ in date and definition across states in one table. Low virality ceiling: treat as trust and archive, with Shorts cut from the best tables.
- **Effort:** medium

### L077. Punjab Is Third in Wheat: Which State Feeds India?

- **Original title:** Who Feeds India? The Top State for Wheat, Rice, Milk, Eggs and Fish
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.4 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 4, fit 9, risk medium)
- **Series:** What Each State Makes (annual, with the Agricultural Statistics release)
- **Template:** Mapped (existing, re-cut to 16:9, one product per chapter) plus Ranking (existing) inside the new DK Long Map Explainer.
- **Hook:** Uttar Pradesh grows about twice as much wheat as Punjab: 35.7 million tonnes against 18.0 million.
- **Outline:** Target 12 minutes. 1) Wheat: Uttar Pradesh, Madhya Pradesh, Punjab, and the five states that grow 86% of wheat in our tables (T059), then the twist that Punjab leads on yield (T081). 2) Rice: Uttar Pradesh first, Telangana second after its rice area rose 239% in twelve years (T058, T067), yield leaders (T080). 3) Sugarcane: Uttar Pradesh near half of India (T065). 4) Milk (T091). 5) Eggs (T092). 6) Fish: Andhra Pradesh more than a quarter of India (T093). 7) Fruit and vegetables (T074, T075). 8) Tonnes vs yield: who grows the most vs who grows the most per hectare. 9) Your state's plate: where it ranks on each product.
- **Who shares it and why:** Punjab, Uttar Pradesh, Telangana and Andhra Pradesh audiences send it to the friend who says 'Punjab feeds India' (or the reverse), usually as a gotcha.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Farm-state pride plus a belief overturned (the wheat granary is not where people say). Shared by Punjab, Uttar Pradesh, Telangana and Andhra Pradesh audiences and sent to the friend who says 'Punjab feeds India'. Comments: 'Punjab still has the best yield', 'what about Haryana', 'where is my state'. The frame that triggers the share is the tonnes | per hectare split on wheat.
- **Why it could flop:** The belief it overturns ('Punjab feeds India') is mostly about procurement and the central food pool, which production tables do not measure. The top comment will say the video measures the wrong thing, and the thread will slide into MSP and farmer protests, which the outline says to keep out. Uttar Pradesh grows the most wheat largely because it is big, so the real twist is yield, which is only one chapter. Eight product chapters read like a GK list, a lane where 79-91% of search-hit Shorts sit under 1,000 views. The one measured precedent (Graphic Earth spices, 2.5M) comes from a channel with a 349K base and 77% of videos over 100K. Provisional and advance estimates, and thin leads on cotton and meat, add accuracy noise.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Wheat 2024-25 (thousand tonnes): Uttar Pradesh 35,652.9 (India 1,17,944.5); Madhya Pradesh 24,509.1; Punjab 17,993.2. Wheat yield (kg/ha): Punjab 5,123; Uttar Pradesh 3,714. Rice: Uttar Pradesh 20,758.7; Telangana 17,445.7; West Bengal 16,019.4; rice area in Telangana 1,418.4 to 4,809.5 thousand ha (2012-13 to 2024-25). Rice yield: Punjab 4,428; Jharkhand 1,894. Sugarcane: Uttar Pradesh 2,20,801 of India 4,54,611 (about 48.6%); Maharashtra 1,09,970. Milk 2023-24: Uttar Pradesh 38,780; Rajasthan 34,733; Madhya Pradesh 21,326. Eggs (lakh): Andhra Pradesh 2,54,874; Tamil Nadu 2,23,325; Telangana 1,83,850. Fish 2023-24 (lakh tonnes): Andhra Pradesh 51.58 of India 184.02 (about 28%); West Bengal 22.02. Fruit: Andhra Pradesh 19,350.1; vegetables: Uttar Pradesh 38,322.4.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** RBI Handbook T058, T059, T065, T067, T074, T075, T080, T081, T091, T092, T093 (public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/). Underlying producer: Ministry of Agriculture and DAHD (SOURCE_MAP: Directorate of Economics and Statistics, Animal Husbandry and Dairying). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** A faceless, map-led 'top producing states' video is a measured winner: The Graphic Earth's 'Spices Production In India | Top Spice Producing States' reached 2.5M (7.1x its median), and state food maps are huge on Shorts (EduMap 'Indian States and Their Famous Food' 4.9M; food and culture 11.4% at 1M+). The queries 'which state is the highest producer of ...' sit in the 403-suggestion superlative cluster. The tonnes-vs-yield twist keeps the farmland states proud rather than mocked.
- **Risks:** Totals favour big states, so always show yield or per-person alongside. Some series are provisional or advance estimates. Keep farmer-protest, MSP and diet topics out (skip meat). Cotton and meat leads are thin (about 3%), so do not run them as '#1'. Fish and egg units are lakh tonnes and lakh numbers: label clearly. Make it a one-per-year definitive, not a run of single-crop videos.
- **Effort:** medium

### LG07. Best State for Jobs, Study, Health or Business? 12 Official Numbers

- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.3 (virality 4, appeal 6, distinct 5, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** Best State for ___
- **Template:** new: 16:9 decision guide on the Vox engine (use-case chapters, one leader per indicator, closing matrix, no composite winner); the five Short cut-downs use Ranking and Face-off
- **Hook:** Delhi earns the most. Kerala pays the highest rural wage. Goa has the most power per person. There is no single best state.
- **Outline:** Target 14 min. 1 The rules (1 min): one indicator, one leader, period and unit on screen; no composite winner; 'safest' left out because crime data is not collected. 2 To work (2.5): rural construction wage, men: Kerala 922.6 rupees a day vs India 441.1, J&K 584.5, Tamil Nadu 583.2, Madhya Pradesh 307.8 (T112, 2024-25, 20 states); unemployment rate (PLFS 2025): Gujarat 0.9%, Madhya Pradesh 1.5%, Dadra and Nagar Haveli and Daman and Diu 1.6% vs India 3.1% and Goa 8.3%. 3 To live and spend (2.5): income per person Delhi 493,024, Telangana 387,623, Karnataka 380,906 vs Bihar 69,321 (T019, current prices, 2024-25); average CPI inflation 2024-25 Delhi 2.4% and Sikkim 2.5% (a near tie) vs India 4.6% and Manipur 6.5% (T108). 4 To study (2): secondary gross enrolment ratio Chandigarh 115.5, Goa 102.1, Puducherry 97.6 vs India 67.6 and Bihar 46.0 (T001; explain why a ratio can pass 100). 5 To raise a child and stay healthy (2.5): life expectancy Kerala 75.1, Himachal Pradesh 74.4, J&K 74.4 vs India 70.3 (T007, 2019-23); SDG score 2023 Kerala 79 tied with Uttarakhand 79, Tamil Nadu 78, India 71 (T106). 6 To start a business (2): power available per person Goa 3,417.6 kWh, Punjab 2,503.4, Telangana 2,306.1 vs India 1,208.2 and Bihar 343.9 (T138, 2024-25). 7 The scoreboard (1): matrix of which state leads which use-case; no state wins all. 8 What numbers cannot tell you (0.5): safety, occupation salaries, rents. 9 Pick your priority (0.5): comment work, study, child, live or business; the most-picked gets a Short. CUT-DOWNS: five Shorts, one per use-case, each ending on the long.
- **Who shares it and why:** Fans of each state forward their state's one win ('Kerala pays the highest rural wage'), and friends deciding where to move send the link to each other.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Friendly rivalry plus a personal decision tool. Every state wins something, so fans of each state share their win, and friends choosing where to move forward the link to each other. Likely comments: 'my state is missing', 'where is safety / rent / salary', 'Kerala wins on pay but what about jobs', plus the pinned 'pick your priority' thread. Share trigger: the opening line and the closing matrix showing that no state wins all five use-cases.
- **Why it could flop:** The opening line, 'there is no single best state', removes the verdict that drives arguments, and the verdict and 'who is winning' frames were the ones that lifted in the evidence. 'Best state' on big channels sat at or below the channel median (Dhruv Rathee's 7.2M is about 0.55x his median; Mohak Mangal's 0.8x), so demand is search-led, not share-led. Twelve indicators with mixed periods and units, several thin leads (Delhi 2.4 vs Sikkim 2.5 on CPI, Kerala tied with Uttarakhand on SDG) and 'leading group' wording make it feel like a list of facts, and 'safest' is left out although it is the first thing people ask.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Rural construction wage, men (T112, 2024-25): Kerala 922.6, J&K 584.5, Tamil Nadu 583.2, India 441.1, Madhya Pradesh 307.8 rupees a day (20 states). Unemployment (PLFS 2025): Gujarat 0.9%, Madhya Pradesh 1.5%, DNH&DD 1.6%, India 3.1%, Goa 8.3%. Income per person (T019, 2024-25): Delhi 493,024, Telangana 387,623, Karnataka 380,906, Bihar 69,321 rupees. CPI inflation average (T108, 2024-25): Delhi 2.4%, Sikkim 2.5%, India 4.6%, Manipur 6.5%. Secondary GER (T001): Chandigarh 115.5, Goa 102.1, Puducherry 97.6, India 67.6, Bihar 46.0. Life expectancy (T007, 2019-23): Kerala 75.1, Himachal Pradesh 74.4, J&K 74.4, India 70.3. SDG score (T106, 2023): Kerala 79, Uttarakhand 79, Tamil Nadu 78, India 71. Power per person (T138, 2024-25): Goa 3,417.6, Punjab 2,503.4, Telangana 2,306.1, India 1,208.2, Bihar 343.9 kWh.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook T001, T007, T019, T106, T108, T112, T138; public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (UR, 2025). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Gap 4 (decision guides). Roughly 80 'best state' suggestions exist (job, to live, to settle, study, business, healthcare, tourism) and no idea serves job seekers, students, parents or returning NRIs. Measured ceilings: Dhruv Rathee's 'Which is the Best State in India?' 7.2M (10 min) and 'Why is South India more Developed than North India?' 12.9M; verdict and 'next' place frames hit 3x+ 43.5% of the time. A rewatchable decision tool suits long-form watch hours. Framed as 'best for what' to respect the neutrality and thin-lead rules.
- **Risks:** Mixed periods and units across 12 indicators: label each. Thin or tied leads (CPI Delhi 2.4 vs Sikkim 2.5, SDG Kerala = Uttarakhand, unemployment Gujarat 0.9 vs Madhya Pradesh 1.5, life expectancy Kerala 0.7 years ahead) so say 'leading group', not '#1'. The wage table covers only 20 states. Infant mortality is left out because the table shows Manipur 3 below Kerala 5 (small-sample noise). 'Safest' and salary queries are excluded on purpose (crime data not collected, violence rule, occupation salaries are not in official state data). Never crown an overall winner. Reuses the State Files engine.
- **Effort:** medium

### L047. Is Odisha the Next Gujarat? 8% of Factory Capital, 2% of Workers

- **Original title:** Is Odisha the Next Gujarat? Factory Data Gives a Split Answer
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.2 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 6, fit 7, risk medium)
- **Series:** The Next Test
- **Template:** new: DK Why Desk Long (shared) with a two-bar 'machines vs jobs' card (share of India's factory capital vs share of India's factory workers) per state
- **Hook:** Spoken: 'Odisha holds 8% of India's factory capital. But only 2% of its factory workers.' On screen: two bars, 8.0% and 2.0%, with Gujarat's 19.5% and 12.8% ghosted behind.
- **Outline:** Target 13 min. 1. What 'next Gujarat' would mean (1.5 min): Gujarat holds 19.5% of India's factory capital and 12.8% of its factory workers. 2. Odisha's build-up (2.5): fixed capital 1,604,281 lakh rupees (2004-05) to 36,809,621 (2023-24), 22.9x; but since 2011-12 it grew 2.29x vs Gujarat 2.89x, Madhya Pradesh 4.87x, Rajasthan 3.83x, Haryana 3.38x. 3. Machines vs jobs (2.5): 8.0% of capital, 2.0% of workers (314,090); value added per worker about 25.1 lakh vs Gujarat 17.6 and Tamil Nadu 10.2 (derived). 4. The state's own push (2): capital outlay 5,622 to 58,195 crore (+935%, 2012-13 to 2024-25 BE), social-sector spending +556%, own non-tax revenue 58,000 crore. 5. The reach test (1.5): 5G in 5.9% of villages and 1,177 villages with no signal, with the caveat that village counts differ by state. 6. The contenders race (1.5): capital added since 2011-12, Gujarat 5.91, Maharashtra 2.91, Tamil Nadu 2.13, Odisha 2.07, Madhya Pradesh 1.93 lakh crore rupees. 7. Verdict (1.5).
- **Who shares it and why:** Odia viewers share it as 'Odisha vs Gujarat' pride, and business-school and policy audiences argue jobs versus capital, but the audience for that argument is niche.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A verdict question plus regional pride. Shared by Odia viewers and by business-school and policy audiences, plus Gujarat fans who disagree. Comments: 'mining is not manufacturing', 'jobs matter more than capital', 'what about Madhya Pradesh?'. Share trigger frame: 8.0% vs 2.0%.
- **Why it could flop:** The question is spent: Uplearn's 'Is Odisha India's Next Tech Hub?' already reached 471K, so we repeat it with a wonkier answer. 'Share of India's factory capital versus factory workers' is hard for a general viewer to argue with. The data has built-in escape hatches: book-value capital, registered factories only, mining excluded, and the 22.9x build-up is mostly 2004-2012, so no current-surge claim. A split verdict with no winner gives commenters little to fight over. The 5G-village comparison (5.9% vs 85.2%) is not comparable across states. Odisha's measured pull rests on one precedent video rather than a repeatable pattern like Tamil Nadu's.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Factory fixed capital 2023-24 (T117): Odisha 36,809,621 lakh rupees = 8.0% of India's 462,409,035; Gujarat 90,327,908 = 19.5%; Tamil Nadu 37,427,225 = 8.1%. Factory workers (T122): Odisha 314,090 = 2.0% of India; Gujarat 12.8%; Tamil Nadu 16.0%. Capital 2004-05 to 2023-24 (T117): Odisha 1,604,281 to 36,809,621 (22.9x). Since 2011-12: Odisha 2.29x, Gujarat 2.89x, Madhya Pradesh 4.87x, Rajasthan 3.83x, Haryana 3.38x. Capital outlay 2024-25 BE (T174): Odisha 58,195 crore, +935% since 2012-13. 5G villages (DoT, Feb 2026): Odisha 5.9%, Gujarat 85.2%.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ T116, T117, T122, T128, T174, T169, T175, T020, T022; public/datayt/coverage/mobile_coverage_2026-02-28.json (DoT via Lok Sabha Starred Q470, 25 March 2026). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** 'Is X the next Y' frames work on small channels: Uplearn's 'Is Gujarat the next big thing in India?' 551K (230x) and 'Is Odisha India's Next Tech Hub?' 471K (196x). Verdict and 'next' frames hit 3x+ in 43.5% of cases (21.7% at 10x+) in the place cluster. A split answer with a clear metric pair gives viewers something to argue.
- **Risks:** The factory tables cover registered factories, so mining (Odisha's strength) is outside them. Fixed capital is book value, and Odisha's 22.9x is mostly a 2004-2012 jump, so do not imply a current surge. The 5G village share is not comparable across states (Kerala has 1,438 villages, Uttar Pradesh 105,531); 1,177 uncovered villages is a near tie with Arunachal Pradesh (1,176), so no 'most' claim. Capital outlay is budget estimate. Do not state causes (for example mining royalties) without a source. Andhra Pradesh and Telangana factory series break at the 2014 split; keep them out of 2011-12 comparisons.
- **Effort:** medium

### L052. In Bihar 1 in 3 Is Under 15. In Kerala 1 in 5 Is Over 60

- **Original title:** Young Bihar, Ageing Kerala: India's Two Demographies in Numbers
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.2 (virality 4, appeal 5, distinct 4, fit 8, risk medium)
- **Series:** Why Desk
- **Template:** new: DK Why Desk Long (shared) with a paired population-pyramid card per state (under 15, 15 to 59, 60+) and an India map filled by age share
- **Hook:** Spoken: 'In Bihar, 35.6% of people are under 15. In Kerala, 20.7% are over 60.' On screen: two stacked bars, one tilted young, one tilted old, India's 25.5 and 12.9 as marker lines.
- **Outline:** Target 15 min. 1. Cold open: two bars (1 min). 2. Births (2.5): birth rate 2023 per thousand, Bihar 25.8, Uttar Pradesh 23.6, Rajasthan 22.9, Tamil Nadu 12.0, India 18.4. 3. Fertility, two surveys (3): 2023 sample registration Bihar 2.8, Uttar Pradesh 2.6, Delhi 1.2, Tamil Nadu 1.3, India 1.9; NFHS-6 Bihar 2.7, Uttar Pradesh 2.2; show both and why they differ. 4. Age shares (3): under-15 and 60+ in NFHS-6 for Bihar, Uttar Pradesh, Tamil Nadu, Kerala, India. 5. Living longer (2): life expectancy 2019-23 Kerala 75.1, India 70.3, Chhattisgarh 64.6. 6. What each state needs (2): classrooms and first jobs vs elder care and pensions; pension spending 2024-25 BE Uttar Pradesh 86,488 crore rupees, Tamil Nadu 42,509. 7. Census 2027 and what it will update (1.5): last full count 2011; verify the dates before use. 8. Your state's two numbers (0.5).
- **Who shares it and why:** Teachers and students share the two tilted bars, and Kerala or Tamil Nadu viewers send them to Bihar or Uttar Pradesh friends, but there is no winner to argue over.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** Identity plus a long-run 'two futures' frame. Shared between Kerala and Tamil Nadu viewers and Bihar and Uttar Pradesh viewers, and by teachers and students. Comments: 'what about retirement and pensions?', 'jobs for the young', 'where are the other states?'. Share trigger frame: the two tilted bars, 1 in 3 under 15 against 1 in 5 over 60.
- **Why it could flop:** It is a snapshot with no verdict and no projection (a 2036 or 2047 claim needs data we do not hold), so it is merely informative. 'India's young north, ageing south' is already a well-worn explainer theme. The research shows education/health topics at 0.73 lift and population-by-state lists 84% under 1,000 views. Fertility by state drifts into delimitation politics, and two official sources disagree on Uttar Pradesh (2.2 vs 2.6).
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** NFHS-6 (2023-24) share under 15: Bihar 35.6%, Meghalaya 34.1%, Uttar Pradesh 29.9%, Tamil Nadu 20.2%, Kerala 20.3%, India 25.5%. Share aged 60+: Kerala 20.7%, Goa 17.2%, Tamil Nadu 16.3%, Bihar 11.5%, Uttar Pradesh 10.3%, India 12.9%. Birth rate 2023 (T002): Bihar 25.8, Uttar Pradesh 23.6, Tamil Nadu 12, India 18.4. Fertility 2023 (T006): Bihar 2.8, Uttar Pradesh 2.6, Delhi 1.2, Tamil Nadu 1.3, India 1.9; NFHS-6: Bihar 2.7, Uttar Pradesh 2.2. Life expectancy 2019-23 (T007): Kerala 75.1, Chhattisgarh 64.6, India 70.3. Pension 2024-25 BE (T171): Uttar Pradesh 86,488, Tamil Nadu 42,509 crore rupees.
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/nfhs6/nfhs6_states.json (indicators 2, 3, 18); public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ T002, T003, T006, T007, T171. Optional pulls named in SOURCE_MAP.md: Census of India, Sample Registration System, IIPS LASI (ageing study). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** Population and census is the second-biggest search theme (392 suggestions) and 'north india vs south india' is a typed comparison. Population-distribution videos travel: Real Life Lore's 'Why Most Indians Live Above This Line' reached 3.6M, and population/density topics ran 1.46x on global channels. Census news (self-enumeration 16 to 30 November 2026, to be verified) gives timeliness, although earlier Census 2027 videos only had a 29K median.
- **Risks:** Two official sources disagree (Uttar Pradesh fertility 2.2 in NFHS-6 vs 2.6 in the 2023 registration data): show both, never pick the convenient one. No projections are in hand; any 2036 or 2047 claim needs a pulled source. Census 2027 dates are not confirmed in our notes; verify. Keep delimitation, seats and money-sharing out (elections rule) and keep religion and caste out. Do not frame a young state as a problem or an old state as a failure. NFHS-6 covers household population. Related NFHS Shorts on 60+ exist (S013, S036); this is broader.
- **Effort:** medium

### L053. 10 Indian State Face-Offs: Can You Pick the Winner Every Time?

- **Original title:** Call the Duel: Can You Guess the Winner in 10 State Face-Offs?
- **Status:** Not shortlisted (ranked lower). **Score** 4.2 (virality 3, appeal 4, distinct 7, fit 6, risk low)
- **Series:** Call the Duel
- **Template:** new: DK Call the Duel (16:9 higher-or-lower quiz): two state cards, 5-second countdown with a pause for the viewer, reveal bars, running score, end score card with tiers
- **Hook:** Spoken: 'Which state has more factories, Tamil Nadu or Gujarat? Pick before the number appears.' On screen: two state cards and a 5-second timer, 'score: 0/10'.
- **Outline:** Target 12 min. Ten rounds (about 1 min each), then the score card (1 min), each with the exact figure, period and source on the reveal: 1. Factories 2023-24: Tamil Nadu 40,121 vs Gujarat 33,311. 2. Exports 2024-25: Maharashtra 65,855 vs Gujarat 116,332 (US$ million). 3. Infant mortality 2023: Bihar 23 vs Uttar Pradesh 37 (per thousand). 4. Women in the labour force 2023-24: Delhi 18.5 vs Bihar 30.5. 5. Rural construction wage 2024-25: Tamil Nadu 583.2 vs Kerala 922.6 rupees. 6. Unemployment 2025: Goa 8.3 vs Bihar 3.8. 7. Wheat 2024-25: Punjab 17,993.2 vs Madhya Pradesh 24,509.1 thousand tonnes. 8. Rice 2024-25: Telangana 17,445.7 vs West Bengal 16,019.4 thousand tonnes. 9. Domestic tourist visits 2024: Tamil Nadu 306.84 vs Uttar Pradesh 646.81 million. 10. Eggs 2023-24: Tamil Nadu 223,325 vs Andhra Pradesh 254,874 lakh. End card: score tiers and 'which round fooled you?'. Release after 3 to 4 duel videos so rounds tease them.
- **Who shares it and why:** A viewer posts their own score and dares a friend to beat it, but this needs an audience that already exists and rarely works for a stranger's channel.
- **Viral mechanism (writer's claim):** A quiz the viewer can fail. People share their own score ('I got 6/10'), challenge friends and family, and argue in comments about the rounds they lost ('Namakkal eggs should be first'). Share trigger frame: the end score card and the line 'most viewers get the Bihar round wrong'. We should only say that after we have seen real data from our own audience.
- **Why it could flop:** The measured evidence is against it: 35 quiz-style Indian state Shorts had a median of 63 views, and 'quiz or guess' titles reach 1M only 5.9% of the time. There is no search demand and no topic for the recommender to place a new channel's quiz against, the 12 minutes of 5-second countdowns have no pause for the viewer, and the figures reuse the other videos' numbers. It is the highest-effort build for the least evidence.
- **Key numbers (as proposed):** Round answers (all above): factories 40,121 vs 33,311 (T116); exports 116,332 vs 65,855 USD million (T181); infant mortality 23 vs 37 (T004); women in labour force 30.5 vs 18.5 (PLFS); rural wage 922.6 vs 583.2 (T112); unemployment 8.3 vs 3.8 (PLFS 2025); wheat 24,509.1 vs 17,993.2 (T059); rice 17,445.7 vs 16,019.4 (T058); tourist visits 646.81 vs 306.84 million (T013); eggs 254,874 vs 223,325 lakh (T092).
- **Data editor:** not checked in this run; re-check every number before a script is written.
- **Source:** public/datayt/sources/rbi_handbook/ T116, T181, T004, T112, T059, T058, T013, T092; public/datayt/sources/mospi/plfs_annual.json (female LFPR 2023-24, unemployment 2025). (in hand)
- **Evidence it could work:** This is the experimental slot and the evidence is mixed, so treat it as a test.
